# Officehours — Backlog (Source of Truth)

**This file is the single canonical source of truth for backlog status.** Every commit that closes a deferred item updates this file. Every agent reading this repo (Claude Code, Codex, Cursor, Copilot, Amp, Windsurf, Devin, Jules) reads `AGENTS.md` first; `AGENTS.md` points here for "what's next" and "what shipped."

If you find a backlog claim in any other markdown file (`OFFICEHOURS-*.md`, `CAL-LAB-ROADMAP.md`, etc.), this file overrides it. Those files are archived stubs.

---

## How to maintain this file (the contract)

Every commit that ships a deferred item or introduces a new deferral must touch `BACKLOG.md` in the same commit. Concretely:

1. **Closing an item.** Flip its row's status from `OPEN` → `SHIPPED`. Add the commit SHA in the *Closed by* column. The `commit-msg` git hook (`/.githooks/commit-msg`) blocks any commit message that names a backlog ID (e.g. `B7`, `A12`) when `BACKLOG.md` is unchanged in the staged diff.

2. **Introducing a new deferral.** Add a row to the relevant tier (A inward / B feature / C signal-gated). Use the next free ID in that tier (B14, B15, …). The commit body that introduces the deferral must include `Defers: <new-id>` so future audits trace back.

3. **Re-categorizing.** If an item moves tier (e.g. an OPEN B-item gets demoted to C because the signal hasn't arrived), edit the row inline. Note the move in the *Notes* column.

4. **Decision-first items.** Items marked `DECISION-FIRST` block on a product call, not engineering. Don't start work; surface the decision to the user.

5. **Don't delete rows.** Closed items stay in the table. The whole point is auditability.

---

## Project identity (one paragraph)

Officehours is a single-host scheduling app inspired by Cal.com. **Small visible surface, deep production-engineering stack.** The user-visible product is one host's `/h/<handle>` page where visitors book 15-minute slots. Underneath: idempotency, audit logs, rate limiting, webhooks, soft delete, SSE live queue, attribution cookies, feature flags, observability — every primitive a real SaaS ships invisibly. The backlog below is what's left to make the surface complete; the deep-stack work was finished in the §10.1 wave (commits `60c7e25` → `f6972cd`).

---

## Status legend

- `SHIPPED` — landed in the named commit, verified by tests.
- `OPEN` — engineering ready, blocking on no external dependency. Pick from these.
- `DECISION-FIRST` — engineering ready, blocked on a product call (charge users? require SSO?). Don't start without the call.
- `SIGNAL-GATED` — engineering real, but only worth maintaining when a measurable signal arrives (real prod traffic, p95 above threshold, user request). Don't speculatively start.
- `DEFERRED-BY-DESIGN` — explicitly fenced off in a doc with trigger conditions. See referenced doc.

| B.PT26 | i18n sweep — auth + loading + ICU date sweep + availability chrome strings | SHIPPED | 1a9fa37 | Audit `§4.8` items that didn't fit B.PT25. **ICU date sweep**: dropped hand-rolled `WEEKDAY_SHORT` + `MONTH_SHORT` arrays in `host-profile.tsx`, `bookings-list.tsx`, `booking-detail.tsx`. Replaced with `useFormatter().dateTime(d, { weekday: "short", month: "short", day: "numeric" })` from `next-intl`. Locale resolution honors `oh_locale` cookie (en/es); `oh-eyebrow` CSS uppercase preserves the prior visual rhythm so the swap is invisible in en. **Auth pages** (`/login`, `/register`): server components, so used `getTranslations("Auth")` from `next-intl/server`. New `Auth` namespace covers titles, subtitles, GitHub button, `or` separator, the link-to-other-page row, AND the 5 next-auth `?error=...` codes (Verification / AccessDenied / Configuration / OAuthSignin / OAuthCallback / generic fallback). **Loading skeleton** (`/invitations/[token]/loading.tsx`): single eyebrow string `"Invitation"` lifted to `Invitations.skeletonEyebrow` via `getTranslations`. **Availability chrome** (`availability-fields.tsx`): swapped 18 strings — modal title (`editHours`/`newHours`), time-picker labels + aria (from / to / startTime / endTime), button labels (remove/save/add/done), days-row + drawer (days/none/pickDays/editDaysAria/editBlockAria), empty state (noHoursSet/addBlockHint), validation errors (errorNeedsDay/errorEndBeforeStart/errorOverlap with `{days}` ICU param). Refactored `validateDraft` to return a sentinel KEY (e.g. `"needsDay"`, `"overlap:Mon, Wed"`) instead of a localized string, so the helper stays pure (testable independently of locale) and the consumer maps to `t(...)` for rendering. **Defers: B.PT26B** — the `formatDayLabel` range-compression helper (e.g. `[mon,tue,wed]` → `"Mon – Wed"`) still uses English-only day names from the inline `DAYS` array. Localizing the day-name part needs an ICU `Intl.DateTimeFormat` weekday lookup AND a refactor of the range-compression's name-emission step. Scoped to its own row because it's the trickiest piece (the helper is consumed in 4 places + walks day indices, not Date objects, so the formatter integration isn't a one-liner). **es.json** ships proper Spanish for every new key; `request.ts`'s default-spread fallback means a future locale that omits a key falls back to en cleanly. **Files**: `src/app/h/[handle]/components/host-profile.tsx`, `src/app/(host)/bookings/components/bookings-list.tsx`, `src/app/(host)/bookings/[publicUid]/components/booking-detail.tsx`, `src/app/(host)/availability/components/availability-fields.tsx`, `src/app/login/page.tsx`, `src/app/register/page.tsx`, `src/app/invitations/[token]/loading.tsx`, `messages/en.json`, `messages/es.json`. **Verification**: tsc clean, lint 0 errors, vitest 368 passed (was 368 — no test surface change for i18n sweep) / 5 failed (same pre-existing flakes — orthogonal), Playwright public 3/3 (login/register/h/[handle] hydrate cleanly), authed `/availability` hydration + 5-reload stress green. |
| B.PT26B | Localize `formatDayLabel` range compression in availability-fields.tsx (deferred from B.PT26) | SHIPPED | 228324c | The day-name part of `formatDayLabel(days, "short" \| "long")` (`availability-fields.tsx:607`) still uses the inline English `DAYS` array (`{ key, short: "Mon", long: "Monday" }`). The range-compression algorithm walks day INDICES (`DAY_INDEX[a]`) and emits names via the inline lookup. To localize: derive a 7-entry day-name map from `Intl.DateTimeFormat(locale, { weekday: "short" \| "long" })` for both lengths, pass it (or a name fn) into `formatDayLabel`. Consumed in 4 callsites — chip + drawer title + sr-only description + the validateDraft `overlap:` payload. Re-test the range-compression cases verbatim in es: `[mon..wed]` → `"Lun – mié"`, `[mon..fri]` → `"Lun – Vie"`, `[sat..sun]` → `"Sáb – Dom"`, `[mon, wed, fri]` → `"Lun, mié, vie"`. Estimated ~1-2 hours plus a property-based test using `fc.subarray(DAY_KEYS)` (Pattern 1 from `docs/edge-case-testing.md`) to fuzz the range-compression logic and ensure no regressions in the en path. |

| B.PT34 | GitHub OAuth account linking by verified email | SHIPPED | _to be filled by commit_ | User report: tapping "Continue with GitHub" on `/register` (or `/login`) when a credentials account already exists for the same email silently created a SECOND `User` row, signed the host in as the new account, and bootstrapped a fresh empty workspace via `events.createUser` → `bootstrapUserWorkspace`. The host's bookings + handle live on the original account, so the dashboard reads as "all data wiped." Auth.js v5 default is to NOT link OAuth accounts to existing User rows even when emails match — see the `OAuthAccountNotLinked` error class — because some OAuth providers don't verify the email they hand back, which would let an attacker take over an existing account by claiming a matching unverified email. GitHub DOES verify emails (the API only returns the user's verified primary email), so the trust decision is safe for this specific provider. Set `allowDangerousEmailAccountLinking: true` on the GitHub provider config in `src/auth.ts:16`. Cal.com + dub make the same call. Existing duplicate User rows in dev DBs need manual cleanup via Prisma Studio — flag does NOT retroactively merge accounts; it only changes future-sign-in behavior. Reference: Auth.js docs `Define Email Account Linking` (Context7-verified 2026-04-30). |

| B.PT39 | `/bookings` granular Suspense + URL-driven tab state | SHIPPED | _to be filled by commit_ | User report: hard-refresh on `/bookings` showed the route-level `loading.tsx` skeleton flash before the data island streamed in (B.PT24's loading.tsx file was the cause — Next App Router auto-wraps async pages in a Suspense boundary whose fallback is the route-level loading.tsx, so EVERY hard-refresh showed full-page skeletons even though hydration was perfect). | B.PT40 | Revert /bookings to upstream-shape (fix Suspense SSR + layout shift) | SHIPPED | _to be filled by commit_ | B.PT39's granular Suspense pattern shipped two regressions: **(1) SSR fetch failure** — with the legacy `@trpc/react-query` proxy + `createServerSideHelpers`, the queryClient inside `<HydrationBoundary>` is a separate instance from the one the SSR helper populates. On server-render of the rows client component, the cache it saw was empty → `useSuspenseQuery` invoked the queryFn → tRPC's `httpBatchLink` with relative URL `/api/trpc/...` failed to parse on Node fetch (`TypeError: Failed to parse URL`), Next fell back to client rendering with a console error stack. **(2) Layout shift** — the rows skeleton (`<div className="mt-6"><ul>...3 rows</ul></div>`) had its own `mt-6` plus the parent's `mt-6`, totaling 48px above the skeleton vs 24px above resolved content; swap caused 24px+ vertical jump pushing footer/below content up. The Tanstack-recommended fix for (1) is `@tanstack/react-query-next-experimental`'s `ReactQueryStreamedHydration` provider — a new dep + provider rewrite, experimental package. Pragmatic fix for our existing setup: revert to the upstream `fix/next-pin-16.1.7` shape — page.tsx awaits prefetch at top, single `<HydrationBoundary>` wraps the whole page-level `<BookingsList>` client component, `useQuery` (not `useSuspenseQuery`) reads the hydrated cache. Page TTFB is slightly longer (server blocks on prefetch ~50-200ms in dev) but no skeleton, no flash, no layout shift. Matches upstream's "page takes slightly longer to load, then full content" UX verbatim. **Kept from B.PT39**: URL-driven tab state — `?tab=upcoming\|past` search param, `searchParams` read in `page.tsx`, passed as `activeTab` prop to `<BookingsList>`, controlled `<Tabs onValueChange>` pushes via `router.push("?tab=...", { scroll: false })`. Refresh stays on the right tab, browser-back navigates between them, deep-linkable. Files: `page.tsx` reverted to upstream shape with searchParams handling layered in; `bookings-list.tsx` restored as the page-level component (BookingsList + BookingTabTrigger + BookingsListPanel + BookingRow + LiveQueue + LiveDot + EmptyBookings + fmt utilities) with `activeTab` prop for URL-driven control. Deleted: `bookings-rows-boundary.tsx`, `bookings-rows.tsx`, `bookings-rows-skeleton.tsx`, `bookings-tabs.tsx`. `loading.tsx` stays deleted (B.PT39 already removed it; matches upstream). Vitest 363/367 + tsc clean + lint 0 errors + authed hydration 6/6. |
| B.PT41 | Layout-level prefetch for chrome queries (no more avatar/workspace flash) | SHIPPED | _to be filled by commit_ | User report: avatar initials + workspace switcher label flashed placeholder values on first paint of every route, then "snapped" to real values once the client-side fetch resolved. Root cause: `(host)/layout.tsx` did ZERO prefetch — chrome (`OhDashboardBar` + `OhUserMenu`) calls `useQuery(users.me)` + `useQuery(workspaces.list)`, but those queries only land in the dehydrated state when the PAGE explicitly prefetches them. Per-route audit: `/bookings`, `/profile`, `/settings/general` prefetch `users.me`; `/workspaces`, `/settings/billing`, `/settings/developer`, `/workspaces/[slug]/settings` prefetch `workspaces.list`. Routes missing one or both (e.g., `/availability`, settings sub-routes that don't workspace-scope) flash the chrome placeholder. cal.com solves this by populating the chrome queries at a parent layout (`<Shell>` reads from cached queries the layout populated upstream); dub uses SWR + a client-side `<WorkspaceAuth>` gate that blocks the layout behind a `<LayoutLoader>` until workspace data arrives — heavier, layout itself shows a loader. Followed cal.com's lighter approach: `(host)/layout.tsx` (server component, was async-no-await) now creates an SSR helper, awaits parallel prefetch of `users.me` + `workspaces.list`, wraps the entire `<OhDashboardLayout>` + `{modal}` tree in a `<HydrationBoundary>`. Tanstack's `hydrate` is additive into the singleton browser queryClient, so per-page prefetches still cover their own data needs (the layout's hydration union with each page's hydration in the same cache). Result: avatar initials + workspace switcher label render with correct values on first paint of every host route — no flash, no snap, no flicker. Authed hydration 6/6 still green (the new layout-level await adds ~5-10ms to TTFB, well under the streaming threshold). |
| B.PT42 | Onboarding checklist hydration — prefetch schedule.get (no more card-flash + unchecked-step flash) | SHIPPED | _to be filled by commit_ | User report on a fresh-account hard-refresh of `/bookings`: (1) the "Draw your weekly hours" checkbox showed unmarked even though the user had set their availability, and (2) the entire onboarding card briefly reappeared after it had been completed, then disappeared. Same root cause: `<OnboardingChecklist>` reads three queries — `users.me`, `schedule.get`, `bookings.listForHost` — but `/bookings/page.tsx` only prefetched two of them (`users.me` + `bookings.listForHost`), and `(host)/layout.tsx`'s B.PT41 prefetch covered `users.me` + `workspaces.list`. `schedule.get` was unhydrated → server-rendered the checklist with `availabilityCount: 0` → `availabilityDone: false` → card NOT yet `isComplete()` → renders. Client mounts → `schedule.get` fetches → `availabilityDone` flips true → card disappears (or step row swaps from unchecked to checked if other steps still pending). Reference patterns: dub reads its onboarding state from a SWR-cached workspace context populated at layout level (no flash because the data is in SWR before the component mounts); cal.com handles onboarding as a separate route flow (`/onboarding/*`) so doesn't have an inline-checklist hydration race at all. Pragmatic fix for our inline-checklist shape: add `trpc.schedule.get.prefetch()` to the `/bookings/page.tsx` prefetch array. The checklist now server-renders with all three queries hydrated → correct done/pending state on first paint → no flash, no snap. NOT moved to layout level because `<OnboardingChecklist>` is `/bookings`-only and the schedule query is irrelevant for the chrome on other routes. |
| B.PT217 | Visitor-page redesign — kill scrollbar gutter sliver on visitor pages. The visitor surface had no equivalent — even with `h-dvh overflow-hidden` on OhVisitorShell, html's reserved gutter painted a sliver of html bg on the right. **Two coordinated changes**: (a) `oh-visitor-shell` class added to OhVisitorShell's outer div. (b) `globals.css` gets a parallel rule `:where(html, body):has(.oh-visitor-shell) { overflow: hidden; scrollbar-gutter: auto }` mirroring the dashboard pattern. Scoped so only visitor routes (`/h/[handle]`, `/h/[handle]/booked/[uid]`, future `/w/[slug]/*`) drop the gutter; auth + dashboard keep the global `stable` gutter for jump-free overflow toggles. ALSO swapped `<main>` from `overflow-auto` → `overflow-hidden` (no Tailwind built-in `scrollbar-hidden`; would need a custom `@utility`; `overflow-hidden` is simpler when scrolling isn't wanted). **Files modified**: `oh-visitor-shell.tsx` (~5 LOC delta — class hook + comment, main overflow swap), `globals.css` (~12 LOC delta — new `:has(.oh-visitor-shell)` block with explanatory comment). **Verification**: `pnpm tsc --noEmit` clean; user confirmed visual fix. |

Two issues fixed: (1) **Color**: sisal (`--oh-frame`, 12% ink in srgb over paper) read as too dark per user. Swapped to `bg-oh-bg-muted` which maps to `--oh-twdivider-bg` (8% ink in oklab over paper) — subtler "darker paper" with a Tailwind shorthand, lives in `globals.css:137`. (2) **Scroll**: `min-h-dvh` lets the box grow past viewport when inner content overflows. Per Tailwind docs (`tailwindcss.com/docs/height` + v3.4 dynamic-viewport blog), `h-dvh` is the canonical viewport-fit unit (mobile-safe, adapts to address-bar collapse unlike `vh`). Pattern: `h-dvh overflow-hidden` on outer box, `min-h-0 flex-1` on the inner flex column. The `min-h-0` is critical — flex children's default `min-height: auto` prevents shrinking below content's intrinsic height, so without it the outer `overflow-hidden` can't contain the inner. `<main>` also gets `min-h-0 flex-1 overflow-auto` so internal content can scroll inside the paper panel without escaping the viewport-fit outer. **Files modified**: `oh-visitor-shell.tsx` (~30 LOC delta — outer `min-h-dvh` → `h-dvh overflow-hidden`, color `bg-[color:var(--oh-frame)]` → `bg-oh-bg-muted`, inner gets `min-h-0 overflow-hidden`, main gets `min-h-0 overflow-auto`, comment block rewritten). **Verification**: `pnpm tsc --noEmit` clean. |

(a) New OUTER div: `flex min-h-dvh flex-col bg-[color:var(--oh-frame)] p-[15px]` — sisal viewport bg with 15px padding. Sisal = the `--oh-frame` token (color-mix in srgb of ink 12% over paper, per `.claude/rules/oh-css-vars.md`); no `bg-oh-frame` Tailwind shorthand exists, so arbitrary `bg-[color:var(--oh-frame)]` is used. The 25px radius matches landing card's `HANDLE_CARD_RADIUS` (host-profile.tsx) so the cross-route paper panel and the landing card share one corner-curve vocabulary. **Why two-layer at OhVisitorShell**: highest visitor-only position; affects every visitor route (`/h/[handle]`, `/h/[handle]/booked/[uid]`, future `/w/[slug]/*`) without bleeding into dashboard or auth surfaces. Going higher (route layout's `oh-root` div) would risk affecting non-visitor consumers. **Files modified**: `oh-visitor-shell.tsx` (~20 LOC delta — wrap return in outer sisal div, inner paper panel keeps shell contract incl. header/footer slots). **Verification**: `pnpm tsc --noEmit` clean. |

| B.PT211 | Visitor-page redesign — modal slot-stack `padding: 15` → Tailwind `p-[15px]` className (consistent with landing); first/last chip outer radius derived from Apple HIG concentric formula `outer - margin` (was hardcoded `5`) | SHIPPED | `f431e5a` | User after B.PT210: "on the Modal slot-stack sure do so [convert to Tailwind class] and also could you update the slotRadiusStyle so that the radius that it gives (use apples higs for this from docs) is concentric with its parent so we keep consistency? rather than a hard coded 5?" | B.PT201 | Visitor-page redesign — nested shared layoutIds on identity row/avatar/title (`oh-identity-row` / `oh-identity-avatar` / `oh-identity-title`) kill the inner chip + title fade-in | SHIPPED | `bd8fe06` | User-authored fix that finally resolved the open-direction chip + title fade artifact. After 12 iterations of `layoutCrossfade={false}` whack-a-mole (one shared-layout pair at a time), the user spotted that motion's auto-crossfade was ALSO firing on the identity header's *inner* spans (avatar + title text + the row that wraps them) because they were anonymous `layout` / `layout="position"` children — not shared-layout elements. The same per-frame-layoutId pattern that fixed the slot text/duration spans in B.PT184 had to be applied at the identity tree too. **Fix**: promote the inner identity nodes from anonymous projections into shared-layout elements with explicit layoutIds: `oh-identity-row` (the flex container), `oh-identity-avatar` (motion.span around the Avatar), `oh-identity-title` (motion.h1 with displayName). Pinned `initial = animate = exit = 1` on all three so motion's `mixValues` sees no opacity delta. Hide the stripped Layer 1 row via `visibility: hidden` once motion has measured it, so only the Layer 2 clone renders during the handoff. **Why it worked**: motion's auto-crossfade is **per-shared-layout-pair**. Earlier commits suppressed it on `handle-card` (B.PT189), `oh-slot-list` (B.PT198), `oh-identity` root (B.PT200), and `oh-slot-N` (B.PT200). The inner identity children were still anonymous, so when the modal mounted motion treated them as "new entering elements" and ran the default entrance fade. Pairing them to shared layoutIds with matching elements on both sides means motion sees a continuous transition rather than a fresh mount → no entrance opacity tween fires. Same root-cause class as B.PT196/197 (picker/form fade) but at a deeper nesting level. **Files modified**: `host-profile.tsx` (~25 LOC delta — landing identity row/avatar/title get layoutIds + opacity pins; identityContent prop's clone gets matching IDs + pins; visibility-hidden on stripped Layer 1 row), `handle-modal.tsx` (~6 LOC delta — comment update on identity phantom block reflecting the new approach). **Verification**: gates green; visual smoke confirmed by user. |
| B.PT200 | Visitor-page redesign — `layoutCrossfade={false}` on `oh-identity` and `oh-slot-N` shared-layout pairs (kill chip + title fade-in/out) | SHIPPED | `254a52f` | User confirmed via screenshot + observation: "the chips on click instantly fade out and start fading in as the animation continues, and additionally it also happens with the title. only the children of the chips container, the parent (slot-list) is fine." Image showed amber slot-list at full opacity but chips visibly washed out / faded mid-animation. **Diagnosis (motion source)**: `layoutCrossfade={false}` is per-shared-layout-pair. B.PT189 covered `handle-card`, B.PT198 covered `oh-slot-list`. The `oh-identity` and `oh-slot-N` (×4) pairs each had their own crossfade running, where motion's `mixValues` mutates `target.opacity = mixNumber(0, lead.opacity, easeCrossfadeIn(progress))` regardless of explicit `initial.opacity` / `animate.opacity` values. **Fix**: add `layoutCrossfade={false}` to the four remaining shared-layout elements: `motion.div layoutId="oh-identity"` in handle-modal.tsx (modal phantom), `motion.header layoutId={landingLayoutId("oh-identity")}` in host-profile.tsx (landing), and BOTH inert + non-inert variants of `<motion.div / motion.button layoutId={layoutId}>` in SlotRow's render. Per source line 8045: with `crossfade=false`, `promote()` calls `prevLead.hide()` and `shouldCrossfadeOpacity` becomes false → mixValues skips the opacity tween → chip + title render at their explicit opacity throughout. **Files modified**: `handle-modal.tsx` (~5 LOC delta — identity motion.div), `host-profile.tsx` (~10 LOC delta — identity motion.header + SlotRow's two motion variants). **Verification**: `pnpm tsc --noEmit` clean; `pnpm lint` 0 errors. |

Two debug-only changes: (a) slot-list bg swapped from cream `#F5EFDF` to `bg-amber-300/70` on BOTH landing (`host-profile.tsx:548`) and modal (`handle-modal.tsx:403`) sides — distinct from existing debug colors (lime Frame 9, cyan text, pink duration). Marked `DEBUG-B.PT199` with revert note. (b) `visualDuration` slider in `visitor-debug-overlay.tsx` `max: 3 → 15`. Step kept at 0.05 for sub-100ms precision. Lets the user dial slow-mo up to 15s for multi-second tracer captures. **Files modified**: `host-profile.tsx`, `handle-modal.tsx`, `_components/visitor-debug-overlay.tsx` (~10 LOC delta total). **Verification**: `pnpm tsc --noEmit` clean; `pnpm lint` 0 errors. |
| B.PT198 | Visitor-page redesign — `layoutCrossfade={false}` on the oh-slot-list shared-layout pair (kills the 0→1 cream-bg fade-in caught by tracer) | SHIPPED | `5234d63` | User after B.PT197: tracer (`handoff/trace-page-fade.js`) caught the actual culprit. Trace data showed only `slotListPhantom` opacity changes during open: 0 → 0.27 (t=17ms) → 0.56 → 0.75 → 0.85 over the morph. Every other element (body, .oh-root, main, modalWrapper, modalArticle, phantomContainer, calendarWrapper) stays at opacity 1. That kills the auto-crossfade for THAT pair's elements. But the `oh-slot-list` motion.div is its OWN shared-layout pair — its `crossfade` projection option defaults to `true`. Per motion source `mixValues` line ~9123: when `shouldCrossfadeOpacity===true && this.options.crossfade===true`, motion sets `target.opacity = mixNumber(0, lead.opacity, easeCrossfadeIn(progress))` which interpolates phantom slot-list's opacity 0→1 over the easeCrossfadeIn curve (compressed 0..0.5). The trace's 0 → 0.85 over 400ms matches that curve exactly. Our explicit `initial.opacity = 1` is overridden by motion's mixValues mutation of `animationValues.opacity`. **Why only just visible**: B.PT195 made the phantom slot-list visible in production (cream bg with inner shadow). The auto-crossfade was always firing but invisible (when slot-list opacity was 0, fading 0→0.x looked the same as 0). **Fix**: add `layoutCrossfade={false}` to BOTH slot-list motion.divs (landing in `host-profile.tsx`, modal in `handle-modal.tsx`). Per motion source line 8045: when promote() runs with `node.options.crossfade===false`, it calls `prevLead.hide()` (visibility:hidden cascading) AND shouldCrossfadeOpacity becomes false → mixValues skips the crossfade branch → phantom renders at `latestValues.opacity = 1` (our explicit value). **Files modified**: `host-profile.tsx` slot-list motion.div + `handle-modal.tsx` slot-list motion.div (~3 LOC each). **Verification**: `pnpm tsc --noEmit` clean; `pnpm lint` 0 errors. Run the tracer again to verify slotListPhantom opacity stays at 1 throughout. |

Mirror of B.PT196 for the close direction. The picker/form motion.divs still had `exit={{ opacity: 0 }}` from before — on a picker → form swap (or form → picker via back button) the OLD element fades out 1→0 over the cream slot-list bg (visible after B.PT195 dropped the calendar wrapper bg). On close the inner AnimatePresence may also run picker's exit if AnimatePresence is alive when HandleModal unmounts. Either way, the same opacity-fade-against-cream artifact applies. **Fix**: `exit={{ opacity: 1 }}` matching `initial={{ opacity: 1 }}` and `animate={{ opacity: 1 }}`. AnimatePresence `mode="wait"` still serializes the picker ↔ form swap (waits for the outgoing element to "exit" before the incoming mounts), but with exit at opacity 1 the wait completes instantly. Net: no fade in either direction; picker ↔ form swaps are instant. **Files modified**: `handle-modal.tsx` picker + form motion.divs (~6 LOC delta). **Verification**: `pnpm tsc --noEmit` clean; `pnpm lint` 0 errors. |
| B.PT196 | Visitor-page redesign — picker/form `initial.opacity=1` (was 0) so calendar content doesn't fade in on modal open | SHIPPED | `d684bb9` | User after B.PT195: "another issue when the animation just starts in inspect mode it makes like the whole page like that animates like spawn as opacity 0 then animate quickly to opacity 1 like if it started as fade in this happens as soon as you click chip in layer one." **Diagnosis**: the picker + form motion.divs (line 601 / 624 in `handle-modal.tsx`) had `initial={{ opacity: 0 }}, animate={{ opacity: 1 }}` inside `<AnimatePresence mode="wait" initial={false}>`. The fade was previously **invisible** because the calendar wrapper had `bg-[color:var(--oh-paper)]` — picker fading from 0→1 over an opaque paper bg looked identical (paper → paper). After B.PT195 dropped that bg so the cream slot-list could peek through, the picker fade became visible (paper → cream is a visible color shift). **Fix**: change `initial: { opacity: 0 }` → `initial: { opacity: 1 }` on both picker and form motion.divs. First-mount fade gone. The picker → form swap still reads cleanly under `mode="wait"`: old element fades out via `exit: { opacity: 0 }`, AnimatePresence waits, new element appears at opacity 1. Lose the fade-IN on swap; keep the fade-out and the wait gate. **Files modified**: `handle-modal.tsx` (picker + form motion.divs, ~12 LOC delta). **Plus a diagnostic tool**: `handoff/trace-page-fade.js` — paste-ready browser console tracer that records body / .oh-root / main / modal wrapper / modal article / phantom container / slot-list / calendar wrapper opacity per RAF for 1.5s after a chip click. Use to verify the fix and catch any future opacity flashes. **Verification**: `pnpm tsc --noEmit` clean; `pnpm lint` 0 errors. |
| B.PT195 | Visitor-page redesign — slot-list cream container visible in production (preserves nested-card aesthetic, expands like the chips do) | SHIPPED | `7782f1c` | User: "in production mode the idea is for the container its in either to have the same inner shadow the closed state has or for the container to like expand the same way the outer container of the chips do which is probably what i prefer can you see why there is no like container inner shadow for the closed state and how we can fix it?" Calendar at `z-10` is now ABOVE the cream bg. | B.PT194 | Visitor-page redesign — modal backdrop scrim removed + first/last chips get concentric corner radius (Apple HIG inner = outer − padding) | SHIPPED | `972ce30` | User: "now when the modal is open there is like a backdrop can we remove it idk where it came from but i dont want it and also round the corners of the first and last chips (for the 1 one just the top corners for the bottom one just the bottom corners) when they are in the open state right now i see them all as sharp corners use apple higs formula." Two cleanups in `handle-modal.tsx`: **(1) Remove the scrim**: a `<motion.div className="fixed inset-0 z-40 bg-[color:var(--oh-ink)]/40">` was rendering a 40% ink scrim behind the modal at z-40. Removed entirely. The modal's inner shadow + cream-vs-paper contrast define its edge sufficiently. Also makes inspect mode's dual-layer view cleaner (landing card behind isn't dimmed). **(2) Concentric corner radius on phantom slots**: per Apple HIG's documented concentric-corner formula `inner radius = outer radius − margin`, where slot-list's outer `cornerRadius=20` and Frame 18's inner padding=15, so `inner radius = 20 − 15 = 5`. Applied only to first chip's TOP corners and last chip's BOTTOM corners; middle chips stay sharp so the row dividers read as a clean segmented stack. Implementation: chip phantom's `style` block keeps `borderRadius: 0` as the catch-all (overrides SlotRow's CSS base of 14) and conditionally spreads `borderTopLeftRadius / borderTopRightRadius: 5` for `i === 0` or `borderBottomLeftRadius / borderBottomRightRadius: 5` for `i === 3`. **Files modified**: `handle-modal.tsx` (~30 LOC delta — drop scrim motion.div + comment, replace borderRadius:0 with conditional per-corner spread). **Verification**: `pnpm tsc --noEmit` clean; `pnpm lint` 0 errors. |
| B.PT193 | Visitor-page redesign — duration `layoutAnchor={false}` + transition prop on inner spans (kill non-monotonic position dip + respect duration override) | SHIPPED | `0853598` | User after instrumented dual-side per-frame trace + their own observation: "we should let it grow or shrink naturally instead of it doing so with an animation meaning for it to have a LINEAR curve... the 3 second duration doesnt affect that animation in specific so it looks like it snaps relative to a 3 second animation but it is animating just at normal speed not slow mo." Two distinct verified bugs: **(1) Inner spans were ignoring the Spring tuning duration override.** Frame 9 / text / duration motion.spans had no `transition` prop → motion fell through to `defaultLayoutTransition = { duration: 0.45, ease: [0.4, 0, 0.1, 1] }`. With Spring tuning duration override at 1.5s or 3s, the OUTER chip's animation slowed accordingly but inner spans STAYED at 0.45s ease — visible as a "snap" relative to the slowed-down outer chip. **Fix**: pass `transition` prop through to all three inner motion.spans so they animate with the same curve/duration as the outer chip. **(2) Duration's screen X dipped non-monotonically during open** — verified in trace: 801 → 778 → 768 → ... → 864. Duration first moved LEFT before reversing RIGHT to track the expanding right edge. Root cause: motion's `layout="position"` + `layoutAnchor={x:0, y:0}` made motion run its own per-element layout animation interpolating duration's relative-to-frame9-LEFT position from landing-context-243 to modal-context-608. Combined with frame9's FLIP transform, the compound math produces a visible left-then-right dip — the user's "snap." Text doesn't show this because text is at frame9's left edge, so its relative-from-left is constant, motion doesn't interpolate, no dip. **Fix**: `layoutAnchor={false}` on duration. Per `motion-dom/dist/index.d.ts:957`: *"`false` disables relative projection entirely."* Duration stops running its per-element layout animation and inherits frame9's transform directly via CSS — duration stays glued to frame9's right edge throughout the morph (matches the Figma auto-layout fill-container behavior the user described). Text keeps `{x:0, y:0}` because its motion is correct. **Files modified**: `host-profile.tsx` SlotRow (~30 LOC delta — `transition={transition}` on Frame 9 / text / duration motion.spans, `layoutAnchor={false}` replaces `{x:0, y:0}` on duration, comment updates). **Verification**: `pnpm tsc --noEmit` clean (`layoutAnchor: boolean | {x,y}` is in `MotionNodeOptions`); `pnpm lint` 0 errors. Manual smoke pending: with Spring tuning duration at 3s, all inner spans should now slow with the override; the duration label should track frame9's right edge with no left-then-right dip. |

Located the flex container in `host-profile.tsx` SlotRow component: Frame 9 motion.span (line ~778, the `flex h-[50px] items-center justify-between gap-3` container), text motion.span (line ~795, "intro" + "quick chat"), duration motion.span (line ~830, "15 min" etc.). Added contrast backgrounds + 1px outlines for each: Frame 9 = lime, text = cyan, duration = pink. Lets the user watch the spans' rendered rects throughout the morph and identify whether duration snaps to modal-right-edge from frame 0 (motion not interpolating) or smoothly translates (motion interpolation working). **Files modified**: `host-profile.tsx` SlotRow (~9 LOC delta — append `bg-*-300/* outline outline-1 outline-*-700` classes on the three motion.span elements with `DEBUG-B.PT192` marker comments). **Verification**: `pnpm tsc --noEmit` clean; `pnpm lint` 0 errors. | B.PT191 | Visitor-page redesign — duration `layoutAnchor` reverted to `{x:0, y:0}` (B.PT190's `{x:1, y:0}` BACKFIRED — caused duration to snap because relative-from-right was constant in both contexts) | SHIPPED | `7b2ba73` | User after B.PT190: "it still when the animation first runs like as soon as one clicks the chip in layer 1 it snaps to the final position it doesnt act the same way the text does that smoothly moves." Text translates smoothly; duration with `{x:1, y:0}` still snaps. **Diagnosis (motion source)**: `calcRelativeAxisPosition` (motion.dev.js) computes `target.min = layout.min - anchorPoint` where `anchorPoint = mixNumber(parent.min, parent.max, anchor.x)`. With anchor.x=1, anchorPoint = parent.x.max (right edge). Duration is right-aligned in BOTH source (landing chip) and dest (modal phantom) — its right edge always equals Frame 9's right edge — so relative-from-right ≈ 0 in BOTH contexts. Motion sees `relativeTargetOrigin` ≈ `relativeLayout` (no delta), `mixBox(origin, current, progress)` interpolates between two equal values → constant relative position → no per-child position animation. Duration renders at its CSS-natural (modal) position from frame 0 = "snaps to final." With default `{x:0, y:0}` (top-left, matches text span), motion calculates relative-from-LEFT: duration's left-from-Frame-9-left is much smaller in landing (Frame 9 ~303 wide) than in modal (Frame 9 ~668 wide). Motion sees a real delta → animates duration's position from the landing-context relative-left to the modal-context relative-left → duration's screen position smoothly translates from landing-right-edge to modal-right-edge as chip morphs. **The right-edge anchor visual effect is achieved by INTERPOLATING the position delta**, not by motion's relative-anchor calculation. The docstring "useful for centered layouts to prevent drift" is for elements whose CSS layout position-relative-to-parent doesn't change between contexts (e.g., always-centered). Right-aligned via `justify-between` looks similar from a CSS-positioning perspective but differs because the OFFSET-from-relevant-edge is the same — which is exactly when `layoutAnchor` should NOT be used (it suppresses the animation). **Fix**: revert duration's `layoutAnchor` to `{x:0, y:0}` matching the text span. Both spans now use the same default anchor, motion runs interpolation on both, both translate smoothly with the chip morph. **Files modified**: `host-profile.tsx` SlotRow (~5 LOC delta — change anchor on duration motion.span). **Verification**: `pnpm tsc --noEmit` clean; `pnpm lint` 0 errors. Manual smoke pending: duration label should now smoothly translate alongside the text span as the chip morphs, with both staying anchored to their respective edges throughout. |
| B.PT190 | Visitor-page redesign — `layoutAnchor` props on text + duration spans (duration stays right-anchored during morph instead of snapping) | SHIPPED | `a6ff75a` | User: "for the time markers ex. '15 min...etc' all of them so i assume that they are together in a flexbox right and then why doesnt the minute timer behave the same way the text does of it justifying itself to top right and not snapping as soon as animation triggers." User's correct hypothesis: in Figma this is achieved with auto-layout flex container with auto gap + fill container, so both ends stay pushed to opposite sides as the container grows. **Verified discovery**: `layoutAnchor` is a documented `MotionNodeOptions` prop in `motion-dom/dist/index.d.ts:957` with the docstring: *"point on the parent is used as the reference for the child's position. `{ x: 0, y: 0 }` (default) anchors to the top-left corner. `{ x: 0.5, y: 0.5 }` anchors to the center, useful for centered layouts (e.g., flexbox) to prevent drift during parent layout animations."* Earlier B.PT186 wrongly removed `layoutAnchor` props that codex/chatgpt had originally shipped, on the bad assumption that the prop was being silently ignored. This commit restores them with motion-source verification: `use-visual-element.mjs` `createProjectionNode` destructures `layoutAnchor` from props and passes it via `setOptions`. **Mechanism**: motion's `mixValues` (motion.dev.js line ~9139) computes the inner element's relative-position via `calcRelativePosition(..., this.options.layoutAnchor || undefined)`. With default `{x:0, y:0}` (top-left), the duration's position is calculated relative to Frame 9's LEFT edge — as Frame 9 morphs 303 → 668 wide, duration's offset-from-left changes, so motion translates it leftward to maintain that offset → duration "snaps" toward the new center. With `{x:1, y:0}` (top-right), motion calculates relative to Frame 9's RIGHT edge — duration stays anchored to the right throughout the morph, matching the CSS `justify-between` intent and the user's described Figma fill-container behavior. **Files modified**: `host-profile.tsx` SlotRow (~30 LOC delta — restore `layoutAnchor={{ x: 0, y: 0 }}` on text span, restore `layoutAnchor={{ x: 1, y: 0 }}` on duration span). **Verification**: `pnpm tsc --noEmit` clean (TS accepts because `layoutAnchor` is in `MotionNodeOptions`); `pnpm lint` 0 errors. Manual smoke pending: close animation should keep "15 min", "25 min", "30 min", "1 hr" duration labels glued to the right edge of each chip throughout the morph instead of snapping toward center. |
| B.PT189 | Visitor-page redesign — documented `layoutCrossfade={false}` motion prop kills auto opacity tween (close-direction text fade glitch) | SHIPPED | `e4b55b8` | User after B.PT188 (provider didn't fix the residual): asked for a real diagnostic tool + fix. **Discovery via deeper motion source dive**: framer-motion 12.38's projection API is configured from component props in `node_modules/.pnpm/framer-motion@12.38.0/.../use-visual-element.mjs` `createProjectionNode`, which destructures `{ layoutId, layout, layoutScroll, layoutRoot, layoutAnchor, layoutCrossfade }` from `props` and passes them into `setOptions`. **`layoutCrossfade?: boolean`** is a fully documented `MotionNodeOptions` prop (`node_modules/motion-dom/dist/index.d.ts:970`) with the docstring: *"By default, shared layout elements will crossfade. By setting this to `false`, this element will take its default opacity throughout the animation."* This is the public, typed, documented escape — B.PT188's `<SwitchLayoutGroupContext.Provider>` was the right idea but using framer-motion-internal API; the proper public API is `layoutCrossfade={false}` on the layoutId-bearing motion element. **Mechanism (motion source motion.dev.js)**: when a new lead promotes via `promote()` (line 8045), if `node.options.crossfade === false` motion calls `prevLead.hide()` instead of running the auto opacity tween. `hide()` sets `isVisible = false` on the projection (line 5805); the render path applies `targetStyle.visibility = "hidden"` (line 9501). With visibility:hidden cascading via CSS to the entire phantom subtree, every descendant span (Frame 9, text, duration) is also visibility-hidden — kills any nested fade artifact in one stroke. **Fix**: `layoutCrossfade={false}` on both `motion.article` elements with `layoutId="handle-card"` (one in `host-profile.tsx`, one in `handle-modal.tsx`). On every promote the prev lead hides via visibility:hidden — the morph reads as a single-element transition. Removed B.PT188's `<SwitchLayoutGroupContext.Provider>` wrap (replaced by the cleaner public API). **Plus a debug helper for the user**: `handoff/trace-chip-opacity.js` is a paste-ready browser-console snippet that observes the entire shared-layout subtree's computed opacity / visibility / inline opacity per animation frame for 5 seconds; result lives in `window.__chipTrace` for `console.table` inspection. Lets the user see frame-by-frame what motion is actually setting on the chip and verify the fix worked. **Files modified**: `host-profile.tsx` (~22 LOC delta — drop SwitchLayoutGroupContext import + Provider wrap, add `layoutCrossfade={false}` on landing motion.article); `handle-modal.tsx` (~10 LOC delta — same prop on modal motion.article); `handoff/trace-chip-opacity.js` (new file). **Verification**: `pnpm tsc --noEmit` clean (TypeScript accepts the prop because it's declared in `MotionNodeOptions`); `pnpm lint` 0 errors. Manual smoke pending: close should hold the visible side at constant opacity 1, with the inactive side instantly hidden via visibility:hidden — no fade-out at progress 0.5, no fade-back-in at 0.7. |
| B.PT188 | Visitor-page redesign — `<SwitchLayoutGroupContext.Provider shouldPreserveFollowOpacity>` (suppresses the auto fade-out on the follow chip during close) | SHIPPED | `aa3c5c9` | User after B.PT187 (working but residual): "the issue is that the initial problem still isnt fixed... when its about the 0.7-8 progress almost done the text inside the chip fades out, dissappears, and then fades in again." Remaining glitch even after the codex per-frame layoutId fix is restored. **Root cause traced through motion source `motion.dev.js`**: at `mixValues` line ~9123, motion overrides explicit `animate.opacity` for shared-layout elements when `shouldCrossfadeOpacity === true`. It computes `target.opacity = mixNumber(0, lead.opacity ?? 1, easeCrossfadeIn(progress))` and `target.opacityExit = mixNumber(follow.opacity ?? 1, 0, easeCrossfadeOut(progress))`. The render selector at line ~9416 picks `valuesToRender.opacityExit` for the FOLLOW element UNLESS `this.preserveOpacity` is true. `easeCrossfadeOut = compress(0.5, 0.95, noop)` — the follow's opacity goes 1 → 0 starting at progress 0.5, hitting ~0.4 by progress 0.7 (matches user's reported 0.7-0.8 window). On close, lead = landing (latest registered when its layoutId is restored), follow = phantom. Phantom is the visible element at modal coords during the morph — its opacityExit fade-out is what user sees as "text fades out". Then phantom unmounts at the end and motion snaps to lead-only render, perceived as "fades back in." **Fix**: `preserveOpacity` is set on the previous lead by `promote()` (motion source line 8035) when called with `preserveFollowOpacity: true`. The promotion config comes from `node.options.initialPromotionConfig.shouldPreserveFollowOpacity(node)`, which is read from `<SwitchLayoutGroupContext.Provider>` in the React tree. With `shouldPreserveFollowOpacity: () => true`, every promote() preserves the follow's explicit opacity instead of running the auto fade-out. **Implementation**: import `SwitchLayoutGroupContext` from `motion/react` (re-exports framer-motion 12.38). Wrap the existing `<LayoutGroup>` in `<SwitchLayoutGroupContext.Provider value={{ shouldPreserveFollowOpacity: () => true }}>`. The context type is marked "Internal, exported only for usage in Framer" in framer-motion's d.ts but is publicly exported and stable across framer-motion 12.x. **Files modified**: `host-profile.tsx` (~25 LOC delta — import SwitchLayoutGroupContext from motion/react, wrap LayoutGroup with the Provider). **Verification**: `pnpm tsc --noEmit` clean; `pnpm lint` 0 errors. Manual smoke pending: close animation should now hold phantom chip + text at constant opacity 1 throughout exit (no auto fade-out at progress 0.5-0.95), so the user no longer sees text fade out / disappear / fade back in mid-close. |
| B.PT187 | Visitor-page redesign — RE-RESTORE per-frame layoutIds + opacity pinning (B.PT186 reverted the wrong way; codex/chatgpt's fix was correct) | SHIPPED | `cadd6a7` | User after B.PT186: "its wrong now, you introduced a bug that had already been fixed by chatgpt." Provided the original codex/chatgpt prompt that landed the fix, which made it explicit: nested shared chip layers needed BOTH stable layoutIds AND explicit opacity-1 pinning (initial/animate/exit) to prevent motion's close-time shared-element crossfade from fading a second text copy in early. **Why my B.PT186 motion-source diagnosis drew the wrong conclusion**: the path-check at `mixValues` line ~9123 (`!this.path.some(hasOpacityCrossfade)`) actually SUPPRESSES the auto-crossfade on a child IF an ancestor already has `opacityExit` set. The outer chip's crossfade always sets opacityExit → inner spans' own crossfade is correctly skipped — but ONLY if the inner spans (a) have layoutId so they're recognized as shared-layout AND (b) have explicit opacity values pinned so motion doesn't fall through to the entrance/exit fade branch. Stripping layoutIds in B.PT186 made the inner spans single-element layout-tracked, so on close the landing's inner spans MOUNTED FRESH (no shared identity with the modal's exiting copies), and motion ran the standard entrance fade-in on them — producing the 0.7-progress text pop-in the codex prompt originally called out. **Fix**: restore the three derived layoutIds (`<parent>-frame-9`, `<parent>-frame-17`, `<parent>-frame-12`) on the inner motion.span elements, restore the `initial`/`animate`/`exit` opacity-1 pinning. Keep `layoutAnchor` removed (motion source confirms it's a projection-internal option, not a component prop, silently ignored). **Files modified**: `host-profile.tsx` SlotRow (~25 LOC delta — re-derive frame9/text/duration layoutIds, re-apply layoutId + opacity pin to the 3 inner motion.span). **Verification**: `pnpm tsc --noEmit` clean; `pnpm lint` 0 errors. Manual smoke: close animation should hold text fixed at top-left throughout the morph, no 0.7-progress pop-in. |
| B.PT186 | Visitor-page redesign — REVERT B.PT184 per-frame layoutIds (root cause of close-direction text fade-out-then-in glitch) | SHIPPED | `eabf9b9` | User after B.PT184: "the issue is still happening when animating from layer 2 to layer 1 not 1 to 2 1 to 2 works fine but when the animation is from 2 to 1 the text when its about 0.4 complete it fades out then it fades in for some reason can we find the root cause as to why and how to fix it? not just guessing a real cause." User wanted ROOT CAUSE, not guessing. **Root cause (verified against motion source @ `node_modules/motion/dist/motion.dev.js`)**: motion's shared-layout `mixValues` function (line ~9123) UNCONDITIONALLY overrides explicit `animate.opacity` when `shouldCrossfadeOpacity === true`. It sets `target.opacity = mixNumber(0, lead.opacity, easeCrossfadeIn(progress))` and `target.opacityExit = mixNumber(follow.opacity, 0, easeCrossfadeOut(progress))`. The render selector at line ~9416 picks `valuesToRender.opacityExit` for the FOLLOW element instead of `latestValues.opacity` — unless `preserveOpacity` is set (which requires `SwitchLayoutGroupContext.Provider` with `shouldPreserveFollowOpacity`, framer-motion-internal API). `easeCrossfadeIn = compress(0, 0.5, circOut)` and `easeCrossfadeOut = compress(0.5, 0.95, noop)` are asymmetric s-curves: lead fades in over progress 0-0.5, follow fades out over 0.5-0.95. **Why close glitched but open didn't**: lead election uses "latest mounted." On open the phantom is unambiguously latest (just mounted). On CLOSE the phantom is STILL marked lead (it was mounted later than the persistently-mounted landing) until it actually unmounts via `relegate()` (motion source line 9908). So motion drives the LANDING text's opacity through `easeCrossfadeOut` (1→0 over progress 0.5-0.95) as if it were the exiting element. Then phantom unmounts mid-animation, `relegate()` promotes landing to lead, opacity jumps back to 1 — visible as "text fades out, disappears, then fades back in." **Why B.PT184 caused this**: the per-frame layoutIds (`-frame-9`, `-frame-17`, `-frame-12`) made each inner span a SHARED-LAYOUT element with its own crossfade. The outer chip's crossfade was already running — adding crossfade to inner spans compounded the artifact. Codex's earlier commit `0755eb1` had `<motion.span layout>` and `<motion.span layout="position">` WITHOUT layoutId on the inner spans — they were single-element layout-tracked, riding the parent chip's transform without their own shared-layout crossfade. **Fix**: revert B.PT184. Drop layoutId from the three inner motion.span elements. Drop the `initial`/`animate`/`exit` opacity pinning (no longer needed — without their own layoutId, no auto-crossfade applies to them). Drop `layoutAnchor` props — also-found-during-research that motion's `layoutAnchor` is an internal projection option (`this.options.layoutAnchor` per source line 9139) that isn't exposed as a component prop, so the agent's `layoutAnchor={{x:0,y:0}}` etc. were being silently dropped anyway. **Files modified**: `host-profile.tsx` SlotRow (~25 LOC removed — drop frame9LayoutId/textLayoutId/durationLayoutId derivations, drop layoutId/layoutAnchor/opacity-pin props on the 3 inner motion.span elements). **Verification**: `pnpm tsc --noEmit` clean; `pnpm lint` 0 errors. Manual smoke pending: close animation should now show text staying at constant opacity (no mid-progress fade-out-then-in glitch). The outer chip's layoutId crossfade remains (B.PT181/183 setup) — it's the only layoutId on the slot subtree and only fires once per morph. |
