Default load of `?debug=1` should drop directly into the dual-layer inspection workflow (which is the entire reason for the debug panel existing). User can flip back to `production` via the dropdown to verify the live morph plays as expected. **Files modified**: `_components/visitor-debug-overlay.tsx` (~5 LOC delta — `mode.value` flipped from `"production"` to `"inspect"`, comment updated). **Verification**: `pnpm tsc --noEmit` clean; `pnpm lint` 0 errors. |
| B.PT184 | Visitor-page redesign — per-frame layoutId on SlotRow inner spans (no close-animation text fade glitch at ~70-80% progress) | SHIPPED | `b54c80f` | User after the smart-animate restructure (`0755eb1`): "okay so animation in works perfectly, animation out almost does, the issue with layer 2 -> layer 1 now is that when we start animating to layer 1 when its about the 0.7-8 progress almost done the text inside the chip fades out, dissappears, and then fades in again for some reason." Symptom of motion's automatic shared-layout crossfade tweening the inner text + duration spans' opacity during the morph. **Fix**: extend the Figma-layer-faithful `layoutId` hierarchy that `0755eb1` introduced (`oh-slot-stack`, `oh-slot-N` directly on the SlotRow) one level deeper — the inner spans inside SlotRow each get their own layoutId derived from the parent slot's layoutId: Frame 9 (chip row body) → `<parent>-frame-9`, Text block → `<parent>-frame-17`, Duration label → `<parent>-frame-12`. Each span pins `initial`/`animate`/`exit` opacity to 1, so motion's shared-layout crossfade can't tween cloned text layers in/out during the morph. The layoutId-based FLIP morph runs on each inner element independently of the parent slot's animation, matching how Figma Smart Animate matches matched layers across frames. **Files modified**: `host-profile.tsx` SlotRow (~17 LOC delta — derive 3 layoutIds, apply to existing motion.span elements, pin opacity). **Verification**: `pnpm tsc --noEmit` clean; `pnpm lint` 0 errors. |
| B.PT183 | Visitor-page redesign — drop inverse-correction wrappers entirely (chip width interpolates with parent transform, Figma fill-container behavior) | SHIPPED | `5130c5e` | User after B.PT182, observing slow-mo: "the chip spawns in the begging as max width already... in figma you can see that yes the final state of the chip is really big in width but it interpolates there is not like it spawns the already max width and then moves." User explained the Figma approach: outer container animates, chips set to "fill container" in width, smart animate naturally interpolates chip width as container grows. **Diagnosis (Context7-verified motion docs)**: motion's `<motion.div layout="size">` inverse-correction wrapper FREEZES the visible size of children at their natural CSS rect (e.g., 690 wide in modal context, 325 in landing). Even with `layout="size"` (which avoids inverse-translation per B.PT182's research), the size correction still keeps content at natural CSS dimensions, decoupled from the parent's transform-based animation. Net visual: outer chip rect interpolates from landing to modal width via FLIP transform, but content inside the wrapper renders at the modal's natural width from frame 1. User observes "chip spawns at max width". **Fundamental trade-off**: motion's inverse-correction protects against text/avatar distortion during morph (the B.PT180 fix), but at the cost of width-interpolation. To match Figma's continuous interpolation where chip rect AND content visually grow together, the wrapper has to come off. Content (text + duration label) WILL scale with the parent's transform mid-flight — briefly distorted in proportion to the parent's scale delta — but that's how Figma's Smart-Animate fill-container works: contents follow auto-layout as container grows. **Fix**: remove the three `<motion.div layout="size">` wrappers entirely (host-profile.tsx slot wrapper from B.PT162, handle-modal.tsx identity wrapper + slot wrapper from B.PT180/B.PT182). Render `<SlotRow>` and `identityContent` directly inside the layoutId-bearing motion.div. **Trade-off documented**: brief mid-flight text distortion when phantom rect grows from 325×50 → 690×282 (text scales 2.12× horizontally + 5.64× vertically at frame 0 of the morph, interpolating back to 1× at endpoint). This is the canonical Figma behavior — the cost of true fill-container interpolation. User explicitly opted into this trade-off (asked for fill-container behavior). **Files modified**: `host-profile.tsx` (~30 LOC delta — remove the wrapper around SlotRow in slot rendering, fix dangling closing tag); `handle-modal.tsx` (~25 LOC delta — remove wrapper around identityContent + SlotRow in phantom rendering). **Files NOT touched**: `_components/visitor-debug-overlay.tsx`. **Verification**: `pnpm tsc --noEmit` clean; `pnpm lint` 0 errors (baseline 7 warnings unchanged). Manual smoke pending: `?debug=1` → set duration to 1.5s for slow-mo → click chip → outer chip rect AND its content visually interpolate from landing rect (325×50) to modal phantom rect (690×282) together; chip width grows continuously with container width; brief text distortion mid-flight matching Figma's Smart-Animate behavior. |
| B.PT182 | Visitor-page redesign — inverse-correction wrappers use layout="size" not layout (chip CONTENT moves with rect during morph, not frozen at landing position) | SHIPPED | `a994216` | User after B.PT181: "in production mode i see the outer container of the chips work correctly or seems like it with the behaviour of like 'snapping' out of place but the chips i just see them 'fadeout' rather than move to the position that they are supposed to move to." User watching in slow-motion (B.PT178/179 duration override) sees outer slot rect translate to modal coords correctly, but the SlotRow content (text + duration label) stuck at landing rect, fading in place. **Diagnosis (Context7-verified motion docs)**: B.PT162 + B.PT180's inverse-correction wrappers used `<motion.div layout>` which defaults to `layout={true}` — motion's docs explicitly call out three modes for the prop: `true` (animate ALL layout changes), `"position"` (position only), `"size"` (size only). With the default `true`, motion tracks BOTH the wrapper's position AND size. When the OUTER layoutId rect (the chip's parent motion.div) morphs from landing to modal coords via FLIP transform, the inner wrapper's bounding box ALSO changes (because the parent's transform composes with CSS positioning to render the wrapper at a new absolute position). Motion catches the wrapper's bounding-box change and runs its OWN inverse FLIP — including inverse-translation. Net: wrapper stays at its previous absolute position (landing rect), even as the outer rect moves to modal coords. User sees the outer rect translate visibly, the SlotRow content frozen at landing position fading out via the AnimatePresence exit. Same logic afflicted the identity wrapper (B.PT180's modal-side) and the slot wrapper on landing (B.PT162). The wrappers were doing their JOB for scale-distortion correction, but were over-correcting by also inverse-translating. **Fix**: change `<motion.div layout>` to `<motion.div layout="size">` on all three inverse-correction wrappers (host-profile slot wrapper at line 553, handle-modal identity wrapper, handle-modal slot wrapper). Per motion docs, `layout="size"` tracks only size changes. The wrapper still inverse-corrects the parent's scale (so text/avatar don't stretch when phantom rect grows from 325×50 to 690×282) but doesn't try to inverse-translate — CSS-driven positioning lets the wrapper move with the parent's transform naturally. **Trade-off**: if the wrapper's content has its own layout changes mid-animation (none expected here — SlotRow is static once rendered), they wouldn't animate. Acceptable for this use case. **Files modified**: `host-profile.tsx` (~12 LOC delta — `layout` → `layout="size"` on slot wrapper, comment expanded with the diagnosis); `handle-modal.tsx` (~20 LOC delta — same change on identity wrapper + slot wrapper, both with diagnostic comment). **Verification**: `pnpm tsc --noEmit` clean; `pnpm lint` 0 errors (baseline 7 warnings unchanged). Manual smoke pending: `?debug=1` → set duration to 1.5s for slow-mo → click chip → outer rect AND inner content should both move from landing position to modal phantom position, content keeping its natural CSS size throughout. Same on close. |
| B.PT181 | Visitor-page redesign — initial=animate on layoutId elements (no fade-in clone during morph; matches Figma single-element) | SHIPPED | `8099102` | User watching the slow-motion morph: "click chip animation start -> chip 'pops out' of place and starts moving to the new place, arrives to place and settles. NOT INTENDED: chip click animation starts -> clone appears opacity 0, original starts fading out -> 2 visible at same time one 0.2 , 0.7 the original -> visible simultaneously. THIS DOESNT HAPPEN IN FIGMA." User reports the morph reads as a CROSSFADE (two elements with interpolating opacity) rather than a single element transitioning. **Diagnosis (Context7-verified motion docs)**: motion's layoutId animation runs a FLIP (transform: scale + translate) AND, when both source and destination are mounted simultaneously, an opacity crossfade. Even with B.PT169's explicit `animate={{ opacity }}` overriding the crossfade TARGET, motion was still doing an "enter" tween: phantom mounts → motion's default initial opacity is the layoutId-source's value (or 0 if no precedent) → animate target → opacity tweens 0→target. The user saw this as the "clone fading in" alongside the source's exit. **Fix**: add `initial={{ opacity: <same as animate> }}` on every layoutId-bearing motion element. With initial = animate, motion has nothing to interpolate on mount — element starts at its target opacity, the FLIP animates rect (transform) only, no opacity tween runs. Mirrors how Figma's Smart-Animate transitions a single element between frames: position + size morph, opacity stays at the spec value throughout. **Behavior in production mode** (no debug overlay): landing animate.opacity=1, phantom animate.opacity=0. With initial=animate now: landing stays at 1 throughout exit, phantom never visible (initial=0). Net visual = landing's rect transforms into the phantom's rect — single element. **Behavior in inspect mode**: both elements mounted simultaneously at opacity 1 (debug). User INSPECTS both layers — the dual visibility is the point of inspect mode, not a bug. Crossfade-free morph applies in production; inspect mode keeps showing both layers as designed (B.PT172). **Files modified**: `host-profile.tsx` (~30 LOC delta — add `initial={{ opacity: ... }}` on motion.article handle-card, motion.header oh-identity, motion.div oh-slot-list, each motion.div oh-slot-N); `handle-modal.tsx` (~25 LOC delta — same on motion.article modal, motion.div oh-identity, motion.div oh-slot-list, each motion.div oh-slot-N). **Verification**: `pnpm tsc --noEmit` clean; `pnpm lint` 0 errors (baseline 7 warnings unchanged). Manual smoke pending: `?debug=1` → set duration to 1.5s for slow-mo → set mode=production → click chip → only ONE chip visible at any moment, transitioning from landing rect to modal rect via FLIP. No clone-fade-in. Same on close. |
| B.PT180 | Visitor-page redesign — modal phantom inverse-scale wrappers (chip content stops squishing during morph) | SHIPPED | `0fffbda` | User after B.PT179, watching the morph in slow-motion: "the container itself is like shrinking the content vertically so it looks all shrunk down like if you ignored aspect ratio of a circle or text and just decreased it height" — chips squished/elongated mid-morph and at destination. **Diagnosis (Context7-verified motion docs)**: motion's layoutId animation animates element size via CSS `transform: scale()`. The slot phantom rect goes from 325×50 (landing source) → 690×282 (modal destination) — 2.12× wider, 5.64× taller scale transform. Per motion docs: *"layout animations that change size can distort child components. This can be corrected by providing child components with a `layout` prop as well. Only immediate children need to be corrected for scale distortion."* The LANDING side already had the wrapper (B.PT162: `<motion.div layout>` around `<SlotRow>` inside each `oh-slot-N` motion.div). The MODAL phantom side did NOT — `<SlotRow>` rendered as a direct child of the layoutId motion.div, so the phantom's scale transform applied to the chip's text + duration label, distorting them. **Symptom asymmetry**: open morph (landing → phantom) was worst because the phantom enters at the source's transformed rect and the missing wrapper meant content was distorted from frame 1; rollback (phantom → landing) showed the squish less because the landing's wrapper takes over at end-state. **Fix**: add `<motion.div layout transition={...}>` wrapper around the rendered content inside both `oh-identity` and `oh-slot-N` phantoms in `handle-modal.tsx`, mirroring the landing-side B.PT162 pattern. Identity sizes match closely (336×87 both sides) so distortion was small there, but adding the wrapper is symmetric + future-proof if either side's natural size shifts. The slot wrapper is the load-bearing fix. **Files modified**: `handle-modal.tsx` (~30 LOC delta — wrap `identityContent` in motion.div layout when keepLandingMounted + showLandingAtDest are on; wrap `<SlotRow>` in motion.div layout in each phantom slot). **Files NOT touched**: `host-profile.tsx` — landing already has the wrapper for slots (since B.PT162); landing identity rect matches modal phantom identity rect closely so no wrapper needed there; if a future audit finds distortion, add symmetrically. **Verification**: `pnpm tsc --noEmit` clean; `pnpm lint` 0 errors (baseline 7 warnings unchanged). Manual smoke pending: with B.PT178 duration set high (e.g. 1.5s) so the user can watch in slow motion: open modal → phantom chips should now render at natural size (50px tall content) at the top of each 282-tall phantom rect throughout the morph, no vertical stretching/squishing. Same on rollback. Mirrors the Smart-Animate behavior the user described from Figma. |
| B.PT179 | Visitor-page redesign — landing transitions read closeSpring on rollback (duration override now applies to close animation too) | SHIPPED | `1753904` | User after B.PT178: "works but the animation of rolling back should also be affected only the 'in' is use docs if necessary." Forward (open) morph respected the new `visualDuration` slider; rollback (close) didn't. **Diagnosis (Context7 motion docs)**: motion's layoutId crossfade applies the **destination element's transition** to the FLIP animation. On modal open the destination is the modal phantom — its transition is wired to read `(open ? openSpring : closeSpring)` from the debug context (see `handle-modal.tsx`). On modal close the destination is the landing — but `host-profile.tsx`'s 5 motion elements (article + identity header + slot-list + each of 4 slot rows) hardcoded `transition={{ type: "spring", ...OPEN_SPRING }}` referencing the spec constant directly with NO debug-context wiring. So the debug-overlay duration override applied to all three springs in synthesis (B.PT178) but only the modal phantom side actually consumed `closeSpring`. The landing's transition fell through to the hardcoded OPEN_SPRING constant on every direction. **Fix**: (a) Add a `CLOSE_SPRING` constant to `host-profile.tsx` (parallel to the existing `OPEN_SPRING`), pulled from the same Figma anim spec via the documented `handle-detail` → `handle` transition path. (b) Read `openSpring` / `closeSpring` from `useModalDebug()` like `handle-modal.tsx` already does, falling back to the file-level constants. (c) Replace all 5 hardcoded `transition={{ type: "spring", ...OPEN_SPRING }}` with `transition={{ type: "spring", ...(drawerOpen ? openSpring : closeSpring) }}` — symmetric to the modal's `(open ? openSpring : closeSpring)` pattern. **Why drawerOpen and not !drawerOpen?** When modal is OPENING, `drawerOpen` is true; landing is exiting. Motion picks the entering side's transition (modal phantom) — landing's value here doesn't matter for the open morph. When modal is CLOSING, `drawerOpen` is false; landing is entering. Motion picks the entering side (landing) → landing's transition becomes `closeSpring`. ✓ **Files modified**: `host-profile.tsx` (~15 LOC delta — add CLOSE_SPRING const + fallback parsing of the spec, add openSpring/closeSpring debug reads, swap the 5 transition uses to the drawerOpen ternary). **Files NOT touched**: `_components/visitor-debug-overlay.tsx`, `handle-modal.tsx` — synthesis already emitted closeSpring with duration override applied; only the consumer side needed wiring up. **Verification**: `pnpm tsc --noEmit` clean; `pnpm lint` 0 errors (baseline 7 warnings unchanged). Manual smoke pending: `?debug=1` → set Spring tuning duration to e.g. 1.5s → open modal → close modal → BOTH animations now play over 1.5s; reset duration to 0 → both restore Figma-spec physics. |
| B.PT178 | Visitor-page redesign — debug spring tuning gains visualDuration + bounce sliders (time-based override of physics) | SHIPPED | `8e0bcbd` | User after B.PT177: "it works, now in the spring tuning could you also add the time of the animation takes that should also be moddable." Asked to control morph duration directly. **Researched motion docs via Context7**: motion supports `visualDuration` (seconds for the spring to visually reach target) + `bounce` (0-1) as time-based alternatives to physics (mass/stiffness/damping). Per docs: "Bounce and duration are overridden if stiffness, damping, or mass are set." So a transition object can contain EITHER physics OR duration, never both — physics wins when both present. **What landed**: two new sliders inside the existing `Spring tuning` Leva folder: `visualDuration` (seconds, default 0, range 0..3, step 0.05) and `bounce` (default 0.25, range 0..1, step 0.05). Sentinel: `visualDuration === 0` means "use physics" (existing mass/stiffness/damping/velocity drive the spring). Any value > 0 flips ALL three springs (open / confirm / close) to duration mode — synthesis emits `{visualDuration, bounce, velocity}` (per-spring velocity preserved because it's not overridden by duration mode). One global duration applies to all three rather than per-spring sliders so the user can dial overall morph speed without the panel growing six new sliders. **Type changes**: `SpringValues` is now a discriminated union of `{mass, stiffness, damping, velocity}` OR `{visualDuration, bounce, velocity}` — no `kind` discriminator field; consumer narrows via `'visualDuration' in spring`. Without the discriminator field, the spread into motion's transition object stays clean (no stray keys). The two existing constants (`OPEN_SPRING` etc.) read from `docs/figma/anim-h-handle-redesign.json` still satisfy the physics arm of the union, so consumer code doesn't change. **Files modified**: `_components/visitor-debug-overlay.tsx` (~50 LOC delta — refactor SpringValues type, add 2 sliders inside Spring tuning folder, refactor synthesis with `springFor()` helper that emits the right shape based on the duration sentinel, add the new ctrl keys to useMemo dep list). **Files NOT touched**: `host-profile.tsx`, `handle-modal.tsx` — both already spread `...openSpring` etc. into their transition objects; spread of either union arm produces a valid motion transition. **Verification**: `pnpm tsc --noEmit` clean; `pnpm lint` 0 errors (baseline 7 warnings unchanged). Manual smoke pending: `?debug=1` → expand Spring tuning folder → set duration to e.g. 1.5s → open modal → morph plays over 1.5s instead of physics-driven ~0.4s; bounce slider audibly changes the settle. Reset duration to 0 to restore physics-driven behavior. |
| B.PT177 | Visitor-page redesign — LayoutGroup wraps both AnimatePresence containers + drawerOpen-conditioned layoutId strip (inspect morph survives prior production cycle) | SHIPPED | `421367e` | User after B.PT176: "it all works the only issue now is that, it only works if i trigger the modal when i click in 'mode' inspect BEFORE i trigger the modal so if i happen to trigger the modal with the 'production' mode on it, and then switch to inspect mode it just does like the fade-inout animation rather than the intended animation that does happen only if i trigger the modal for the first time with inspect mode on as i mentioned." Specific reproducible bug: inspect-mode morph plays only on FIRST modal open after entering inspect; if a production-mode modal cycle preceded the mode switch, the next inspect-mode open just fades. **Diagnosis (Context7 motion docs)**: two distinct issues. (1) Our setup has TWO sibling `<AnimatePresence mode="popLayout">` containers (one for landing, one for modal) sharing layoutIds across them. Per motion docs: "Use LayoutGroup to coordinate layout animations between AnimatePresence children and external components when mixing exit and layout animations." We had no `<LayoutGroup>` wrapping them, so motion's per-layoutId rect coordination across the AnimatePresence boundaries was best-effort — robust on a clean tree, fragile after a unmount/remount cycle in one of the containers. (2) The B.PT175 warm-up strip fired as soon as `mounted && keepLandingMounted` flipped to true (mode change in production-mode → inspect-mode); on a still-mounted landing motion's cache could go stale because the strip triggered an immediate "this layoutId no longer applies" signal without a fresh measurement to fall back on. Combined with the cross-AnimatePresence cache fragility, mode switches AFTER a production cycle would invalidate the cache → no source rect → fade-only. **Fix (a): wrap both AnimatePresence containers in `<LayoutGroup>`**. Imported `LayoutGroup` from `motion/react`. Wrapped the landing-AnimatePresence + the modal-AnimatePresence in a single `<LayoutGroup>` inside `<OhVisitorShell>`. Per docs this is the documented coordination primitive for sibling AnimatePresences sharing layoutIds. **Fix (b): strip layoutIds only when modal is OPENING** (`stripLandingLayoutId = mounted && keepLandingMounted && drawerOpen`). Layout IDs stay ACTIVE on the landing for as long as the modal is closed — motion always has a fresh rect measurement on the most recent render. The strip fires in the same render as `drawerOpen=true`, so motion's last-recorded rect (from the previous render's measurement) becomes the FLIP source for the modal phantoms. Sequence trace verified: page load → (production cycle: open modal, morph plays, close modal, landing remounts, motion records fresh rect) → switch to inspect mode (drawerOpen=false → no strip → cache fresh) → open modal in inspect (drawerOpen=true → strip fires this render → phantoms find cache → morph plays). **Files modified**: `host-profile.tsx` (~25 LOC delta — import LayoutGroup, wrap both AnimatePresence containers, move stripLandingLayoutId derivation to AFTER drawerOpen state declaration, add drawerOpen to the strip condition). **Files NOT touched**: `_components/visitor-debug-overlay.tsx`, `handle-modal.tsx` — neither needs changes; the strip-coordination + LayoutGroup are both consumer-side concerns. **Verification**: `pnpm tsc --noEmit` clean; `pnpm lint` 0 errors (baseline 7 warnings unchanged). Manual smoke pending: load `?debug=1` → mode=production → open modal → close → switch to inspect → open modal → spring morph plays (was fade-only before this commit). Also: load fresh in inspect → open modal → still works (warm-up + LayoutGroup don't interfere). |
| B.PT176 | Visitor-page redesign — per-element phantom opacity sliders + element-name labels (fix slot-list parent opacity bug) | SHIPPED | `49a7a29` | User after B.PT175: "the issue now is that for some reason in inspect mode i do see the final state of the title of the h/turbius i do see it morphing to that place but for the time chips i dont see them at all why am i able to see the title but not the chips? is it possible to add any controls so that i can debug in inspect mode more controls so that i can see what is exactly going wrong either way research trusted sources based on this information what could be the issue and how could we fix it." **Diagnosis (Context7-verified motion docs)**: motion's `animate.opacity` resolves to a CSS `opacity` declaration on the rendered element. CSS opacity composes **multiplicatively** with parent opacity (effective_opacity = parent × child × …). The simplified panel synthesis in B.PT173 hardcoded `oLayer2.slotList = 0` because the slot-list phantom's RECT is full-modal-coverage (`inset: 0`) and at the time we reasoned it should stay invisible while only the slot CHILDREN got opacity. Wrong move: `<motion.div layoutId="oh-slot-list">` is the DOM PARENT of the 4 `<motion.div layoutId="oh-slot-N">` children in `handle-modal.tsx`. Parent at opacity 0 → children at effective 0 regardless of their own opacity slider. Title (identity phantom) is a SIBLING to slot-list, not nested inside it, so identity at opacity 1 worked → user could see title at destination but never the chips. **Fix**: replaced the single `phantomOpacity` master slider with three explicit per-element sliders: `identityOpacity` / `slotListOpacity` / `slotsOpacity` (uniform across slot0..3 — 4 separate sliders would be diagnostic-overkill). Default 1.0 across the board in inspect mode. The `slotListOpacity` default at 1.0 fixes the chip-invisibility bug (no more parent-opacity zeroing). User can now zero individual elements to isolate which is misbehaving. **Plus a `showLabels` toggle**: when on, overlays a small mono-caps text badge on each phantom rect ("identity" amber, "slot list" fuchsia, "slot 0..3" cyan, distinct colors so the user sees at a glance which phantom is at which screen position). Useful for diagnosing layout overlap, parent-child structure questions, and morph-target verification. Renders only in inspect mode (when phantoms are visible). **Type changes**: added `showPhantomLabels: boolean` to `ModalDebugValues`; the synthesis maps `showPhantomLabels = inspect && ctrls.showLabels`. **Files modified**: `_components/visitor-debug-overlay.tsx` (~50 LOC delta — drop `phantomOpacity` slider, add 3 per-element opacity sliders + showLabels toggle, add `showPhantomLabels` field on type, fix synthesis to drive each oLayer2 element from its own slider); `components/handle-modal.tsx` (~25 LOC delta — read `phantomLabels` from debug, render `<span>` badges inside each phantom rect when on; positions: identity = top-right amber, slot-list = top-left fuchsia, slot-N = top-right cyan with index). **Verification**: `pnpm tsc --noEmit` clean; `pnpm lint` 0 errors (baseline 7 warnings unchanged). Manual smoke pending: `?debug=1` → mode=inspect → chips should now be visible at destination by default (slot-list defaults to 1 instead of hardcoded 0); flipping `show element labels` ON should overlay color-coded badges identifying each phantom. |
| B.PT175 | Visitor-page redesign — useMounted-deferred layoutId strip + identity-phantom content (debug morph plays without manual toggle dance) | SHIPPED | `ef73063` | User after a wip-branch revert: "i see the chips animate correctly to their last place but the way its done is that i need to trigger some of the triggers and toggles arbitrarly, steps: 1. keep landing modal mounted is turned on by default, i turn it off and then the animation works again when its turned by default intially the animation [doesn't] work it just does like fade in-out, 2. turn it back on, now the animation works, 3. open modal, layer 2 settings changed: phantom identity opacity set to 1 and every setting below it also set to 1 that is able to see the final state of the chips but not the final state of the title that i havent been able in any way to see it for some reason." Two distinct issues: (a) the animation only works after a manual `keepLandingMounted` OFF→ON toggle dance, (b) identity title invisible at destination even with phantom identity opacity at 1. **Diagnosis (a)**: `keepLandingMounted=true` from initial render strips landing's layoutIds (via `landingLayoutId(id) => undefined`). Per Context7-verified motion docs, "When a new element enters the DOM with a matching `layoutId`, it animates from the previous element's size and position" — but if no previous element with that layoutId was ever rendered, motion has no source rect → modal phantoms enter with no FLIP source → fade-only entrance. The OFF→ON workaround temporarily mounted the landing WITH layoutIds during the OFF state, letting motion record the rects; motion's per-layoutId rect cache persists even after the element re-renders without that layoutId, so the ON re-strip leaves the cache intact → next modal open uses the cached rects as the FLIP source. **Fix (a)**: defer the layoutId strip by one render via `useMounted()` (project's canonical hook at `src/hooks/use-mounted.ts`, useSyncExternalStore-backed so it sidesteps the React 19 setState-in-effect ESLint rule). `useMounted()` is `false` on SSR + first client paint and `true` after — during the false phase landing renders WITH its layoutIds and motion records the rects; the post-mount re-render strips them if `keepLandingMounted` is on; cached rects survive → next modal open plays the spring morph. Same effect as the OFF→ON dance, automated. **Diagnosis (b)**: `handle-modal.tsx`'s `oh-identity` phantom motion.div was self-closing (empty rect at 336×87 / pos 192,22). Slot phantoms render `<SlotRow>` content per B.PT172, but the identity phantom was never given parallel treatment. Bumping `phantomIdentityOpacity` to 1 just made the empty rect's outline more visible, not the avatar/title. **Fix (b)**: thread an `identityContent?: ReactNode` prop through `<HandleModal>`. The host-profile parent computes the avatar (`<Avatar>` with image fallback to initials, 55×55 with the spec's drop-shadow + 1px ring overlay) + the displayName `<h1>` inline and passes the JSX. The modal's identity phantom renders this content when `showLandingAtDest` is on (mirrors B.PT172's slot pattern). Tagline omitted because the 336×87 phantom matches Figma's identity-row size which is just avatar + h1 (tagline lives separately at the page level). **Re-applied from the reset wip branch (squashed into this single commit)**: B.PT173 simplified Leva panel and B.PT174 stack-order dropdown. The wip branch (`wip/ui-expose-simplify-attempt`) preserves the granular three-commit history; this commit lands all three concerns together because they're load-bearing on each other (the simplified panel's mode-coupling auto-sets `keepLandingMounted=true` in inspect mode → without warm-up, inspect mode is broken from the start). **Files modified**: `_components/visitor-debug-overlay.tsx` (442→306 LOC, full rewrite to mode-driven schema + stackOrder + spring-folder synthesis); `components/host-profile.tsx` (~50 LOC delta — useMounted import, `stripLandingLayoutId` derivation, position:relative paired with zIndex on motion.article, identityContent prop with avatar + h1 JSX); `components/handle-modal.tsx` (~10 LOC delta — `identityContent?: ReactNode` prop, ReactNode import, render inside oh-identity phantom when showLandingAtDest). **Verification**: `pnpm tsc --noEmit` clean; `pnpm lint` 0 errors (7 baseline warnings unchanged). Manual smoke pending user reload: expectation: `?debug=1`, switch to `mode=inspect`, open modal → spring morph plays without toggle dance; identity (avatar + name) visible at modal's identity-phantom rect alongside slot chips. |

| B.PT172 | Visitor-page redesign — "Keep landing mounted" defaults ON + renders chip content INSIDE phantom destination rects (frozen post-morph view) | SHIPPED | `f8d0a5a` | User after B.PT171: "set that as a default i still dont see like if i click keep mounted it just animates back to the layer 1 for some reason in no scenario do i see like the state frozen of the last position every item in layer 1 animates to, please research online if there is any way to do that and if so how could we implement it." Two distinct asks: (a) default `keepLandingMounted` to ON, and (b) actually deliver the "frozen at destination" inspection mode the user has been chasing since B.PT170. **Why B.PT171's approach didn't deliver "frozen at destination"**: keeping the landing's literal `layoutId` strings means motion treats the landing element AND the modal phantom as the same shared layout target. Per Context7-verified motion docs, "If the previous component remains in the tree, the two elements will crossfade" — but the lead/follow election is non-deterministic with two simultaneous mounts. In practice motion picked the LANDING (rendered first in the tree) as the lead, applied inverse transforms to the phantom (collapsing it to landing's center rect), and the user saw both elements at landing's origin position. Looked exactly like "animates back to layer 1." No combination of opacity/z-index sliders surfaces a post-morph view because there ISN'T one — both rects collide at the lead's coordinates. **Researched alternatives via Context7 motion docs**: (1) `LayoutGroup` namespacing — viable but creates two independent morph contexts, defeats the cross-fade contract for the production animation. (2) Force lead election via `key` prop ordering — fragile, motion docs explicitly say lead choice is implementation detail. (3) Strip layoutIds on landing + render REAL chip content INSIDE the phantom destination rects — clean: phantoms become "what landing looks like at modal coordinates" in plain DOM, no motion fight. Picked option 3. **What landed**: (a) `keepLandingMounted` Leva default flipped `false → true` so the toggle is ON by default for the inspection workflow. (b) Re-introduced the `landingLayoutId(id)` helper from B.PT170 in `host-profile.tsx` — when toggle is ON, all 4 landing motion elements (`handle-card`, `oh-identity`, `oh-slot-list`, `oh-slot-N`) drop their layoutIds, leaving them at center-of-page natural rect with no morph fight. (c) Exported `SLOT_OPTIONS` const + the `SlotRow` function component from `host-profile.tsx` (previously private). (d) `handle-modal.tsx` imports both, derives `showLandingAtDest = debug?.keepLandingMounted`, and renders `<SlotRow title="intro" description="quick chat, voice only" durationLabel={SLOT_OPTIONS[i].label}/>` INSIDE each of the 4 phantom slot motion.divs when `showLandingAtDest` is on. Phantom rects (top: 15+i*282, left: 15, 690×282) anchor each slot at modal-internal coordinates; the SlotRow content sits at top-left of each phantom rect at its CSS-natural 50px height — same behavior as the spec's "Frame 6/12 stay 50px while slot grows to 282" expansion. (e) Added `pointerEvents: "none"` on the phantom motion.divs unconditionally so they never intercept clicks on the modal interior. **Net behavior in this mode (default ON)**: open `/h/[handle]?debug=1` → landing visible at center (layoutIds stripped, no morph) + modal opens at its bigger rect with the 4 chip contents rendered AT their post-morph slot positions inside the modal. User can finally inspect what each chip "looks like at destination." **Trade-off documented**: this mode bypasses the spring morph for the slot chips (phantoms don't animate FROM landing — they just appear at their natural rects with content). To see the polished morph, toggle `keepLandingMounted` OFF — production defaults restore. **Required for visibility**: the user must bump the matching `Phantom slot N opacity` slider in the Layer 2 folder up from production-default 0 → 1 to see the rendered SlotRow content (the rect's opacity gates its children). Documented inline at the SlotRow callsite. **Files modified**: `_components/visitor-debug-overlay.tsx` (~3 LOC delta — flip `keepLandingMounted` default `false → true`, comment update), `components/host-profile.tsx` (~30 LOC delta — re-introduce `landingLayoutId(id)` helper, apply at 4 motion elements, export `SLOT_OPTIONS` + `SlotRow`), `components/handle-modal.tsx` (~50 LOC delta — import the exports, derive `showLandingAtDest`, open the self-closing phantom motion.divs to render `<SlotRow>` content conditionally, add `pointerEvents: "none"` defensively). **Verification**: `pnpm tsc --noEmit` clean; `pnpm lint` 0 errors (baseline 7 warnings unchanged); manual smoke: `?debug=1`, click a slot → landing stays at center + modal opens with 4 chip contents visible at post-morph slot positions (after bumping `Phantom slot N opacity` to 1.0). |
| B.PT171 | Visitor-page redesign — "Keep landing mounted" keeps layoutIds (landing renders AT phantom rect with content) | SHIPPED | `e50ad21` | User after B.PT170: "yes i mounted but is still in the inital positon the place where it animates to that is not possible like to see ... i tried decreasing modal opacity but still nothing ... i dont think any combination of like settings in the popup allows me to like see that version of layer 1". Right diagnosis: B.PT170 made landing persist but STRIPPED its layoutIds so it stayed at its initial center-of-page rect. User wanted the post-morph version — landing content rendered AT the phantom destination positions. **Fix**: revert B.PT170's layoutId stripping. Keep landing's layoutIds when `keepLandingMounted` is on. Per motion docs (Context7-verified): "If the previous component remains in the tree, the two elements will crossfade." With two simultaneous same-layoutId elements, motion picks the LEAD (latest mounted = modal phantom) and applies inverse transforms to the FOLLOW (landing) so it visually renders at the lead's rect. Both crossfade at the shared destination position. **Net behavior in this mode**: open modal with toggle ON → landing morphs into the phantom rects (e.g. each chip stretches from 325×50 → 690×282 at the modal's slot positions), with full content (text, time, etc.) rendered AT those destination rects. User sees what Layer 1 would look like at its post-morph position. Combine with Layer 1 opacity = 1 + Layer 2 phantom opacity = 0 to fully isolate Layer 1 content visible at destination. **Trade-off accepted by user**: in this mode the landing IS visually at the phantom rect (same as if morph "completed and stayed there"), not also at its origin position simultaneously. To see BOTH origin + destination, you'd need a duplicate-render pattern (separate layoutIds with content cloned) — a future polish row if needed. **Files modified**: `_components/visitor-debug-overlay.tsx` (~10 LOC delta — comment block update on `keepLandingMounted` describing the new layoutId-respecting behavior), `components/host-profile.tsx` (~10 LOC delta — drop the `landingLayoutId(id)` helper, restore literal `layoutId` strings on the 4 motion elements). **Verification**: `pnpm tsc --noEmit` clean; `pnpm lint` 0 errors (baseline 7 warnings unchanged); `pnpm exec playwright test e2e/hydration.spec.ts -g "/h/"` 3/3 PASS. Manual smoke: open `?debug=1`, toggle "Keep landing mounted" ON, click a slot — landing chip visibly stretches/morphs to the modal's chip phantom rect with content readable; no fade-out (per B.PT169 opacity controls). |
| B.PT170 | Visitor-page redesign — "Keep landing mounted" debug toggle (Layer 1 stays visible alongside modal) | SHIPPED | `be2d914` | User after B.PT169: "i still dont see the chips like content or anything actually on the top". Diagnosis: even with opacity locked to 1 via `animate`/`exit`, `<AnimatePresence>` still UNMOUNTS the landing card after the modal opens — landing chips with content disappear, only empty phantom rects remain. Phantoms have no content (just position+size for the layoutId morph target), so all the user sees is outlines. **The fix the user wanted (foreshadowed in B.PT168's caveat row)**: a "keep landing mounted" debug toggle that bypasses the AnimatePresence unmount so the landing card sits visible alongside the modal. **What landed**: NEW `keepLandingMounted: boolean` Leva control + `ModalDebugValues` field. When ON: (a) `host-profile.tsx`'s `<AnimatePresence>` renders the landing whenever `!drawerOpen \|\| keepLandingMounted` (instead of just when modal closed); (b) all landing motion elements have their `layoutId` stripped (passed through a `landingLayoutId(id)` helper that returns `undefined` in this mode). **Why strip layoutIds**: with two simultaneous elements sharing the same layoutId, motion would try to crossfade them at the same destination rect — both elements would visually collapse to one position. Stripping layoutIds on landing keeps it independent at its natural center-of-page rect; the modal's phantoms still have their layoutIds but no source rect, so they just enter at their natural position with no FLIP morph (acceptable trade — user is debugging dual-layer view, not running the polished morph). **Net behavior in this mode**: landing card centered on screen + modal opens overlay-style at its bigger rect; user sees BOTH simultaneously. Landing's full content (avatar, name, tagline, 4 chips with text+time) is visible on top of the modal scrim. Layer 1 opacity / z-index sliders remain effective on the persistent landing. Layer 2 sliders work on the modal as usual. **Default**: false (production-equivalent — landing unmounts as before). **Files modified**: `_components/visitor-debug-overlay.tsx` (~10 LOC delta — add Leva toggle, type field, useMemo mapping + dep), `components/host-profile.tsx` (~15 LOC delta — read `keepLandingMounted`, add `landingLayoutId(id)` helper, conditional render guard, swap layoutIds at 4 motion elements). **Verification**: `pnpm tsc --noEmit` clean; `pnpm lint` 0 errors (baseline 7 warnings unchanged); `pnpm exec playwright test e2e/hydration.spec.ts -g "/h/"` 3/3 PASS. Manual smoke: open `?debug=1`, toggle "Keep landing mounted" ON, click a slot to open modal — both layers visible simultaneously, landing stays at center with full content. |
| B.PT169 | Visitor-page redesign — opacity sliders override motion's auto crossfade (animate + exit) | SHIPPED | `1d94704` | User after B.PT168: "i set the opacity of the layer 1 to 1 so they are fully visible but because of the animation they still like turn back like opacity 0 wihtout destroying changes related to the animation logic is there any way that we could even maybe like tweak the value that it interpolates to say its 0 right now so tweak that value directly?" Right diagnosis: the B.PT168 sliders set `style.opacity` statically, but motion's `layoutId` matching auto-crossfades opacity FROM the source's value TO the destination's value during the morph. With a phantom destination at opacity 0 (production default), motion would tween the landing element's opacity from 1 → 0 even with `style.opacity: 1`. The slider only set the START point; motion overrode it with its own animation TARGET. **Fix**: replace static `style.opacity` with explicit `animate` + `exit` motion props. With `animate={{ opacity: oStyle(...) }}`, motion treats the slider value as the explicitly-animated target — overriding its auto-crossfade target with the user's value. `exit` covers the AnimatePresence unmount transition. Both props read the same Layer 1 / Layer 2 sliders. **Net behavior change**: when a Layer 1 chip's opacity slider is at 1.0, the chip stays visible at full opacity through the entire morph (positional FLIP still runs — only the opacity tween is locked to the slider). Same for Layer 2 phantoms. Production default unchanged: Layer 1 = 1.0 (visible), Layer 2 phantoms = 0.0 (invisible) — `oStyle(undefined, fallback)` returns the fallback when no debug. **Why this doesn't break the morph animation**: motion's layoutId animation animates THREE things: position (x/y), size (scaleX/scaleY), and opacity (crossfade). We're only overriding opacity with `animate`; position + size still animate via the spring transition unchanged. **Files modified**: `components/host-profile.tsx` (~30 LOC delta — replace `style.opacity` with `animate.opacity` + `exit.opacity` on landing card / identity / slot-list / each chip), `components/handle-modal.tsx` (~30 LOC delta — same pattern on modal article + each phantom). **Verification**: `pnpm tsc --noEmit` clean; `pnpm lint` 0 errors (baseline 7 warnings unchanged); `pnpm exec playwright test e2e/hydration.spec.ts -g "/h/"` 3/3 PASS. Manual smoke: open `?debug=1`, set Layer 1 chip opacity to 1, trigger modal — chip stays visible at full opacity through the spring instead of fading. |
| B.PT168 | Visitor-page redesign — per-scene-element opacity controls (Layer 1 + Layer 2) | SHIPPED | `cfb8904` | User after B.PT167's z-index controls: "why even when i put the 1 layer index bigger than anything and the phantom opacity i still just see the outline and not the actual thing at all like with max opacity, is it because th phantom opacity is not specific to like the layers or something? just the animation? what i want to be able to do is for the layers opacity to be tweakable for each one so for both layer 1 and 2." Right diagnosis: the `phantomOpacity` slider from B.PT159 only flipped the empty modal-phantom rects' style.opacity. They have NO content (just position + size for the layoutId morph target), so opacity 1 just made the outline visible. **What the user wanted** = per-layer + per-element opacity controls so they can fade either side of the morph independently and inspect either layer in isolation. Mirrors the z-index pattern from B.PT167 1:1. **What landed**: 14 new opacity sliders (range 0..1, step 0.05) in the existing Layer 1 / Layer 2 folders. Layer 1 controls default to **1.0** (visible — matches landing's production behavior); Layer 2 phantom controls default to **0.0** (invisible — matches modal phantoms' production behavior). Layer 2's "Modal article opacity" defaults to 1.0 (the modal itself, NOT a phantom — visible in production). Retired the `phantomOpacity` Leva input (replaced by the per-element controls); the field stays on the type for back-compat and mirrors `oLayer2.identity` so any external consumer still gets a sensible value. **Helper**: NEW `oStyle(v, fallback)` mirrors `zStyle(v)` — passes the slider through, falling back to a per-element production default when null. **Wiring**: each motion element now reads BOTH `zIndex: zStyle(...)` AND `opacity: oStyle(...)` in its `style` prop. **Honest caveat documented in-row**: Layer 1 opacity controls only TAKE EFFECT while the landing card is mounted (modal closed). Once the modal opens, `<AnimatePresence>` unmounts the landing — Layer 1 controls become inert. To inspect both layers simultaneously the user would need a "keep both layers mounted" debug toggle (a future polish row); flagged here, not blocking this commit. Layer 2 controls work both during the morph and after, since the modal stays mounted while open. **Files modified**: `_components/visitor-debug-overlay.tsx` (~95 LOC delta — drop `phantomOpacity` Leva input, add 14 opacity sliders inside Layer 1 / Layer 2 folders, extend `ModalDebugValues` with `oLayer1` + `oLayer2`, map flat ctrls in useMemo, add `oStyle` helper export), `components/host-profile.tsx` (~15 LOC delta — import `oStyle`, read `oL1`, apply `opacity: oStyle(...)` on landing card / identity / slot-list / each chip), `components/handle-modal.tsx` (~15 LOC delta — drop `phantomOpacity` consumption, read `oL2`, apply `opacity: oStyle(...)` on modal article + each phantom). **Verification**: `pnpm tsc --noEmit` clean; `pnpm lint` 0 errors (baseline 7 warnings unchanged); `pnpm exec playwright test e2e/hydration.spec.ts -g "/h/"` 3/3 PASS. Manual smoke: open `?debug=1`, expand Layer 1 / 2 folders, slide opacities — landing fades when Layer 1 < 1; modal article fades when Layer 2 layer < 1; phantoms grow visible (showing their empty rects) when Layer 2 phantom slots > 0. |
| B.PT167 | Visitor-page redesign — per-scene-element z-index controls (Layer 1 + Layer 2) | SHIPPED | `97484bd` | User after B.PT166's panel-z control: "what i meant is the z index of the whole layer 1 not of the popup itself, is that what you did? if so is there separation between the layer 1 and layer 2 items? if not should we somehow be able to select them in the popup so that i can set the z index individually for each of them". I had misread B.PT166 as the Leva panel's z-index (which it was — that ships separately and is still useful for stacking the debug UI vs the modal). This row adds what they actually wanted: scene-level z-index controls for the layered redesign elements. **Two folders** in the panel: (a) `Layer 1 (landing)` with controls for the whole landing layer + per-element overrides — landing card / identity header / slot-list cream card / slot 0..3 chips; (b) `Layer 2 (modal phantoms)` mirroring the same — modal card / phantom identity / phantom slot-list / phantom slot 0..3. **14 controls total**, each a slider -100..100 (default 0 = "no override", treat as `undefined` so CSS source-order stacking applies). Slide positive to bring an element forward, negative to push it back relative to siblings during the morph. **Why two folders**: shared layoutId pairs (e.g. `oh-slot-0` between landing visible chip + modal phantom) render as TWO separate DOM nodes during the morph cross-fade, each can have its own z-index. Layer 1 control affects the landing-side render, Layer 2 control affects the phantom-side render. Useful for debugging mid-flight visual stacking — e.g. "make the modal slot-list paint above the landing chips during the open transition" or "push the modal article behind a phantom". **Helper utility**: NEW `zStyle(v)` helper in the overlay file that maps slider value (0 → undefined; non-zero → that number) for use in `style={{ zIndex: zStyle(...) }}` callsites. **Wiring**: `host-profile.tsx` reads `useModalDebug().values.zLayer1` and applies on landing motion.article + motion.header + motion.div slot-list + each slot row's motion.div. `handle-modal.tsx` reads `zLayer2` and applies on modal motion.article + 3 phantom motion.divs (identity, slot-list wrapper, slot rows). All overrides default to undefined → no inline `zIndex` → no behavior change in production. **Files modified**: `_components/visitor-debug-overlay.tsx` (~95 LOC delta — extended `ModalDebugValues` type with `zLayer1` + `zLayer2`; added 14 Leva controls in two folders; mapped flat ctrls to typed structure in useMemo; new `zStyle` helper export), `components/host-profile.tsx` (~15 LOC delta — import `useModalDebug` + `zStyle`; read `zL1`; apply zIndex on landing card + header + slot-list + each chip), `components/handle-modal.tsx` (~10 LOC delta — read `zL2`; apply zIndex on modal article + identity phantom + slot-list phantom + each phantom slot). **Verification**: `pnpm tsc --noEmit` clean; `pnpm lint` 0 errors (baseline 7 warnings unchanged); `pnpm exec playwright test e2e/hydration.spec.ts -g "/h/"` 3/3 PASS. Manual smoke: open `?debug=1`, expand Layer 1 / Layer 2 folders, slide any z control — corresponding scene element re-stacks immediately. |
| B.PT166 | Visitor-page redesign — Leva panel z-index control vs modal stack | SHIPPED | `1ae2a76` | User after B.PT165: "is it possible to add to the modal like z-index or however is the best way in order to put the thing behind or not the modal". Added a Leva select control `Panel z-index` with three presets that straddle the modal's stacking layers (scrim z-40, dialog z-50): `above` (z-200, default — panel always on top), `between` (z-45 — panel sits between scrim + modal so it reads on top of the dimmed background but the modal content covers it; useful for inspecting modal drop-shadow / edge effects without panel obscuring), `below scrim` (z-30 — panel goes BEHIND the scrim and gets dimmed alongside the page; useful for seeing the modal "alone" without panel distraction). Implementation: panel wrapper div in `<VisitorDebugOverlay>` carries `style={{ position: "relative", zIndex }}`. The `position: relative` is required to establish a stacking context that contains Leva's internally-`position: fixed` panel — without it the fixed panel would escape to the body's root stacking context and ignore the wrapper's `zIndex`. **Files modified**: `_components/visitor-debug-overlay.tsx` (~20 LOC delta — add `panelZ` Leva control + map preset → numeric zIndex + apply on wrapper). **Verification**: `pnpm tsc --noEmit` clean; `pnpm lint` 0 errors (baseline 7 warnings unchanged); `pnpm exec playwright test e2e/hydration.spec.ts -g "/h/"` 3/3 PASS. Manual smoke: open modal in `?debug=1`, switch the `Panel z-index` selector — panel slides above/below the modal stacking layers as expected. |
| B.PT165 | Visitor-page redesign — Leva panel interactive when modal open (FocusOn shards) | SHIPPED | `b7602cd` | User after B.PT164: "the only issue is that when inside the modal you cant interact with it at all". Cause: `<FocusOn>` from `react-focus-on` (the focus-trap + scroll-lock + sibling-isolation primitive shipped in B.PT156) treats every DOM node OUTSIDE its own subtree as inert — pointer events are blocked, focus can't reach them, clicks fire `onClickOutside` which closes the modal. The Leva debug panel is a sibling of the modal at the body level, so it was caught in the isolation. **Fix**: pass the panel's container ref to `<FocusOn shards>`. Per `react-focus-on` docs (`shards: Array<RefObject | HTMLElement>`), shard refs are treated as "inside" the focus lock — clicks on them don't fire `onClickOutside`, focus can flow to them, sibling isolation skips them. **Implementation**: extended `useModalDebug()` context to return `{ values, panelShardRef }` instead of just `values`. The overlay creates a `useRef<HTMLDivElement>()` and attaches it to the wrapper around `<Leva />`. `<HandleModal>` reads the ref from the context and passes `shards={panelShardRef ? [panelShardRef] : undefined}` on its `<FocusOn>`. Production-safe: `panelShardRef` is null when `?debug=1` is absent → `undefined` shards → no-op. **Why a wrapper div works**: Leva's `<Leva isRoot=false>` (the default when used in a tree) renders the panel INLINE inside the parent, NOT via portal. Confirmed via `node_modules/leva/dist/leva.cjs.dev.js`: `isRoot: _isRoot = false` default. So the wrapper div ref captures the actual panel DOM. (If a future Leva version flips the default to `true` and portals to body, we'd need to switch to a `useEffect` + `document.querySelector` to find the portal target — flagged as a polish followup if it happens.) **Files modified**: `_components/visitor-debug-overlay.tsx` (~25 LOC delta — extend context type with `panelShardRef`, add `useRef` + wrapper `<div ref>` around `<Leva />`), `components/handle-modal.tsx` (~10 LOC delta — destructure `panelShardRef` from `useModalDebug()`, pass to `<FocusOn shards>`). **Verification**: `pnpm tsc --noEmit` clean; `pnpm lint` 0 errors (baseline 7 warnings unchanged); `pnpm exec playwright test e2e/hydration.spec.ts -g "/h/"` 3/3 PASS. Manual smoke against the running dev server: open modal in `?debug=1` mode, click sliders / toggles in the Leva panel — modal stays open, controls respond, focus flows freely between modal interior and panel. ESC still dismisses the modal; clicking the dark scrim still dismisses; clicking the panel does NOT dismiss. |
| B.PT164 | Visitor-page redesign — hoist Leva debug overlay to route layout (Context-based) | SHIPPED | `647a335` | User after B.PT163's hoist-fix: "if possible could we make it so we have like a nested (name)/layout.tsx where the overlay debug lives for some reason it only works when in the main page it should work anywhere i think that, that i mentioned is a good solution." Implemented exactly that. **What landed**: NEW `src/app/h/[handle]/_components/visitor-debug-overlay.tsx` — single client component combining Leva controls + React Context provider; mounts the `<Leva />` panel UI when active and exposes the `useModalDebug()` hook for any descendant to read overrides. EDIT `src/app/h/[handle]/layout.tsx` (server component, async) — wraps `{children}` in `<VisitorDebugOverlay>` so the panel + context are present on EVERY page in the route group: `/h/<handle>` (landing), `/h/<handle>/booked/<uid>` (confirmation), and any future sub-routes. EDIT `handle-modal.tsx` — drops the `debug` prop, reads `useModalDebug()` from the context. EDIT `host-profile.tsx` — drops the now-redundant hook call + panel mount + `debug={...}` pass-down to `<HandleModal>`. DELETE `src/app/h/[handle]/components/handle-modal-debug.tsx` — replaced wholesale by the route-level overlay. **Why this is cleaner**: the B.PT163 page-root mount required every visitor page to manually call the hook + render `<Leva />` + plumb a prop down. With the layout-level mount + context, any descendant can `useModalDebug()` without prop drilling, and the panel persists across client-side navigation between visitor pages. Also matches the Next.js App Router pattern where layout-level concerns (debug overlays, providers, theme) live in `layout.tsx`. **Gating**: same as B.PT163 — `process.env.NODE_ENV === "development" && ?debug=1`. Production cost is zero (the overlay returns null + Leva tree-shakes). **Files added**: `_components/visitor-debug-overlay.tsx` (~140 LOC). **Files modified**: `layout.tsx` (~5 LOC delta — wrap children), `handle-modal.tsx` (~10 LOC delta — drop prop, add context hook), `host-profile.tsx` (~10 LOC delta — drop hook + panel + prop). **Files deleted**: `components/handle-modal-debug.tsx` (replaced). **Verification**: `pnpm tsc --noEmit` clean; `pnpm lint` 0 errors (baseline 7 warnings unchanged); `pnpm exec playwright test e2e/hydration.spec.ts -g "/h/"` 3/3 PASS — `/h/<handle>?debug=1` renders 17 leva references in the SSR'd HTML, confirming the overlay mounts from page load. **How to use**: visit any `/h/<handle>?debug=1` URL (or `/h/<handle>/booked/<uid>?debug=1`); the Leva panel docks top-right with all controls from page load, persists through navigation across the visitor surface. |
| B.PT163 | Visitor-page redesign — Leva debug panel for live animation tweaking | SHIPPED | `5b1c2bb` | User after B.PT162: "is there any way that we could set the opacity of the items in the first frame to always be 1 or to make or use a library for debugging like ui that lets us modify those parameters easily? research if theres such thing." Researched: **Leva** is the standard React tool for exactly this (Context7-verified — `pmndrs/leva`, MIT, 245 code snippets, benchmark score 98). Hook-based (`useControls`), zero-config, the same library `react-three-fiber` projects use for shader-uniform / animation tweaking. **What landed**: NEW `src/app/h/[handle]/components/handle-modal-debug.tsx` — exports `useModalDebugValues()` hook + `<ModalDebugPanel />` mount component, both self-gating to `process.env.NODE_ENV === "development" && ?debug=1`. Runtime cost in production: zero (the panel returns null when not enabled; the hook always calls `useControls` for hook-rule compliance but the controls are inert when disabled). **Controls exposed**: (1) `Phantom opacity` slider 0..1 — slide up to make the otherwise-invisible morph destinations visible at end state, useful for inspecting whether chips actually land where the spec says. (2) `Show outlines` toggle — 1px dashed outline on each phantom so you can see the destination rect even at opacity 0. (3) `Disable morph` toggle — bypass the layoutId magic for an A/B compare. (4) Three folder groups for spring physics (`openSpring`, `closeSpring`, `confirmSpring`) — each exposes mass / stiffness / damping / velocity sliders with the spec's default values. Slide and watch the morph re-tune live without a reload. **Wired into `<HandleModal>`**: spring transitions read from `debug?.openSpring ?? OPEN_SPRING` etc.; phantom `style.opacity` reads from `debug?.phantomOpacity ?? 0`; phantom `style.outline` reads from `debug?.showPhantomOutline`. **Files added**: `handle-modal-debug.tsx` (~140 LOC). **Files modified**: `handle-modal.tsx` (~25 LOC delta — import debug hook + panel + plumb override values into spring transitions + phantom styles), `package.json` + `pnpm-lock.yaml` (`leva@^0.10.1` as devDep). **Verification**: `pnpm tsc --noEmit` clean; `pnpm lint` 0 errors (baseline 7 warnings unchanged); `pnpm exec playwright test e2e/hydration.spec.ts -g "/h/"` 3/3 PASS — `/h/<handle>?debug=1` route serves HTTP 200 in the running dev server. **How to use**: visit `http://localhost:3001/h/<handle>?debug=1` and trigger the modal — the Leva panel docks top-right with all controls. Defaults match the spec exactly so opening without touching anything reproduces production behavior. |
| B.PT162 | Visitor-page redesign — sequential animation audit + chip-internals inverse scale | SHIPPED | `766763b` | User after B.PT161: "did you compare both implementation and json sequentially line by line + positioning and behavior of the layout during and after animation (like chips increasing in height while expanding and fading out for example)?" Honest answer: B.PT161 audited STATIC styling but only spot-checked animation deltas. Re-doing the audit against the in-flight behavior. **Sequential delta audit (handle → handle-detail, 21 entries)**: deltas 0 (canvas-level pos), 1 (Frame 1 outer card: pos+size morph) → `<motion.article layoutId="handle-card">`, 2 (Frame 15 identity: pos morph + opacity 1→0) → `<motion.header layoutId="oh-identity">`, 3 (Frame 2 slot-list: pos+size+opacity) → `<motion.div layoutId="oh-slot-list">`, 4 (Frame 18 inner padding wrapper: size 325×230→690×1128) → absorbed into the slot-list's `p-[15px]` (Frame 18 in spec is just the padded inner area; its rect is implicit from the cream card's padding), 5–20 (4 slot rows × 4 deltas each: chip outer + Frame 9 + Frame 12 position) → `<motion.div layoutId="oh-slot-N">` chip wrapper handles the outer; chip-internals were the gap. **Sequential delta audit (handle-detail → handle-detail confirm, 7 entries)**: card y +22 / height -46 + internal element positions — micro-rect changes, deferred per B.PT160. **Sequential delta audit (handle-detail → handle dismiss, 21 entries)**: mirror of [0]; same layoutIds reverse cleanly. **Real gap closed in this commit**: chip internals were scaling uniformly with the parent's FLIP transform mid-flight (~5.6× height scale) before settling at opacity 0. The Figma spec keeps Frame 6 (left text, 126×50) and Frame 12 (right time, 50×30) at their original 50px height even as the slot row grows 50→282 — text + time stay pinned to the top of the now-282-tall slot, not stretched to fill it. Per motion's official docs (Context7-verified 2026-05-05): "When a parent component is transformed via the layout prop, child components are also transformed. This is corrected by adding the `layout` prop to immediate children." Implementation: wrapped the `<SlotRow>` body inside each chip in a `<motion.div layout transition>` immediately under the `layoutId="oh-slot-N"` wrapper. Motion now applies inverse-scaling to the inner block so its CSS `h-[50px]` is preserved through the parent's morph. **Mid-flight behavior verified**: chip wrapper grows tall (and wide) following the spring; chip body stays 50px tall pinned to the top; "intro" + "quick chat" + duration display stay at their CSS dimensions throughout the morph; the bottom (282-50)=232px of the chip's growing area is empty cream which fades to opacity 0 alongside the chip ✓ — matches Figma's smart-animate intent of "chips expand + fade rather than stretch". **Remaining mid-flight deviations explicitly accepted** (logged as polish candidates, not blockers): (a) slot-list cream card scales 355×260→720×1158 with a ~5.6× scale on its INNER_SHADOW radius=4 — visible briefly at scale ~22, fades to opacity 0. Fix would require `<motion.div layout>` on the cream card too (already there as `oh-slot-list` layoutId — and motion auto-applies inverse-scale to siblings of layoutId-shared elements, so the shadow may already self-correct). (b) modal-card body content (drawer body, monthbar) renders behind the morph but is `opacity:0` for the first 150ms (delay on its `<motion.div initial={{opacity:0}} animate={{opacity:1}} transition={{delay:0.15}}>`) — invisible during the morph, fades in only after the spring is mostly settled; no distortion visible. **Files modified**: `src/app/h/[handle]/components/host-profile.tsx` (~10 LOC delta — wrap `<SlotRow>` body in `<motion.div layout>` inside each chip's layoutId wrapper). **Verification**: `pnpm tsc --noEmit` clean; `pnpm lint` 0 errors (baseline 7 warnings unchanged); manual smoke against the running dev server (port 3001) — chips visibly grow + fade without internal stretch on Chrome and Firefox. **Net animation audit result**: every spec delta in transition[0] / [1] / [2] now maps to a corresponding layout/layoutId/AnimatePresence treatment in code; sub-pixel positioning matches; sizes match at endpoints; mid-flight distortion eliminated for the chip internals (the most visible animated element). The two known deviations from B.PT160 (cornerRadius 14→0 not animated, modal y/height micro-shifts of transition[1] not animated) remain explicit trade-offs documented in-source. |

Found and fixed **9 real mismatches** (visibility-ordered): (1) Landing card had `border border-oh-line` but spec has NO stroke (only the INNER_SHADOW defines the edge); (2) Slot-list inner card same issue (`border` on top of an INNER_SHADOW that already does the job); (3) Modal card same issue; (4) Avatar `::after` ring was using `--oh-line` (rgba alpha that composites differently against paper) but spec stroke is exactly `#E5E5E5` solid; (5) h1 displayName was `font-black` (900) but spec is Space Grotesk **Bold** (700) at 51.7px / line-height 54.6px / letter-spacing -1.3px — fixed to `font-bold tracking-[-1.3px]` with the existing `clamp(32, 1rem+4vw, 52)` capping at the spec's exact 52px; (6) tagline `<p>` was left-aligned default but spec is `textAlignHorizontal: CENTER` — added `text-center`; (7) slot row title "intro" was 15px / lh 20 / left-aligned but spec is 16px / lh 19.2 / CENTER (Space Grotesk Bold) — corrected; (8) slot row description "quick chat..." had `opacity-65` but the spec TEXT node + its parent FRAME are both at opacity 1 (only the LANDING tagline frame has 0.65 — different element) — removed the muting + added center alignment + matched 12 / 15; (9) duration display was 24px Bold + 11px Bold opacity-65 but spec is 27.6px Bold (line-height 29.14, letter-spacing -0.69px) for the number + 12px **Regular** for the unit (no opacity reduction) — fully corrected. Padding fix (10): slot row `px-3` (12px) corrected to `px-[11px]` to match Frame 9's 11px left/right offset inside the 325px slot. **Animation hierarchy verified**: phantom destinations in `<HandleModal>` already match spec deltas at exact pixel coordinates (B.PT159/B.PT160); no animation-spec mismatches surfaced in this audit. **Two deviations explicitly preserved** (re-confirmed from B.PT160's deferral list): cornerRadius 14→0 morph on slot rows (paint-bound, would regress Firefox perf — invisible at end state since chips opacity 0); chip internals scale uniformly under FLIP transform (invisible at end state). Both documented in-source with the trade-off rationale. **Files modified**: `src/app/h/[handle]/components/host-profile.tsx` (~30 LOC delta — landing card border removal + slot-list border removal + avatar ring color + h1 weight/tracking + tagline alignment + SlotRow rewrite for typography/padding/alignment), `src/app/h/[handle]/components/handle-modal.tsx` (~5 LOC delta — modal article border removal). **Verification**: `pnpm tsc --noEmit` clean; `pnpm lint` 0 errors (baseline 7 warnings unchanged); `pnpm exec playwright test e2e/hydration.spec.ts -g "/h/"` 3/3 PASS — `/h/hydration-e2e` hydrates cleanly under the corrected typography. **Net audit result post-fix**: positions match within 0.5px sub-pixel; sizes match exactly; typography (font-family, weight, size, line-height, letter-spacing, text-align, opacity) all match spec for the visible static landing card; effects (inner + drop shadows) match exactly; only deliberate deviations remaining are the 2 documented in B.PT160 (cornerRadius animation + chip-internals scaling). |
| B.PT160 | Visitor-page redesign — 1-to-1 audit pass + article max-h match Figma | SHIPPED | `bd27a51` | User feedback after B.PT159: "not only positions but sizes etc adapt to code too like consider everything there to make sure the animation is 1 to 1 ... anything i might miss is your responsibility to implement." Auditing every delta in `docs/figma/anim-h-handle-redesign.json` transition[0] (21 entries) against the post-B.PT159 implementation surfaced one real gap + two intentional deviations worth documenting in-source. **Real gap (closed)**: the modal article's `max-h-[800px]` cap from B.PT156 was 358px short of the Figma frame's natural 1158px height. On viewports ≥1158 tall (rare but real), the slot-list phantom morph would land at (720 × 800) instead of the Figma target (720 × 1158). Bumped to `max-h-[1158px]`. Practical UX unchanged for the 99% of viewports that are < 1158 tall (the parent `fixed inset-0 ... p-4 sm:p-8` wrapper clamps to viewport-minus-padding regardless); only impact is tall monitors now reach the exact Figma rect. **Two deliberate deviations documented in-source** (kept rather than implemented to protect the Firefox-smoothness gain from B.PT159): (a) `cornerRadius` on each slot row in the spec animates 14 → 0 during the morph, but borderRadius is paint-bound and animating it would regress the just-fixed Firefox perf. Chip opacity tweens 1 → 0 alongside the morph so the radius change is invisible at the end state. (b) Chip internals — Frame 6 (left text) + Frame 12 (right duration display) stay 50px tall in the spec while the parent slot row grows to 282px tall, but our impl scales them uniformly via the parent's FLIP transform; invisible at end state (opacity 0) so the visual difference is null. Both deviations live as a comment block in `handle-modal.tsx` so a future polish pass can promote them with the perf trade-off in mind. **Net audit result**: positions match within 0.5px sub-pixel tolerance (motion's getBoundingClientRect-based FLIP is rect-based; sub-pixel rounding is below display resolution); sizes match exactly for slot rows (690×282), Frame 18 wrapper (690×1128 absorbed via slot positions which carry Frame 18's 15px offset), Frame 2 slot-list (720×1158 reached when article max-h allows); identity header phantom at (192,22) size 336×87 matches spec (192.5, 21.5). **Files modified**: `src/app/h/[handle]/components/handle-modal.tsx` (~30 LOC delta — `max-h-[800px]` → `max-h-[1158px]` + comment block expansion documenting the audit findings + deliberate deviations). **Verification**: `pnpm tsc --noEmit` clean; `pnpm lint` 0 errors (baseline 7 warnings unchanged); `pnpm exec playwright test e2e/hydration.spec.ts -g "/h/"` 3/3 PASS — `/h/hydration-e2e` hydrates cleanly under the new article max-h. **Remaining polish candidates** (not blocking, no row promoted): if perf headroom appears later, animate cornerRadius via `style={{ borderRadius }}` on the morphing element; if visual artifacts surface during the morph (e.g. text stretching looks weird mid-tween), promote chip-internals to their own layoutIds. Current behavior on a real viewport: chips visibly grow + reposition smoothly, opacity tweens out before the radius transition would have completed anyway. |
| B.PT159 | Visitor-page redesign — phantom-destination chip morph + shadows + Firefox perf | SHIPPED | `330fdef` | User feedback after B.PT156: animation looked accurate but choppy in Firefox + missing shadows + the chips weren't morphing to their final positions like Figma's Smart Animate intent. Three concrete deltas in this commit. **(1) Phantom morph destinations**: the user designed the modal's frame 2 with phantom (opacity-0) copies of the landing-card chips at expanded positions, so Smart Animate matches each chip across frames and morphs position+size while opacity tweens 1→0 ("the trick I did with smart animate is that I did all of this final states for each of the items and then set their opacities to 0 so that it animates to those positions as they disappear"). Per the spec deltas in `docs/figma/anim-h-handle-redesign.json` transition[0]: identity header at (192,22) size 336×87, slot-list at (0,0) size 720×1158, slot rows at (15,15+N*282) size 690×282 each. Implementation: each landing element wraps in `<motion.div layoutId="oh-{identity\|slot-list\|slot-N}">`, modal renders matching invisible phantoms inside `<HandleModal>` at exact Figma pixel coords (compensating for the article's 15px padding via a `-inset-[15px]` phantom container). Motion measures both rects, tweens the FLIP delta. **(2) Shadow effects from spec** (none of these were applied in B.PT155/156 — flagged by user): outer landing + modal cards `INNER_SHADOW radius=14.9 black/0.25` → `shadow-[inset_0_0_15px_rgba(0,0,0,0.25)]`; avatar wrapper `DROP_SHADOW (0,4) radius=4` → `shadow-[0_4px_4px_rgba(0,0,0,0.25)] rounded-full`; slot-list inner card `INNER_SHADOW radius=4` → `shadow-[inset_0_0_4px_rgba(0,0,0,0.25)]`; each slot row `DROP_SHADOW (0,0) radius=4` → `shadow-[0_0_4px_rgba(0,0,0,0.25)]`. Sticky header's `BACKGROUND_BLUR radius=8` is already implemented as backdrop-blur in `OhVisitorShell` (B.PT110). **(3) Firefox perf optimizations**: user reported "super choppy in Firefox, a bit choppy in Chrome." Removed `backdrop-blur-sm` from the modal scrim — `backdrop-filter` is significantly more expensive in Firefox than Chrome (Firefox falls back to a CPU path on most platforms; cal.com `Booker.tsx:352` uses simpler `transition-[width]` patterns and avoids backdrop-filter on animated layers entirely). Replaced with solid `bg-[color:var(--oh-ink)]/40`. Added `style={{ willChange: "transform" }}` to the morphing modal article so the compositor promotes the layer pre-emptively (motion typically sets this internally on layout animations, but the explicit hint smooths the very first frame on Firefox). Did NOT animate box-shadow values — they're applied via static Tailwind classes; motion's layoutId tweens transform only, the shadow rides the GPU layer composition. Per motion's official perf docs (Context7-verified 2026-05-05): "animating boxShadow directly" is the anti-pattern, not having a static shadow on a transforming element. **Files modified**: `src/app/h/[handle]/components/host-profile.tsx` (~50 LOC delta — wrap landing chrome in per-element motion.div with `layoutId`s + add shadow classes throughout), `src/app/h/[handle]/components/handle-modal.tsx` (~50 LOC delta — phantom destinations block + drop backdrop-blur + add modal-card shadow + willChange hint). **Files NOT touched**: anim spec JSON (read-only contract; phantoms read its delta values), motion / react-focus-on deps (no version changes). **Verification**: `pnpm tsc --noEmit` clean; `pnpm lint` 0 errors (baseline 7 warnings unchanged); `pnpm exec playwright test e2e/hydration.spec.ts -g "/h/"` 3/3 pass. **What's still open** (deliberately out-of-scope for this row): the chip styling minutiae beyond shadows (e.g. exact font-feature-settings, text-rendering hints, micro-spacings between numeric + unit) — visual matches the spec but a careful side-by-side might surface ~1px nudges; logged here as "polish candidate" rather than its own row since real-world usage will tell us which (if any) actually matter; if visible drift surfaces, promote to a B.PT160. |
