| B.PT158 | figma-extract-anim: prototype + Smart Animate deltas, motion dep | SHIPPED | `5e35a39` | Visitor-page redesign needs 1-to-1 prototype animation parity (3 SMART_ANIMATE frame transitions with `GENTLE_SPRING` + 17 ON_HOVER state-swap variants). Pulling animation specs cleanly without re-inventing Figma's matching algorithm: the kiwi capture already includes `prototypeInteractions` per node (verified in `spec-h-handle-redesign.json`), but consuming code shouldn't have to re-walk that tree every render — extract once, commit a code-friendly JSON, implementation reads from the artifact. **What landed**: NEW `scripts/figma-extract-anim.mjs` — reads a spec produced by `pnpm figma:spec`, walks `prototypeInteractions` on every node, and for each action does: (1) classify as SMART_ANIMATE / INSTANT_TRANSITION / state-swap / unhandled; (2) for SMART_ANIMATE — match layers by RELATIVE name path between source-frame subtree and destination-frame subtree (root path is empty so frames named "handle" / "handle-detail" still match each other; children paths inherit from parent layer name, NOT root, so paths read like `Frame 1/Frame 15/Frame 16/safuentees`), then compute property deltas (position from `transform.m02/m12`, size, opacity, cornerRadius, fillPaints JSON-equality) and emit only changed ones; (3) decode SPRING easing as `{ mass, stiffness, damping, velocity }` while keeping the raw `easingFunction` array so consumers can swap interpretation if a future Figma release changes the order. **Output**: `docs/figma/anim-h-handle-redesign.json` — 3 transitions + 17 state swaps. Frame transitions: `handle → handle-detail` 650ms `[1, 247, 23.58, 0]` 21 deltas / 82 matches; `handle-detail → handle-detail` 562ms `[1, 330.6, 27.27, 0]` 7 deltas / 322 matches; `handle-detail → handle` 562ms `[1, 330.6, 27.27, 0]` 21 deltas / 82 matches. State swaps target component variants outside our spec (`324:7811` hover variant of `324:7813` button) — emitted with `notInSpec: true` so the implementer doesn't silently lose them. **Library pick**: added `motion@^12.38.0` as runtime dep alongside existing `gsap@^3.15.0`. Justification: GSAP's free tier has no native physics-spring (paid Business plugin only); Figma's `GENTLE_SPRING` maps directly to motion's `transition: { type: "spring", mass, stiffness, damping, velocity }` with same parameter semantics — verified via context7 against `motion.dev/docs/vue-transitions` (mass default 1, stiffness default 1, damping default 10; named-spring decode preserves Figma's intent without lossy curve approximation). Hybrid use: GSAP keeps owning timelines / FLIP / ScrollTrigger; motion owns spring-physics transitions specifically. Two libraries don't conflict — both are framework-agnostic underneath. **Files added**: `scripts/figma-extract-anim.mjs` (~250 LOC), `docs/figma/anim-h-handle-redesign.json` (~1500 LOC of generated output, committed for diff visibility per the skill's pattern). **Files modified**: `package.json` (1 npm script `figma:anim` + 1 dep `motion@^12.38.0`), `pnpm-lock.yaml` (motion + transitive deps). **Files NOT touched**: app source — pre-condition for shipping while a parallel agent owns app files. **What's still NOT done** (intentional): app-level wiring of motion/<motion.div> into the visitor route — that's a future B.PT (the actual visitor-page port). The pipeline is now self-contained — re-running `pnpm figma:anim h-handle-redesign` regenerates the JSON whenever the design changes, and any future Figma port (other features) can run the same two commands (`pnpm figma:spec <feature> --node-id=N:M` + `pnpm figma:anim <feature>`) to capture both visual + animation contracts. **Verification**: extractor ran cleanly against the existing spec; `pnpm tsc --noEmit` clean; `pnpm lint` 0 errors (baseline 7 warnings unchanged). |
| B.PT154 | figma-spec: follow INSTANCE.symbolID references in filtered spec | SHIPPED | `e8903e1` | Second real test of the figma:spec pipeline (against the 3 `handle` / `handle-detail` / `handle-detail` frames inside `desktop - dashboard` SECTION 324:15305 — the visitor-page redesign) revealed a second gap. The original filter logic walked the parent-pointer tree from the root node and collected descendants — but **didn't follow `INSTANCE.symbolID` references**. Component instances (the 19 INSTANCEs in our 3 frames) carry only bounding-box geometry (size, transform, fillPaints) and a pointer `symbolData.symbolID` to the SYMBOL node that holds the rendered content. Symbol definitions live as descendants of OTHER frames in the same Figma file (commonly a separate canvas/page acting as the file's component library). Result: the spec had `INSTANCE name="Component 3"` × 8 with no actual visual content, breaking 1-to-1 fidelity for any port. **Fix**: extend `scripts/figma-spec.mjs` step 3 with a fixed-point loop after the descendant walk: enumerate every INSTANCE in the collected set, look up each `symbolData.symbolID` in the full scenegraph, add the SYMBOL node + its subtree if not already collected, repeat until no new symbols surface (covers nested instances inside symbols). Variables (token references) are NOT followed — `TEXT.fontName` and `RECTANGLE.fillPaints` carry concrete values inline so the port can derive token mappings from those + the project's existing `--oh-*` tokens without needing a separate VARIABLE collection. **Verified**: re-ran filter against the same scenegraph (cached at `/tmp/figma_kiwi/scenegraph.json` so no recapture needed). Spec went from 757 → **770 nodes** (757 subtree + 13 from referenced SYMBOL subtrees). All 4 unique referenced symbols are now in the spec with full content: `324:7763` (16×16 icon, 4 VECTOR children), `324:7767` (16×16 icon, 2 VECTORs), `324:7813` (88×48 button, 1 TEXT "Text"), `358:19000` (668×89 input/list row, 1 FRAME "div#placeholder"). **Files modified**: `scripts/figma-spec.mjs` (~50 LOC delta — `collectReferencedSymbols(initialMap, allNodes)` helper after the descendant walk + log line on add count), `docs/figma/spec-untitled-324-15305.json` (regenerated, +13 nodes). **Why this never surfaced before**: B.PT127→B.PT131 (chrome-morph) used Path D (Figma MCP) which inlines instance children automatically; Path B (`pnpm figma:spec`) had its first real run today (B.PT153 fixed the zstd bug; this fixes the symbol-ref bug). Path B is now production-ready for instance-heavy designs. **Verification**: `pnpm tsc --noEmit` clean (no app types touched), `pnpm lint` 0 errors (baseline 7 warnings unchanged — Node script not lint-scanned). |
| B.PT153 | figma-decode: zstd-decompress data frames before kiwi-decode | SHIPPED | `b7f5aed` | First real test of the `pnpm figma:spec` pipeline (Path B in `.claude/skills/figma-to-code/SKILL.md`) on a user-supplied URL exposed a latent bug. The pipeline ran capture cleanly (Chrome CDP probe + WebSocket frames into `/tmp/figma_kiwi/frame_*.bin`), found the schema frame correctly (`frame_0000` with `fig-wire` magic + zstd-compressed kiwi schema, 580 type definitions), but then **decoded 0 pages from 17 captured frames** — every data frame got skipped as `Attempted to parse invalid message`. Hex-dump of the 1.4MB scenegraph payload (`frame_0003_recv_1433240b.bin`) showed it starts with raw zstd magic `28 b5 2f fd` — **the data frames are zstd-compressed**. The vendored `vendor/figma-kiwi/lib/kiwi.mjs` already exposes an `isZstdCompressed(buf)` helper for exactly this case, but `scripts/figma-decode.mjs` line 80 was passing every non-`fig-wire` RECV frame straight to `decodePage(buf, decoder)` which calls kiwi-decode without decompression. Kiwi sees zstd magic bytes as garbage type IDs and throws `invalid message`; the decoder's catch block silently skips the frame, so the pipeline appeared to "work" (exit 0 schema phase, exit 0 wrote-scenegraph phase) but produced an empty scenegraph that the spec-filter step then couldn't find any node in — the misleading error was `Node 324:15305 not found in captured scenegraph` when the real bug was 1MB+ of intact zstd-compressed scenegraph data getting silently dropped. **Fix**: import `isZstdCompressed` from `vendor/figma-kiwi/lib/kiwi.mjs` (already exported, just unused), and in the decode loop call `fzstdDecompress(u8)` before `decodePage()` whenever the frame starts with zstd magic. Schema frames stay untouched — they're framed with `fig-wire` and `extractCompressedSchema` already does its own decompression upstream. **Verification**: re-ran the same pipeline against the user's Figma file (root `324:15305` named "desktop - dashboard", 8035×1471 SECTION). Decoded 2 pages, **8839 total nodes** (1 DOCUMENT, 4 CANVAS, 3 VARIABLE_SET, 193 VARIABLE, 178 VECTOR, 115 SYMBOL, 1823 ROUNDED_RECTANGLE, 4549 FRAME, 1587 TEXT, 344 INSTANCE, 17 SECTION, 25 BRUSH); filtered to the target subtree and wrote `docs/figma/spec-untitled-324-15305.json` with **757 nodes** in the user's frame (404 FRAME / 231 ROUNDED_RECTANGLE / 102 TEXT / 19 INSTANCE / 1 SECTION). `pnpm tsc --noEmit` clean (no app types touched); `pnpm lint` 0 errors (baseline 7 warnings unchanged — Node script not lint-scanned). **Files modified**: `scripts/figma-decode.mjs` (~15 LOC delta — import addition, decode-loop branch on `isZstdCompressed`, comment block citing B.PT153 and the silent-skip mode). **Files added**: `docs/figma/spec-untitled-324-15305.json` (the captured spec for the user's Figma file — committed per the skill's "implementation reads from the spec, not from Figma; the git diff makes drift visible" pattern). **Why this never surfaced before**: B.PT132 vendored the figma-kiwi codebase with the read-path subset audited and the `isZstdCompressed` helper present; the original chrome-morph spec capture (B.PT127→B.PT131) used Path D (Figma MCP) not Path B, so the decoder bug never ran live until today. Path B is now production-ready for repeat porting work. |
| B.PT152 | Calendar-first bookings — drag-to-reschedule pre-flight (slot-collision + availability) | SHIPPED | `0682d80` | User report: dragging a chip to a new time produced `POST /api/trpc/bookings.reschedule?batch=1 404` in the network tab, with a paraphrased "this slot is already occupied" toast. **Root cause**: the `bookings.reschedule` procedure validates the new slot against `generateUpcomingSlots(host)` via string equality (`bookings.ts:1166-1168`); when the new slot ISO string isn't in that set the procedure throws `TRPCError({ code: "NOT_FOUND" })` which tRPC maps to HTTP 404. Two ways to land outside the set: (a) drop on a time that's outside the host's availability ranges (e.g., host availability `09:00-17:00` but the time-grid chrome shows `07:00-20:00` — drops at 7am or 8pm produce slots that aren't in the host's availability hours, so `generateUpcomingSlots` doesn't emit them); (b) drop on a slot already occupied by another booking — the `slotCollision` check inside the transaction at `bookings.ts:1200-1213` throws `TRPCError({ code: "CONFLICT" })` (HTTP 409, NOT 404), so the user's "already occupied" wording is ambiguous between the two failure modes. Both were surfacing as confusing 404s + a generic toast even though the procedure had specific reasons. **Fix scope**: client-side pre-flight check in `onDragEnd` BEFORE opening the ConfirmDialog, derived from data the page already prefetches. The procedure stays the source of truth — race conditions (someone else books in the gap between drop and confirm; host changes availability mid-drag) still fall through to the procedure's error and the mutation hook's toast. Pre-flight is a UX-quality layer, not a security boundary. **What landed**: NEW `src/app/(host)/bookings/components/calendar/drop-validation.ts` — pure-function `validateDrop({ newSlotStart, sourceRefId, events, ranges, now? })` that runs three checks in the same order the server's procedure does: (1) `in-past` — `newSlotStart <= now`; (2) `slot-occupied` — another event in `events` (excluding the source) whose `start.getTime()` matches; (3) `outside-availability` — host has no availability range covering the wall-clock minute on the slot's day-of-week (start inclusive / end exclusive, matching `generateUpcomingSlots`'s `t + stepMs <= rangeEndMs` semantics). Returns `{ ok: true }` or `{ ok: false, reason: "..." }`. NEW `__tests__/drop-validation.test.ts` — 7 cases pinning each rule (in-availability + no-collision passes; another-booking-collision rejects; source-booking-not-counted-as-collision; outside-hours rejects; weekend-day rejects; in-past rejects; range-end-exclusive 17:00 boundary). EDIT `bookings-list.tsx` — pull `schedule.get` from React Query cache (already prefetched by `/bookings/page.tsx` via B.PT42 so this hits the hydrated cache, no extra request); call `validateDrop` after the no-op short-circuit + before `setPending`; on `!ok`, fire a localized toast (`rescheduleSlotTaken` / `rescheduleOutsideAvailability` / `rescheduleSlotInPast`) and return without opening the dialog. **i18n**: 3 new keys per locale in `Bookings` namespace (en + es). **Files added**: `drop-validation.ts` (~95 LOC), `__tests__/drop-validation.test.ts` (~115 LOC). **Files modified**: `bookings-list.tsx` (~30 LOC delta — added schedule.get query, validateDrop call, reasonKey lookup), `messages/en.json` + `messages/es.json` (3 new keys each). **Files NOT touched**: `bookings.ts` router (no procedure change — pre-flight is purely client-side), `event-chip.tsx`, `time-grid-column.tsx`, view files, mutation hook (`useRescheduleBooking` continues to toast `error.message` on race-condition fallthrough). **Verification**: `pnpm tsc --noEmit` clean; `pnpm lint` 0 errors (baseline 7 warnings unchanged); `pnpm test:run` drop-validation 7/7 pass (~107ms); `pnpm exec playwright test e2e/a11y-bookings.spec.ts` 5/5 pass; `pnpm exec playwright test e2e/hydration-authed.spec.ts` 5/5 pass. **What's still open** (deliberately deferred): per-cell visual indicator during drag — color the column tint red when hovering over an occupied/outside-availability cell so the user gets feedback before releasing; today's UX is "drop → toast → re-aim". Procedure-level differentiation between "outside availability" and "slot occupied" — currently both surface as the same NOT_FOUND for a dropped-on-an-occupied-slot case (since the slot is filtered out of generateUpcomingSlots if booked); a richer error code split would let the toast say exactly which check failed without the client needing to re-derive. |
| B.PT151 | Calendar-first bookings — chip time-format wraps in narrow Week columns | SHIPPED | `84ffd6f` | User report: in Week view at narrow column widths the chip's time text "10:00 AM – 10:30 AM" was wrapping to a 2nd line while the visitor name truncated, making the chip occupy more vertical space than the absolute-positioned overlap layout accounts for; overlapping bookings stacked worse than they should and the visual broke. **Root cause**: the time `<span>` in `event-chip.tsx` had `font-mono text-[10px] leading-none opacity-55 tabular-nums` but **no `whitespace-nowrap`** — the internal space between "10:00" and "AM" let the browser wrap on a narrow body. Compounded by the format always emitting both period markers ("AM – ... AM" — 19+ chars) when one would suffice if both endpoints share a period. **Reference (cal.com)**: `apps/web/modules/calendars/weeklyview/components/event/Event.tsx:142-153` puts `whitespace-nowrap` on every time `<p>` across all three display variants (single-line / multi-line / full) and uses 24-hour `HH:mm` format which gives ~13 chars. We keep locale-aware 12-hour `toLocaleTimeString("en-US")` (Spanish locale already emits 24-hour from `toLocaleTimeString` so the same helper is a no-op there) but drop the redundant period marker when both endpoints share a period: "10:00 – 10:30 AM" (16 chars) instead of "10:00 AM – 10:30 AM" (19 chars). Crossing the noon boundary keeps both markers ("11:30 AM – 12:30 PM") so the visual hint is preserved exactly when it carries meaning. **What landed**: (a) Added `whitespace-nowrap` to the time `<span>` in all three display variants in `event-chip.tsx`. The single-line variant uses just `whitespace-nowrap` (the chip has `overflow-hidden` already; clipping is graceful when truly too narrow). The multi-line + full variants gain `truncate whitespace-nowrap` so a too-narrow chip still clips with ellipsis instead of wrapping. (b) Refactored `formatTimeRange` to detect a trailing `\s(am\|pm)$` suffix on each endpoint, drop it from the start string when both endpoints' periods match (case-insensitive), keep both when they differ, and pass-through unchanged when no period marker is present (24-hour locales). (c) Exported `formatTimeRange` from `event-chip.tsx` so the new vitest contract can pin its behavior. (d) NEW `__tests__/event-chip-format.test.ts` — 6 cases: both AM (drops first AM), both PM (drops first PM), AM→PM crossing (keeps both), late-PM range (drops first PM), exact 30-min slot inside AM (drops first AM), 12-noon boundary (12:00 – 12:30 PM, drops first PM). All 6 pass in 11ms. **Adjacent edges considered + handled**: (1) **24-hour locales** — `toLocaleTimeString` for Spanish defaults to "10:00 – 10:30" with no AM/PM marker; `formatTimeRange`'s suffix regex doesn't match → returns unchanged string. No breakage. (2) **Single-line variant** has `flex items-center gap-2` with `truncate` on the name — adding `whitespace-nowrap` to the time keeps it on one line; if the name + time + gap exceed the chip body, the name truncates first (per the existing `truncate` class). (3) **Multi-line / full variant** stacks name + time vertically; both now `truncate` so neither wraps. The full variant's optional subtitle keeps its existing `truncate` and remains the third stacked line. (4) **MonthDayCell chip** (`month-day-cell.tsx`) — doesn't render the time-range text (only status dot + visitor name + status), so no fix needed there. (5) **ConfirmDialog reschedule copy** in `bookings-list.tsx` uses `toLocaleString` for full date+time strings — those live in a wide dialog, not a narrow column, no wrap issue. **Files modified**: `event-chip.tsx` (~25 LOC delta — refactored `formatTimeRange` + exported it + added `whitespace-nowrap` to the 3 time spans + comment block explaining the wrap-failure mode + cal.com reference). **Files added**: `__tests__/event-chip-format.test.ts` (~70 LOC, 6 cases). **Files NOT touched**: `time-grid-column.tsx` / `day-view.tsx` / `week-view.tsx` / `month-view.tsx` / `month-day-cell.tsx` — fix is scoped to the chip primitive; calling code is untouched. **Verification**: `pnpm tsc --noEmit` clean; `pnpm lint` 0 errors (baseline 7 warnings unchanged); chip-format tests 6/6 pass; `pnpm exec playwright test e2e/a11y-bookings.spec.ts` 5/5 pass; `pnpm exec playwright test e2e/hydration-authed.spec.ts` 5/5 pass; `pnpm exec playwright test e2e/playground.spec.ts -g "calendar-views"` PASSES against new baseline (visual diff captures the shorter, non-wrapping time text — Day/Week chips now read e.g. "9:00 – 9:30 AM" on a single line, compared to "9:00 AM – 9:30 AM" before with potential wrap on narrow widths). |
| B.PT150 | Calendar-first bookings — drag-to-reschedule (@dnd-kit/core) | SHIPPED | `4ed6f5b` | Closes the deferred drag-to-reschedule item from B.PT147 (architectural prep — `eventToGridPosition` / `pixelToTime` / `snapPixelToGrid`). Original handoff said v1 read-only but architecture should "accommodate" drag — B.PT147 extracted the math; this wires the actual UX. **Library pick: `@dnd-kit/core` + `@dnd-kit/modifiers`**. Justification: (1) **KeyboardSensor built-in** (Tab → Space/Enter to lift → arrow keys to move → Space/Enter to drop → Escape to cancel) — directly maintains the WCAG 2.1.1 keyboard-accessibility bar shipped in B.PT149; alternative HTML5 D&D / `react-dnd` need hundreds of LOC of custom keyboard layer. (2) **PointerSensor with `activationConstraint: { distance: 8 }`** — drag only starts after 8px of movement, so plain clicks (and cmd-clicks → href, the B.PT142 cmd-click parity affordance) still reach the chip's onClick. (3) **Touch native** — works on mobile without backend swap (`react-dnd` needs `react-dnd-touch-backend`). (4) React 19 supported, modular ~25kb gzipped. (5) Custom collision detection cleanly fits our calendar-grid math. Compared `react-dnd` (HTML5 backend has poor mobile/keyboard support; touch backend separate dep), `react-aria` `useDrag` (heavier API, better fit for component-library integrations than custom timelines), `@hello-pangea/dnd` / `react-beautiful-dnd` (list-only, doesn't fit a 2D grid), HTML5 native (drag image is OS-controlled + can't style + mobile broken + keyboard impossible). **What landed**: NEW `src/app/(host)/bookings/components/calendar/draggable-event-chip.tsx` — wraps existing `<EventChip>` with `useDraggable` so the chip primitive stays focused on render + click semantics (composition over modification). EXISTING `src/lib/mutations/use-reschedule-booking.ts` reused as-is from A.PT9 (visitor reschedule UI). EDIT `src/app/(host)/bookings/components/calendar/time-grid-column.tsx` — wraps the column wrapper with `useDroppable`, exposes `TimeGridDropData` type with date + geometry payload, applies subtle `bg-oh-tint` while a chip is over the column. EDIT `src/app/(host)/bookings/components/bookings-list.tsx` — wraps calendar tree in single `<DndContext>` with PointerSensor + KeyboardSensor + `restrictToFirstScrollableAncestor` modifier; on drop, computes new slotStart via `snapPixelToGrid` (15-min step) → `pixelToTime` against the target column's date → opens `<ConfirmDialog>` carrying the (event, newSlotStart, durationMin) tuple → confirm calls `useRescheduleBooking.mutateAsync` with the existing `bookings.reschedule` publicProcedure (capability via publicUid; audit chain via RESCHEDULED_FROM/RESCHEDULED_TO operationId pair, same as visitor reschedule). EDIT `src/components/oh/confirm-dialog.tsx` — extended to support **controlled mode** (optional `open` + `onOpenChange` props) alongside the legacy trigger-based mode; required because the dialog opens programmatically from a drop event, not from a clickable trigger. The trigger-based callsites are unchanged. `<DragOverlay dropAnimation={null}>` renders the visual ghost following the pointer; the source chip's opacity goes to 0 during the drag so users only see one chip. **Same-day-only semantics**: implementation is structurally cross-day-capable (both columns in WeekView are in the same DndContext), but v1 ships same-day-only by convention — host workflows reschedule within-day far more often than across, and cross-day drops would need richer UX. Cross-day drag → punted to a future B.PT (no new deferral added since v1 rules out cross-day silently via the same procedure semantics — drop on a different column still works, just doesn't get special UX). **Cancelled bookings**: `disabled={event.status === "cancelled"}` on the draggable wrapper — cancelled rows can't be rescheduled (procedure 404s on soft-deleted rows), so disabling drag matches server semantics; chip stays clickable for detail view. **i18n**: 5 new keys added to `Bookings` namespace in `messages/en.json` + `messages/es.json` — `rescheduleConfirmTitle`, `rescheduleConfirmDescription` (with `{title}`, `{from}`, `{to}` ICU placeholders), `rescheduleConfirmLabel`, `rescheduleCancelLabel`, `rescheduleSuccess`. **Files added**: `draggable-event-chip.tsx` (~75 LOC). **Files modified**: `time-grid-column.tsx` (~40 LOC delta — useDroppable wiring, TimeGridDropData type, isOver tint), `bookings-list.tsx` (~110 LOC delta — DndContext, sensors, drag handlers, ConfirmDialog state, DragOverlay, parseColumnDate helper), `confirm-dialog.tsx` (~25 LOC delta — controlled-mode support), `messages/en.json` + `messages/es.json` (5 new keys each), `package.json` + `pnpm-lock.yaml` (`@dnd-kit/core@^6.3.1` + `@dnd-kit/modifiers@^9.0.0` deps). **Files NOT touched**: `event-chip.tsx` (unchanged — drag is composed around it), `day-view.tsx` / `week-view.tsx` / `month-view.tsx` (unchanged — they pass props through to TimeGridColumn which owns the drop zone), `bookings.ts` router (existing reschedule procedure reused as-is — same audit chain whether actor was visitor or host; if differentiation matters in the future, that's a procedure-level extension, not a v1 concern), MonthView's MonthDayCell chips (no drag in month view — chips are too small to drag meaningfully + month doesn't have a per-minute time grid to drop INTO; reschedule from month requires switching to Day/Week first — accepted UX trade-off). **Verification**: `pnpm tsc --noEmit` clean; `pnpm lint` 0 errors (baseline 7 warnings unchanged); `pnpm exec playwright test e2e/a11y-bookings.spec.ts` 5/5 PASS — drag wiring did NOT regress the a11y gate (axe still happy with the calendar surface, and the new `aria-roledescription="draggable"` doesn't introduce contrast violations); `pnpm exec playwright test e2e/hydration-authed.spec.ts` 5/5 PASS — DndContext + DragOverlay hydrate cleanly under React 19 + Next 16; `pnpm test:run src/lib/calendar-grid` 42/42 pass — geometry helpers I'm calling (pixelToTime, snapPixelToGrid) round-trip correctly. **Note on pre-existing failures**: 5 vitest failures in `workspaces-invitations.test.ts` + `webhooks.test.ts` predate this loop (last touched in B.PT8 / B1 / B.PT62b era), unrelated to this scope; user explicitly directed to leave them as-is. **What's still NOT done** (intentional): cross-day drag UX polish (works structurally; missing visual indicator for cross-day drop); E2E playwright drag spec (drag-and-drop in playwright is brittle and requires tedious setup — manual smoke + a11y gate cover the contract). |
| B.PT149 | Calendar-first bookings — Lighthouse-style a11y gate (axe-core) | SHIPPED | `3d7ee16` | Closes the deferred "Lighthouse formal a11y audit" item from B.PT145 + B.PT148. The original handoff cited "Lighthouse a11y ≥ 95" as the calendar surface's a11y goal; B.PT145's review-pass closed the obvious gaps (region labels, aria-current, +N MORE wiring, status in chip aria-label) and B.PT146 closed WCAG 1.4.1 (color-blind a11y) — but no automated gate enforced the bar. Without a gate, regressions slip in silently the next time someone bumps an opacity token or wraps a scrollable region in a different primitive. **What landed**: NEW `e2e/a11y-bookings.spec.ts` — runs `@axe-core/playwright` (the engine Lighthouse uses for a11y) against `/bookings` in all four view modes (`?view=list|day|week|month`) with WCAG 2.0/2.1 Level A + AA tags. Scoped to the calendar surface (`[role="region"]` + `[aria-label="View mode"]`) — pre-existing project-wide chrome contrast issues (`OnboardingChecklist`, `BookingsTabBar`, `OhPageHeader`) are deferred to a project-wide audit (B.PT150 candidate). **Two real gaps closed during the audit** (axe found things the manual review missed): **(1) WCAG 2.1.1 scrollable-region-focusable** — DayView, WeekView, MonthView all use `overflow-y-auto` (and WeekView also `overflow-x-auto`) on their wrapper `div`, which makes them scrollable but NOT keyboard-focusable. Keyboard-only users couldn't scroll the calendar with arrow keys. Fixed by adding `tabIndex={0}` (gated on `isCapped` / `minBodyWidthPx > 0` so the playground / unbounded variants don't accidentally tab-trap when there's nothing to scroll). The role="region" + aria-label from B.PT145 now combine to give SR users "Day view for Mon May 4 — region — scrollable" when they tab in. **(2) WCAG 1.4.3 color-contrast** — the `BookingsViewSwitcher`'s segmented-control labels (DAY / WEEK / MONTH / LIST) failed contrast on the Sisal `oh-frame` strip behind `OhPageHeader`. Two compounding causes: (a) `oh-eyebrow` was defined unlayered in `globals.css` BELOW the tailwindcss import — its `opacity: 0.55` declaration beat utility classes like `opacity-100` / `opacity-65` in the cascade, so EVERY callsite that wrote `oh-eyebrow ... opacity-N` project-wide silently rendered at 0.55 instead of the intended N. The active view-switcher trigger (`opacity-100` override) was rendering at ~0.55 → 4.17:1 contrast vs WCAG AA's 4.5:1 floor for 10px bold body text. (b) shadcn's `TabsTrigger` primitive bakes in `text-foreground/60` — a *color* alpha of 0.6, which compounds with element-level `opacity-N` (0.6 × 0.7 = 0.42 effective alpha → ~2.79:1). Two fixes: **(a)** wrapped `oh-legend` / `oh-description` / `oh-eyebrow` in `@layer components` so utility classes (in `@layer utilities`) override correctly. The composition pattern documented in `.claude/rules/oh-ui.md` *Typography utilities* now actually works — every project-wide callsite that wrote e.g. `oh-eyebrow opacity-100` for an active state finally renders at the brightness it always asked for. **(b)** view-switcher triggers now compose `oh-eyebrow text-foreground` (full color alpha) + `opacity-70` (inactive) / `opacity-100` (active) — single source of muting is the element opacity. Inactive at α=0.7 on Sisal → ~6:1 contrast (passes); active at α=1.0 → ~12:1. **Visual diff project-wide**: the @layer-components change reveals intended brightness at every `oh-eyebrow` callsite that wrote an `opacity-N` override. Spot check via grep: `host-profile.tsx` writes `oh-eyebrow opacity-100` for date eyebrows + `nextAvailable` label (now render at full opacity, was 0.55 — closer to design intent); `(host)/error.tsx` writes `oh-eyebrow tabular-nums opacity-65` for the error code (now 0.65, was 0.55 — slightly stronger); `webhooks-fields.tsx` + `api-keys-fields.tsx` write `oh-eyebrow opacity-45` for truncated metadata (now 0.45, was 0.55 — slightly lighter as intended). All three deltas move TOWARD the explicit intent the developer wrote. **Files modified**: `e2e/a11y-bookings.spec.ts` (NEW, ~85 LOC); `playwright.config.ts` (1 LOC — extend `authed` testMatch); `src/app/globals.css` (~30 LOC delta — wrap typography utilities in @layer components + add B.PT149 explainer comment); `src/app/(host)/bookings/components/bookings-view-switcher.tsx` (~5 LOC delta — `text-foreground` override + opacity-70 inactive); `src/app/(host)/bookings/components/calendar/day-view.tsx`, `week-view.tsx`, `month-view.tsx` (each ~3 LOC delta — `tabIndex={isCapped ? 0 : undefined}` on wrapper); `package.json` + `pnpm-lock.yaml` (`@axe-core/playwright@4.11.3` devDep). **Files NOT touched**: existing route-level a11y (B.PT145 scope), color tokens, structural chrome — fix is scoped to the gate + the two specific axe findings. **Verification**: `pnpm exec playwright test e2e/a11y-bookings.spec.ts` → 5/5 pass (setup + 4 view modes); `pnpm tsc --noEmit` clean; `pnpm lint` 0 errors (baseline 7 warnings unchanged); `pnpm exec playwright test e2e/hydration-authed.spec.ts -g "/bookings"` PASSES — confirms the @layer wrap + tabIndex additions don't break SSR hydration. **Note on `pnpm test:run`**: 5 vitest tests fail in `workspaces-invitations.test.ts` + `webhooks.test.ts`, all pre-existing on this branch (last touched in B.PT8/B1/B.PT62b commits, not by anything in this scope) — none of B.PT149's files touch the workspace or webhook routers. Surfacing as a separate concern. **What's still open** (post-B.PT149): project-wide a11y audit including chrome surfaces (`BookingsTabBar` / `OhPageHeader` / `OnboardingChecklist`) — they currently fail contrast at the `oh-eyebrow` opacity-55 baseline against various backgrounds; the @layer fix here makes overrides finally work but doesn't FIX the baseline (a candidate B.PT150 — would require either a token redesign or a per-callsite color override audit). |
| B.PT148 | Calendar-first bookings — horizontal day-strip for mobile week view | SHIPPED | `3c04f31` | Closes the mobile-day-strip option from B.PT144 (option D — was deferred as "build a custom mobile day-strip component"). After B.PT144 the mobile week view fell back to single DayView; navigation between days required the prev/today/next cursor controls (one day at a time). The mobile day-strip gives the user a 7-day visual context — see Mon-Sun of the cursor's week and tap any to navigate. **Reference**: cal.com doesn't ship a separate day-strip component (their COLUMN_VIEW takes a different multi-column-grid shape that doesn't fit a 400px viewport). Pattern lifted from Google Calendar / Apple Calendar mobile (canonical "horizontal day picker + day grid below"). **Component**: NEW `src/app/(host)/bookings/components/calendar/day-strip.tsx`. Renders 7 day buttons (Mon-Sun of cursor's week) with: weekday short label (`oh-eyebrow`), date number, optional today-dot below the number when today is NOT the cursor day. Currently-selected day gets the inverse-circle treatment (matches DayView's today-circle vocabulary so the "selected" / "today" semantics carry across views). Tapping any button calls `onDateChange(d)` which the parent uses to push `?date=YYYY-MM-DD`. Horizontal scroll on overflow (~7 buttons × 52px = 364px → fits in 400px viewport without scroll, scrolls below that). `[scrollbar-width:none] [&::-webkit-scrollbar]:hidden` removes the visual scrollbar (the buttons themselves communicate scroll affordance). **A11y**: `role="tablist" aria-label="Day picker"` on the strip, `role="tab" aria-selected aria-label="Switch to Mon, May 4"` on each button, `aria-current="date"` on the today date number when applicable. **`bookings-list.tsx` wiring**: mobile week branch (the `md:hidden` block from B.PT144) now renders `<DayStrip>` above `<DayView>`. The cursor-controls above (which step by week on week mode) are NOT removed — strip handles within-week jumping; cursor-controls handle prev/next-WEEK navigation. Two-tier navigation: cursor-controls jump weeks, strip jumps days. **Files added**: `day-strip.tsx` (~110 LOC). **Files modified**: `bookings-list.tsx` (~12 LOC delta — import + wrap mobile DayView in a flex column with DayStrip above). **Files NOT touched**: cursor-controls (unchanged — still steps by view-mode's natural unit), other views, detail modal. **Verification**: `pnpm tsc --noEmit` clean; `pnpm lint` 0 errors (baseline 7 warnings); `pnpm exec playwright test e2e/hydration-authed.spec.ts -g "/bookings"` PASSES — confirms the new component hydrates cleanly. Visual regression baseline (calendar-views playground) shows DayView only on desktop (the playground is desktop-rendered Playwright at 1280×720) so the new mobile-only component doesn't appear in that baseline; would need a mobile-viewport playwright spec to capture it (deferred — same pattern would benefit Day/Week/Month all). **What's still open**: container-query chip width adaptation (refinement); Lighthouse formal a11y audit (needs real-browser run); drag-to-reschedule wiring (architectural prep done in B.PT147, but actual drag is out of v1 scope per the original handoff). |
| B.PT147 | Calendar-first bookings — extract event geometry math (drag-prep) | SHIPPED | `b2c83ff` | Architectural prep for §11 q4 (drag-to-reschedule). The original handoff said v1 is read-only but the architecture should "accommodate" drag (e.g. position blocks via CSS variables that a drag handler can mutate). Current state was: position math (top + height + clipping) inline in `time-grid-column.tsx`'s render loop. To wire a drag handler later, a candidate ghost event needs its top + height computed the same way, and the cursor's Y pixel needs to reverse-map to a slot time. **What landed**: extracted both directions to a pure-functions module with vitest coverage so the contract is first-class. (a) `eventToGridPosition(event, opts)` returns `{ isVisible, top, height, clippedTop, clippedBottom }` — same math the inline version did, plus explicit clipping flags drag handlers can use to decide "anchor at the boundary or the original time." (b) `pixelToTime(yPx, date, opts)` is the inverse — gives the wall-clock time at a given Y pixel for the column's date. Drag flow then becomes: cursor moves → `pixelToTime(cursorY)` → build candidate `{ start, end }` → `eventToGridPosition(candidate)` → render preview at the new top/height. (c) `snapPixelToGrid(yPx, opts)` snaps to 15-minute (default) increments — drag UX should jump in human-comfortable steps, not pixel-perfect. **`time-grid-column.tsx` refactor**: the inline math (~25 LOC) collapses to a single `eventToGridPosition()` call. No behavior change (visual regression baseline unchanged), just clean separation. **Test coverage**: 17 vitest cases in `__tests__/event-geometry.test.ts` — basic positioning at hour boundaries / minute precision, minHeightPx floor for short events, isVisible=false for entirely-out-of-window events, partial clipping (top + bottom), custom oneMinuteHeightPx scaling, pixelToTime round-trip with eventToGridPosition (preserve start time), snapPixelToGrid step behavior including the Math.round(0.5)→1 quirk. All pass in 122ms. **What's still NOT done** (intentional — v1 read-only): no drag handler, no mutation hook for reschedule, no rollback-on-failure UI. The architectural foundation is now in place; wiring the actual drag would need: (1) a drag library pick (HTML5 drag-and-drop, `react-dnd`, `react-aria` useDrag, or `@dnd-kit/core` — `@dnd-kit/core` is the modern recommendation per its accessibility-first design and zero-dep React 19 support); (2) a custom mutation hook in `src/lib/mutations/use-reschedule-booking.ts` following the project's `create-mutation-hook` skill; (3) optimistic update via React Query's `onMutate` with rollback in `onError`; (4) the existing `bookings.create` rate-limit middleware extended to cover `bookings.reschedule` (which doesn't exist yet — would need a new procedure). **Files added**: `src/lib/calendar-grid/event-geometry.ts` (~110 LOC), `src/lib/calendar-grid/__tests__/event-geometry.test.ts` (~150 LOC). **Files modified**: `time-grid-column.tsx` (~30 LOC delta — replace inline math with helper call). **Files NOT touched**: chip components, view components, BookingsList — none need changes since geometry was scoped to time-grid-column. **Verification**: `pnpm tsc --noEmit` clean; `pnpm lint` 0 errors (baseline 7 warnings); `pnpm test:run src/lib/calendar-grid` 42/42 pass (14 overlap + 11 month-grid + 17 event-geometry); `pnpm exec playwright test e2e/playground.spec.ts -g "calendar-views"` passes against the existing baseline (refactor preserves visual output); `pnpm exec playwright test e2e/hydration-authed.spec.ts -g "/bookings"` PASSES. |
| B.PT146 | Calendar-first bookings — non-color status indicators (color-blind a11y) | SHIPPED | `de97700` | Closes the deferred a11y item from B.PT145(g): color was the SOLE differentiator for confirmed-vs-tentative, which fails WCAG 1.4.1 ("Color cannot be the only visual means of conveying information"). Cancelled was already non-color (line-through + dashed border + opacity-65 from B.PT136). Confirmed and tentative both rendered as a 3px-wide left bar / 6px solid dot, distinguished only by the green-vs-amber fill. **Fix**: tentative status now uses a NON-COLOR shape distinction in addition to the color: (a) `EventChip` left bar — confirmed = solid fill, tentative = striped fill via `repeating-linear-gradient(to bottom, var(--oh-status-tentative) 0 4px, transparent 4px 8px)` (~4px-tall stripes alternating color + transparent). Reads as a "dashed bar" without using `border-style: dashed` (which doesn't apply to a `<span>` background). Cancelled gets the existing solid bar + the chip body's line-through. (b) `MonthDayCell` chip status dot — confirmed = solid filled circle, tentative = ring (transparent center + 1px colored border), cancelled = solid (chip body has line-through). Sighted color-blind users distinguish confirmed-from-tentative via fill-vs-stripes / fill-vs-ring SHAPE rather than green-vs-amber color. Screen-reader users were already covered (chip aria-label includes status text from B.PT145). **What this closes**: WCAG 1.4.1 Use of Color (Level A) — full compliance for the calendar's three statuses. **What's still open**: cancelled-vs-confirmed in calendar views — both have solid bars; cancelled relies on chip-body cues (line-through + dashed border) which DO meet WCAG 1.4.1 since they're non-color. The dot in MonthDayCell for cancelled is solid (red), which IS color-only at the dot level; the chip body's line-through carries the non-color cue. Acceptable per WCAG since "the information... is conveyed by other visual means" — the line-through is sufficient. **Files modified**: `event-chip.tsx` (~10 LOC delta — switch bar fill to conditional `repeating-linear-gradient` for tentative); `month-day-cell.tsx` (~12 LOC delta — switch dot fill to ring border for tentative). **Files NOT touched**: `time-grid-column.tsx`, `cursor-controls.tsx`, view files — the chip primitives carry the indicator, no view-level changes needed. **Verification**: `pnpm tsc --noEmit` clean; `pnpm lint` 0 errors (baseline 7 warnings); `pnpm exec playwright test e2e/playground.spec.ts -g "calendar-views"` passes against new baseline (visual diff captures the striped-bar tentative); `pnpm exec playwright test e2e/hydration-authed.spec.ts -g "/bookings"` PASSES — confirms the new conditional inline-style hydrates cleanly. **Visual verification**: playground baseline shows Marcus' chip (the tentative booking) at 9am Monday with a visibly striped left bar where Owen + Greta (confirmed) have solid bars. |
| B.PT145 | Calendar-first bookings — a11y audit + wire +N MORE overflow | SHIPPED | `694a019` | A11y audit on the calendar surface (per the original handoff's "Lighthouse a11y ≥ 95" goal) — code review of every interactive element + ARIA / role / label / focus / current-state pass. **Two real gaps found**: (1) `MonthDayCell`'s "+N MORE" button had `onOverflowClick` defined as a callback but `BookingsList` never passed it — clicking +N MORE on a busy day did **literally nothing**. (2) The MonthDayCell chip's `aria-label` was `${title} at ${time}` while EventChip's said `${title} — ${time} — ${status}`. Inconsistency for screen-reader users — month chips wouldn't announce status (confirmed/cancelled/tentative), week chips would. **Fixes**: (a) Wired `onOverflowClick` → `router.push("?view=day&date=YYYY-MM-DD")` on click; +N MORE button now jumps to Day view for that date. The button also accepts `getOverflowHref` and renders as `<a href>` when provided so cmd-click opens the day view in a new tab (cmd-click parity from B.PT142 extended to overflow). The deliberate trade-off: project doesn't ship a popover primitive (no `<Popover>` in `src/components/ui/`), and building one for v1 is out of scope; jumping to Day view uses existing routing + the existing DayView component. Future enhancement = inline popover. (b) MonthDayCell chip aria-label now matches EventChip: `${title} at ${time} — ${status}`. **A11y polish** (B.PT145 also): (c) Added `aria-current="date"` to the today indicator span in DayView, WeekView (week column header), and MonthDayCell. Screen readers now announce "current, May 4" when scanning the date corners — sighted users had the inverse-circle visual cue, SR users had nothing equivalent. (d) Added `role="region"` + `aria-label="Day view for Mon May 4"` (etc. with locale-formatted date label) on each of DayView, WeekView, MonthView's outer container. Gives SR users context when they tab into the calendar grid. (e) Added `aria-label="Show all N bookings on Mon May 4"` to the +N MORE overflow link so screen readers announce what the click does + how many bookings will be shown. (f) Added `data-status` attribute to MonthDayCell chips matching the EventChip convention, useful for E2E selectors + future styling hooks. **A11y review findings deferred** (not blockers, sized B.PT146+): (g) Status colors as the only **visual** indicator for sighted color-blind users — current 3 colors (green/amber/red) plus the `line-through` for cancelled give cancelled-vs-not as a non-color cue, but confirmed-vs-tentative IS color-only on the chip's left bar. Adding a status icon shape (●/○/×) would close this gap. Defer because real-world usage hasn't surfaced the issue yet. (h) Cursor-controls' Today button has aria-disabled handling (via `disabled` prop) — the aria-label "Jump to today" is a bit awkward when on today; could swap to "Currently on today" via aria-pressed. Nice to have. (i) Lighthouse run against the auth'd `/bookings` route would catch any color-contrast issues with `oh-eyebrow` opacity-55 on paper bg — the project's `--oh-content-muted` is 55% ink which is borderline AA at 10px (4.5:1 needed; 55% × pure ink gives ~4.4:1 against paper). The project ships these tokens app-wide so a regression here is a project-wide concern, not a calendar concern; tracking as a future audit. **Files modified**: `bookings-list.tsx` (~12 LOC delta — onOverflowClick / getOverflowHref handlers + threading to MonthView); `month-day-cell.tsx` (~50 LOC delta — overflow link `<a>` pattern, aria-label status, aria-current, data-status); `month-view.tsx` (~10 LOC delta — region + label + threading); `day-view.tsx` (~10 LOC delta — region + label + aria-current); `week-view.tsx` (~20 LOC delta — region + label using existing memo'd `monday`/`days`, aria-current). **Files NOT touched**: `event-chip.tsx` (already had aria-label with status), `cursor-controls.tsx` (already had aria-labels), `bookings-view-switcher.tsx` (Tabs primitive provides a11y for free), `time-grid-column.tsx` (decorative gridlines already aria-hidden). **Verification**: `pnpm tsc --noEmit` clean; `pnpm lint` 0 errors (baseline 7 warnings); `pnpm exec playwright test e2e/playground.spec.ts -g "calendar-views"` passes against new baseline; `pnpm exec playwright test e2e/hydration-authed.spec.ts -g "/bookings"` PASSES — confirms region+aria-label+aria-current additions don't break SSR hydration. |
| B.PT144 | Calendar-first bookings — mobile fallback views (week→day, month→list) | SHIPPED | `f19fd15` | Resolves §11 q1 (mobile defaults — open since the original handoff). After B.PT143 bumped Week's `min-w` to 1100px and the per-view wrapper widened to 1440px, the desktop UX got noticeably better but the mobile UX got noticeably worse: a 7-column week on a 400px phone shows ~36% of the week with hard horizontal scroll, chips render at ~50-60px wide. **Considered options (the design branch §11 q1 left open)**: (A) URL is source of truth on all viewports — let mobile users see week + month with horizontal scroll; (B) auto-fall-back to LIST on mobile for week + month — uses existing list view, simplest; (C) auto-fall-back to DAY for week + LIST for month — keeps "calendar feel" where the single column works, list where it doesn't; (D) build a custom horizontal day-strip mobile component (cal.com COLUMN_VIEW pattern) — significant work, defer. **Chose C** (week → day on mobile, month → list on mobile) because: (1) the prototype's design intent in the original handoff explicitly says "below 700px… week view substitutes a horizontal day strip" — DAY is the closest viable mapping without building a bespoke strip; (2) MONTH grid cells go to ~57px on a 400px viewport with chips that can't fit and "+N MORE" overflow dominating every cell — list is the better scan-and-tap surface; (3) DAY view already works at any width (single column); (4) zero new components needed. **Implementation**: per `dashboard-forms.md`'s SSR-safe rule ("Breakpoint-dependent primitive swaps must keep both branches rendered so the SSR → mounted re-render is invisible"), I used **CSS-only swap via Tailwind's `md:` breakpoint** instead of `useMediaQuery + useMounted`. Both branches render in the DOM; one is hidden via `hidden md:block` (desktop-only) or `md:hidden` (mobile-only). No JS swap, no hydration flash, no `useMounted` gating. The cost is doubled render work for week + month branches when on either viewport, but our data set is small (≤200 bookings on a typical host) and the calendar views are pure-presentational, so render is cheap. Threshold = 768px (project convention from `responsive-modal.tsx`'s `MOBILE_QUERY = "(max-width: 767px)"` + `useIsMobile`'s `MOBILE_BREAKPOINT = 768`); aligns with Tailwind's `md:` prefix. **URL preserved**: when mobile renders the day fallback, the URL still says `?view=week`. Resizing back to desktop renders Week again. The view-switcher (BookingsViewSwitcher) is still visible on mobile so users can opt out into LIST themselves. **Month-mobile tab handling**: when `?view=month` on mobile, the tabs render with an onValueChange that pushes `?view=list&tab=NEW` — picking a tab takes the user out of month-mode entirely (no more month-grid at any viewport width). Reads as "month is desktop-only; tab clicks reset to canonical list." **Files modified**: `bookings-list.tsx` only (~70 LOC delta — Week and Month branches each split into desktop+mobile pairs with `hidden md:block` / `md:hidden`). **Files NOT touched**: page.tsx, calendar views, cursor-controls, view-switcher, detail modal — all compose without API changes. **Verification**: `pnpm tsc --noEmit` clean; `pnpm lint` 0 errors (baseline 7 warnings); `pnpm exec playwright test e2e/hydration-authed.spec.ts -g "/bookings"` PASSES — confirms both branches hydrate cleanly without mismatch under React 19 + Next 16 (the CSS-swap pattern is hydration-safe by construction since both branches are present in SSR HTML). **What's still open**: §11 q4 drag-to-reschedule (v1 read-only); Lighthouse a11y ≥ 95 audit; container-query chip adaptation (current min-width approach is sufficient for v1); a true mobile day-strip component (option D) — would replace the current Day-as-week-fallback if real-world usage shows users want week-context on mobile. |
| B.PT143 | Calendar-first bookings — per-view max widths + dvh-relative max-height (use the screen we have) | SHIPPED | `1afd01e` | User feedback after B.PT142: "we don't make use of the whole vertical space with such a big screen we have a min height and min width too… check whether we should take more advantage of the space that we are not using or not by looking for example in the public link form in cal.com they use a month and week view see how they use them and if any of the concepts there also apply here." **Cal.com booker reference** (`apps/web/modules/bookings/components/Booker.tsx` + `packages/features/bookings/Booker/config.ts`): the cal.com VISITOR booker commits hard to the layout shape — `BookerLayouts.WEEK_VIEW` sets `width: "100vw"` and `minHeight: "100vh"` (escapes the page shell entirely, fills the viewport); `BookerLayouts.MONTH_VIEW` is a compact card at `minHeight: 450px`; `BookerLayouts.COLUMN_VIEW` also goes 100vw/100vh. The implication: **per-density max widths**, not a single one-size-fits-all shell. **Our problem**: `OhPageShell` caps content at 760px (or 1024px on `wide`); calendar's previous `min-w: 980px` collided → forced horizontal scroll always; `max-h: 640px` ignored viewport height (a 1080p screen has ~800px+ available for the calendar after chrome — we were using 80% of that). **What landed**: (a) Restructured `bookings-list.tsx` into header-chrome + per-mode content. The OhPageHeader + onboarding card + view switcher stay in the standard 760px shell so they line up with the rest of the dashboard's narrow column. The list mode also stays in 760px (list rows don't benefit from a wider column). The CALENDAR mode escapes the shell into a per-view max-width wrapper: Day = 760px (single column; sprawling at 1440 would look empty), Week = 1440px (7 cols × ~190px each at 1440 viewport — comfortable readability), Month = 1200px (7 cols × ~165px each — chip lists fit). Matches cal.com's per-layout commitment without going full-bleed (we have a sidebar + topbar to coexist with). (b) Replaced fixed `maxBodyHeightPx: number = 640` with `maxBodyHeight: string = "calc(100dvh - 280px)"` on all three views. The 280px is the chrome + page-header offset (top bar 32px + page title block ~120px + view-switcher row ~60px + breathing ~70px). On a 1080p display, calendar gets ~800px tall body — fills the available space. On a 720p laptop, ~440px — still usable, scrolls. The CSS `dvh` unit (dynamic viewport height) handles mobile browser-bar collapse correctly. Playground passes `maxBodyHeight="none"` to opt out so the visual regression baseline captures full content. (c) Bumped Week's `minBodyWidthPx` from 980 → 1100. At 1100px the week body has 7 columns × (1100-56)/7 = ~149px each (vs the old 980's 132px). On viewports ≥ 1100 wide, the body fits without horizontal scroll; below that it scrolls horizontally inside the wrapper (cal.com's `width: 165%` pattern, parameterized). **What this fixes from user's complaint**: chip cramping on week view — chips now have ~149-190px to work with depending on viewport; readable at all sizes. Vertical waste — calendar uses ~800px of an ~830px content area on a 1080p screen instead of 640px. **What's NOT changed**: cursor-controls (already aligned to the per-view wrapper since they live inside the new flex column), the calendar internals (positioning math, overlap algorithm, chip components are unchanged), the detail modal (independent state). **Files modified**: `bookings-list.tsx` (~30 LOC delta — restructure + per-view width + cn import), `day-view.tsx` (~10 LOC — prop rename), `week-view.tsx` (~15 LOC — prop rename + min-width default bump + comment update), `month-view.tsx` (~10 LOC — prop rename + comment), `playground/components/calendar-views/page.tsx` (3 LOC — pass `maxBodyHeight="none"` to all three views). **Verification**: `pnpm tsc --noEmit` clean; `pnpm lint` 0 errors (baseline 7 warnings); `pnpm exec playwright test e2e/playground.spec.ts -g "calendar-views"` 1/1 pass against new baseline; `pnpm exec playwright test e2e/hydration-authed.spec.ts -g "/bookings"` PASSES — confirms the new restructure (two `<OhPageShell>` blocks + a custom calendar wrapper) hydrates cleanly under React 19 + Next 16. **What's still open**: §11 q1 mobile-default below 700px (becomes more relevant once these views go live — week's 1100px min-width on a 400px phone screen scrolls horizontally hard; the design call is "DAY mode default on mobile" or "vertical day-strip"); container-query-based chip-width adaptation (current min-width approach is sufficient for v1 but if real-world densities push beyond 4-way overlap the `@container` route is the next step). |
| B.PT142 | Calendar-first bookings — scrollable views + chip cmd-click parity + range clipping | SHIPPED | `4bdb906` | User flagged after the B.PT141 ship: (a) week-view chips were cramped to the point of unreadability when columns went sub-90px wide; (b) the calendar didn't scroll internally — the whole page scrolled instead, pushing chrome out of view; (c) "see other underlying issues" — I caught two more during research/implement. **Cal.com pattern as foundation** (`apps/web/modules/calendars/weeklyview/components/Calendar.tsx`, MIT): scroll-container + sticky day-headers (`sticky top-0 z-80`) + sticky hour-axis (`sticky left-0`) + `width: 165%` inner div forcing horizontal scroll on narrow viewports so chips never go below readable width. Their explicit "MobileNotSupported" bail at <sm is too aggressive for this project (we want responsive); we adapt the pattern with explicit `min-w-[980px]` + `overflow-x-auto`. **Schedule-X reference**: confirmed self-contained scroll inside the widget, sticky toolbar; same pattern. **What landed**: (1) Day view: wraps body in `rounded-(--oh-r-sm) border bg-paper`, adds `max-h: 640px + overflow-y-auto` (default; playground passes `maxBodyHeightPx={0}` to opt out). Day header is `sticky top-0 z-20` with paper bg so chips scrolling under don't bleed. (2) Week view: same chrome + vertical scroll. PLUS: `min-w: 980px` on the inner body forces horizontal overflow when wrapper is narrower (the chip-cramping fix — chips always render at column-width-of-980/7 = ~135px even on viewport narrower than 980); `sticky left-0 z-10 bg-paper` on the hour-axis container so it stays visible during horizontal scroll. The day-headers row's hour-axis spacer cell is also sticky-left so headers stay aligned. Two-axis sticky positioning (top + left) is canonical cal.com / Google Calendar pattern; uses `position: sticky` natively, no JS scroll listeners. (3) Month view: same paper-card chrome + `max-h: 640px + overflow-y-auto`. Weekday header sticky-top on scroll. Month is naturally short (5–6 rows) so the cap rarely triggers — present for vocabulary consistency across all three views. (4) **Cmd+click parity** (the underlying issue I caught): list rows from B.PT138 use `<Link href + onClick interception>` so plain click opens the modal but cmd/ctrl/shift/middle-click open the standalone `/bookings/<uid>` page in a new tab. Calendar chips were `<button>` only — no deep-link parity. **Fix**: `EventChip` and `MonthDayCell`'s chip both gain an optional `href` prop. When provided, render as `<a href>`; click handler intercepts plain left-click only (calls `onClick`); modifier-clicks fall through to the href. `BookingsList` passes `getEventHref={(e) => /bookings/${e.refId}}` to all three views. Same pattern across List, Day, Week, Month — one consistency vocabulary. (5) **Range clipping** (the second underlying issue): `TimeGridColumn` previously rendered events at their absolute time, which means a 5am booking with `startHour=7` would have negative `top: -120px` (visually clipped by container `overflow: hidden`, but the absolute height calc was still wrong); a 9pm booking with `endHour=20` (8pm) would extend below the column. **Fix**: clip event times to `[visibleStartMin, visibleEndMin]` window before computing `top` and `height`. Events entirely outside the window render `null` (skipped). Events partially outside render at the clipped boundary so the host sees "this booking starts before this view's visible range" naturally without a layout break. **Files modified**: `event-chip.tsx` (~25 LOC delta — Component picker + click handler with modifier-fallthrough); `month-day-cell.tsx` (~28 LOC delta — same pattern adapted to the chip-as-row variant); `time-grid-column.tsx` (~20 LOC delta — clipping math + getHref pass-through); `day-view.tsx` (~20 LOC delta — scroll wrapper + sticky header + getHref); `week-view.tsx` (~50 LOC delta — two-axis scroll + sticky header + sticky hour-axis + min-width body + getHref); `month-view.tsx` (~20 LOC delta — scroll wrapper + sticky weekday header + getHref); `bookings-list.tsx` (~5 LOC — `getEventHref` + threading to all three views); `playground/components/calendar-views/page.tsx` (3 LOC — `maxBodyHeightPx={0}` opt-out so the playground baseline still captures full content). **Files NOT modified**: `cursor-controls.tsx`, `hour-axis.tsx`, `bookings-view-switcher.tsx`, `booking-detail-modal.tsx`. **Verification**: `pnpm tsc --noEmit` clean; `pnpm lint` 0 errors (baseline 7 warnings); `pnpm exec playwright test e2e/playground.spec.ts` 14/14 pass against new baseline; `pnpm exec playwright test e2e/hydration-authed.spec.ts -g "/bookings"` PASSES — confirms the new sticky-position + overflow-auto chrome hydrates cleanly under React 19 + Next 16. **Open follow-ups still deferred**: §11 q1 mobile-default view selection (DAY vs LIST below 700px); §11 q4 drag-to-reschedule architecture; Lighthouse a11y ≥ 95 audit; chip width adaptation via container queries (current min-width approach is sufficient for v1, container-queries-based compact mode is a polish item if real-world densities push beyond 4-way overlap on a 980px-min wrapper). |

| B.PT138 | Calendar-first bookings — Phase 5: state-driven detail modal (replaces intercepted-route) | SHIPPED | `501f6a3` | Phase 5 of the calendar-first feature. The booking detail overlay was sitting half-done in the working tree across the last six commits — a parallel-track refactor that swapped the A.PT7 intercepted-route Sheet (`@modal/(.)bookings/[publicUid]/...`) for a state-driven `<BookingDetailModal>` opening a `ResponsiveModal` (Dialog desktop / vaul Drawer mobile). I deliberately avoided touching it in B.PT134-137 to dodge merge conflicts; this commit lands it. **Why state-driven beats intercepted-route**: (1) the transition is synchronous — no client-side router roundtrip on open/close, no flash. (2) prev/next chevrons + ←/→ keys swap the booking IN PLACE via `onNavigate` (modal stays open, just refetches the new uid via the same React Query cache) instead of `router.push("/bookings/<other>")`, which previously walked the URL twice and re-mounted Base UI's Dialog primitive. (3) drops the `[role="dialog"]` ESC-suppression hack — the prior intercepted-route layered ON TOP of the standalone page route, so both ESC handlers fired in parallel and `router.back()` raced `router.push("/bookings")`, popping history twice. With state-driven, the modal owns ESC entirely via the primitive. (4) drops the SSR prefetch duplication between the @modal slot and the standalone page. **What landed**: (a) `src/app/(host)/bookings/components/booking-detail-modal.tsx` (NEW) — thin wrapper composing `ResponsiveModal/Content/Title/Description` with `defaultClose`, controlled `open` state, and a desktop-only `max-h: calc(100vh - 2*var(--oh-modal-vinset))` so long detail panels scroll inside the modal. Title + Description are sr-only; BookingDetail renders its own visible heading. (b) `bookings-list.tsx` MODIFIED — adds `selectedUid` state, threads `onSelect` callback through `BookingsListPanel` → `BookingRow`. The row's `<Link>` intercepts plain left-click (`e.preventDefault()` + `onSelect(uid)`); cmd/ctrl/shift/middle clicks fall through to the standalone `/bookings/<uid>` route so deep-link-in-new-tab still works. Modal mounts at the root of `<BookingsList>`. (c) `booking-detail.tsx` MODIFIED — `variant: "drawer"` renamed to `variant: "modal"` throughout. New optional props `onNavigate?(uid: string) => void` and `onClose?() => void` for the modal flow. ESC handler simplified (no more `[role="dialog"]` workaround). NeighbourLink (the prev/next chevron component) gained an `onNavigate` pass-through that intercepts plain clicks the same way BookingRow does. Cancel mutation `onSuccess` calls `onClose` if provided, else falls through to `router.push("/bookings")` for the page variant. (d) `(host)/layout.tsx` MODIFIED — removes the `modal` prop from the layout signature and the `{modal}` render slot since the parallel `@modal` parallel-route is gone. Comment block updated to reference the state-driven approach + reasons. (e) THREE FILES DELETED — `(host)/@modal/(.)bookings/[publicUid]/booking-detail-drawer.tsx`, `(host)/@modal/(.)bookings/[publicUid]/page.tsx`, `(host)/@modal/default.tsx`. The whole intercepted-route subtree is removed; the standalone `/bookings/[publicUid]/page.tsx` remains as the deep-link / hard-refresh fallback. **Compatibility with B.PT134-137**: the `<BookingDetailModal>` accepts a uid string. The calendar views (Day/Week/Month from B.PT136-137) already track a `selectedRefId` prop that the playground gallery flips on chip click — production wiring in B.PT139 just feeds `selectedRefId` into the same `setSelectedUid` setter the list view already owns, so calendar chips and list rows share one modal instance. **A.PT7 supersession note**: A.PT7 (Sheet drawer for `/bookings/[publicUid]`) shipped 2025-old in commit `0ff29f1`; this commit supersedes the implementation but the row stays SHIPPED for audit history. **Verification**: `pnpm tsc --noEmit` clean; `pnpm lint` 0 errors (baseline 7 warnings); `pnpm test:run` 453 passed + 5 pre-existing failures (workspaces-invitations × 4, webhooks × 1) confirmed unrelated by `git stash` round-trip in B.PT131. Manual smoke deferred to B.PT139 (page wiring) where I'll boot dev and click through the full flow. |

Adaptations: replaced `dayjs` with native `Date.getTime()` (project doesn't use dayjs), typed `event.id` as string (project uses `publicUid`), zero dependencies. **Test coverage**: 14 cases in `__tests__/overlap.test.ts` covering empty input, separate groups, 2-way overlap, 3-way overlap, touching-but-not-overlapping (no group), group end extension across non-adjacent events, single-event width, n=2/3 cascade left-strictly-increasing + width-floor invariants, z-index ordering within group, groupIndex incrementing across groups, `createLayoutMap` lookup. All 14 pass in 102ms. **React components** (under `src/app/(host)/bookings/components/calendar/`): `event-chip.tsx` (status-colored chip — adapted from cal.com `Event.tsx`, restyled with `--oh-status-*` tokens via 3px-wide left color bar + paper-tinted body, single-line/multi-line/full display variants per duration <40m/<45m/≥45m, line-through + dashed border for cancelled bookings), `hour-axis.tsx` (left-side hour labels at 56px wide, `oh-eyebrow` typography mono caps 10px, anchored to top-of-hour), `time-grid-column.tsx` (single-day column renderer adapting cal.com's `EventList.tsx` shape — drops zustand for props, accepts `oneMinuteHeightPx` directly so Day and Week can use different scales, current-time line as paper-halo + ink-line + green dot), `day-view.tsx` (single-column wrapper with full-weekday header + today inverse-circle), `week-view.tsx` (7-column wrapper Mon–Sun with today-tinted column background and per-column current-time line guard). **Hook**: `src/lib/calendar-grid/use-current-minute.ts` — minute-tick hook scheduled to the wall-clock minute boundary so the current-time line moves smoothly without burning render cycles. **Bug caught + fixed during build**: initial wiring multiplied `oneMinuteHeightPx * 60` in DayView/WeekView when passing to children, making the column 50,400px tall (60×60×14 hours). Fixed by passing the prop value directly — the prop already represents pixels-per-minute (default 1 = 60px/hour matching cal.com's `--one-minute-height: 1px`). Caught by the visual regression baseline showing a 101,832px tall page. **Bug caught + fixed during build (#2)**: initial mock data used `new Date("2026-05-04T00:00:00Z")` which the headless Chrome shifted to May 3 evening in negative-offset zones, making the week view show April 27 → May 3 instead of May 4 → 10. Survey reassessed firsthand: Schedule-X 2283★ updated 2026-05-02; FullCalendar 20447★ updated 2026-05-04; both genuinely active, both have React 19 in peers, the practical differentiator remains theme-shadcn first-party. **Spike covered**: live calendar with all four views (Day/Week/Month grid/Month agenda), 6 mock bookings exercising n=3 overlap collision (Owen/Greta/Marcus at 9am Tuesday), three status-color calendars mapping `--oh-status-confirmed/tentative/cancelled` via `lightColors.main + container` config, theme-shadcn first-party CSS imported, `useNextCalendarApp` hook (Next.js-specific) with selectedDate as `Temporal.PlainDate`. **Verified working** (post-fix): Schedule-X v4.5+ rejected our string event dates with `"Event start time needs to be a Temporal.ZonedDateTime or Temporal.PlainDate"` — fix was constructing `Temporal.ZonedDateTime.from({...})` lazily inside the component (after the polyfill global import lands). After the fix, browser console clean, 6 events rendered at correct y-positions, n-way overlap split into three columns automatically per default behavior, status colors apply per `calendarId` mapping. **Visual caveats observed in spike** (not blockers, scheduled for B.PT136 wiring): (1) Hour-axis labels (7AM, 8AM, …) inherit theme-shadcn's `--foreground` which doesn't currently match our `--oh-ink`; (2) "today" date highlight uses theme-shadcn's primary accent (purple) not our ink; (3) calendar's `height: 640px` on the wrapper combined with full-page Playwright screenshot shows event chips bleeding visually below the calendar boundary because Schedule-X relies on container overflow. All three are CSS-variable bridging tasks for B.PT136, not foundational issues. **Files**: `src/app/(dev)/playground/components/calendar-spike/page.tsx` (NEW, ~250 LOC including caveats + proofs sections), `src/app/(dev)/playground/page.tsx` (one new entry in `COMPONENT_GALLERIES`), `e2e/playground.spec.ts` (one new entry in `PLAYGROUND_ROUTES`), `package.json` (4 new runtime deps: `@schedule-x/react`, `@schedule-x/calendar`, `@schedule-x/theme-shadcn`, `@schedule-x/events-service` + 1 dev dep `temporal-polyfill`), 1 new PNG baseline. **Verification**: tsc clean; lint 0 errors (baseline 7 warnings); playwright 13/13 pass against new baseline. **A/B/C surface (HARD STOP — wait for user before B.PT136)**: **A. Schedule-X primary** — RECOMMENDED. Spike confirms it loads under React 19 + Next 16 App Router via `useNextCalendarApp`, theme-shadcn picks up shadcn CSS variables (with the three bridging tasks in B.PT136), n-way overlap collision works built-in, native Temporal API timezones, all four views native, `customComponents` slots (timeGridEvent / monthGridEvent / weekGridDate / etc.) for our chip styling, premium drag-and-drop irrelevant for v1 (read-only). **B. FullCalendar primary** — fallback. 19k+ stars, most mature, free for our needs, robust DST handling. Catch: no first-party shadcn theme — we'd write the `--fc-*` → `--oh-*` variable bridge ourselves (~half-day of work) plus per-component overrides. Still tractable, just heavier than A. **C. Hand-roll over shadcn primitives** — re-implements overlap collision + Temporal timezone math that A/B already solved. Reinvents the wheel; only justified if A and B both fail visual fit in a future iteration. **Recommended: A.** Spike proves the foundation; the visual gaps are configuration, not architecture. If user approves, B.PT136 wires Schedule-X into `/bookings/page.tsx` with the real `bookings.listForHost` query feeding `eventsService.add()`, plus the three bridging tasks (hour-axis foreground, today-highlight ink, container overflow). |

| B.PT133 | Document the Figma → code pipeline + update SKILL.md Path B to use the project's `pnpm figma:spec` | SHIPPED | `4e7786b` | Follow-up to B.PT132 (deferral named in that commit's body). This commit makes the docs accurate. **`docs/figma/README.md`**: prepended a new "Figma → code: extract a spec from a live Figma file" h2 with end-to-end Chrome + PAT setup (4 numbered steps), the run-the-pipeline command + expected output, what the spec JSON contains, debugging via the three sub-step pnpm scripts, and where the cache lives (`$FIGMA_KIWI_DIR`, default `/tmp/figma_kiwi`; idiom for persisting under `docs/figma/.cache/<file-key>` for cross-session reuse). The original "code → Figma" content (Figtail + html.to.design) was demoted into its own h2 with its phases re-leveled (h2 → h3, h3 → h4) so the heading hierarchy stays consistent. **`.claude/skills/figma-to-code/SKILL.md`** Path B section: replaced the made-up `npx figma-kiwi-protocol capture --node-id=12:106 > spec.json` (upstream's CLI doesn't accept `--node-id`; that's a project-level concept) with the real `pnpm figma:spec <feature> --node-id=N:M`, named the audited vendored subset (`vendor/figma-kiwi/`) + the safe-deps replacement (`scripts/figma-decode.mjs` using `kiwi-schema` + `fzstd` via npm), and broke the three-step pipeline (`figma:capture` → `figma:decode --write` → `figma:spec`) out so debugging picks up where it failed. **Files modified**: `.claude/skills/figma-to-code/SKILL.md` (~30 LOC delta in Path B), `docs/figma/README.md` (~190 LOC added top, ~6 LOC heading demotion). **Files NOT modified**: `vendor/figma-kiwi/`, `scripts/figma-*.mjs`, `package.json` (B.PT132 surface; this commit is doc-only). **Verification**: `pnpm tsc --noEmit` clean; `pnpm lint` 0 errors; `pnpm test:run` same 5 pre-existing failures as B.PT132 (workspaces-invitations × 4, webhooks × 1) — confirmed unrelated by `git stash` round-trip in B.PT131. |
| B.PT132 | Vendor read-path of allan-simon/figma-kiwi-protocol + project orchestrator (`pnpm figma:spec`) | SHIPPED | `43cd229` | First step toward closing the F1-class friction (Figma MCP rate limits) catalogued in the `figma-to-code` skill from B.PT131's post-mortem. The agent's research had named `figma-kiwi-protocol` (allan-simon, ~23 stars, MIT, 2026) as the only path that bypasses Figma's REST + MCP rate limits — it taps the same Kiwi-encoded WebSocket frames Figma's editor uses, via Chrome DevTools Protocol. Sandbox blocked the obvious `pnpm add github:allan-simon/figma-kiwi-protocol` (untrusted lifecycle scripts), so this commit goes the more conservative route: **vendor only the read-path subset, audit each file by hand, replace the unsafe parts with npm-published equivalents**. Each vendored file got a verdict in `vendor/figma-kiwi/AUDIT.md`: `lib/{kiwi,scenegraph,css,index}.mjs` are zero-import pure functions (binary inspection / Map-and-string manipulation / math) — vendored verbatim except `index.mjs` which was trimmed to drop the unvendored `svg.mjs` re-exports. `bin/capture.mjs` does CDP fetch + WebSocket scoped to localhost:9222, fs writes scoped to `$FIGMA_KIWI_DIR` (default `/tmp/figma_kiwi`), no `child_process` / `eval` / postinstall — vendored verbatim. The dangerous `bin/decode.mjs` (does `git clone https://github.com/evanw/kiwi.git` + `npm install fzstd` + `npx tsx` at runtime — exactly the pattern the sandbox blocked) is **NOT vendored**; replaced with our own `scripts/figma-decode.mjs` that uses npm-published `kiwi-schema@0.5.0` (by Evan Wallace himself, MIT, no deps — the same library upstream's runtime-cloned repo would build) + `fzstd@0.1.1` (MIT, no deps) as devDeps. **Files added**: `vendor/figma-kiwi/{AUDIT.md,LICENSE,lib/{kiwi,scenegraph,css,index}.mjs,bin/capture.mjs}`, `scripts/figma-decode.mjs`, `scripts/figma-spec.mjs` (project orchestrator: validate env → run capture → run decode → filter to node subtree → emit `docs/figma/spec-<feature>.json`). **Files modified**: `package.json` (3 npm scripts: `figma:capture`, `figma:decode`, `figma:spec`; 2 devDeps: `kiwi-schema`, `fzstd`). **Verification**: `pnpm tsc --noEmit` clean; `pnpm lint` 0 errors (warnings unchanged at 7); `pnpm test:run` 5 pre-existing failures (workspaces-invitations × 4, webhooks × 1) confirmed unrelated — this commit touches no procedure / test surface. Smoke test: `pnpm figma:spec --help` prints usage (passes Phase 4 gate); `node -e "import vendored lib"` round-trip exports work + `rgbaToCSS` produces correct hex/rgba including a `--oh-tint` match for `(10,10,10,0.06)`. **Hard stop after this commit**: user's one-time Chrome+PAT setup is required before `pnpm figma:spec chrome-morph --node-id=12:106` works (relaunch Chrome with `--remote-debugging-port=9222`, generate Figma PAT, set `CDP_WS_URL` + `FIGMA_TOKEN` env). Documented in `pnpm figma:spec --help` and (TODO followup B.PT133) `docs/figma/README.md` setup section. **Skill update deferred to follow-up**: `.claude/skills/figma-to-code/SKILL.md` Path B currently references `npx figma-kiwi-protocol`; should update to `pnpm figma:spec` once the README is also written. |

Matches `oh-host-content { background: var(--oh-paper); }` (`globals.css:3196`) and Figma node 12:205. (2) **Baseline regen mystery**: `expect(page).toHaveScreenshot({fullPage: true, animations: "disabled"})` with `--update-snapshots` consistently produced PNGs with INVERTED color (paper outer / Sisal inner), DESPITE `page.screenshot({fullPage: true, animations: "disabled"})` from the same page state producing CORRECT colors per in-page canvas readback (verified via `getComputedStyle` + `ImageBitmap` sampling). Cause unclear — could be a Playwright caching quirk, dev-server compile race, or interaction with `(dev)/layout.tsx`'s wrapping. Bypassed via a one-off helper test that called `page.screenshot()` and wrote the buffer directly to the four chrome-morph baselines. Both defined on `:root` (lines 132 + 244). Tailwind utility `bg-oh-bg` aliases `--color-oh-bg` → `var(--oh-paper)`. There is NO `bg-oh-frame` utility — must use arbitrary `bg-[color:var(--oh-frame)]`. **Files**: `src/app/(dev)/playground/animations/chrome-morph/_components/variant-surface.tsx` (~6 LOC delta — outer + panel bg swap, comment updates), 4 PNG baselines regenerated. **Verification**: `pnpm tsc --noEmit` clean; `pnpm lint` 0 errors; `pnpm exec playwright test e2e/playground.spec.ts` 11/11 pass. |
