This commit honors the user's explicit instruction over both production and Figma — the lab is dev-only, the user is the design arbiter, and they've stated the inversion clearly enough across two messages that the intent is unambiguous. **Mechanism + chrome typography + animation timing all unchanged**. Panel still animates `top/left/right/bottom + border-radius` from `(barH+12, sideW, 12, 12)` 18px to `(previewFrame, previewFrame, previewFrame, previewFrame)` 25px. Bar + sidebar still transparent (now over paper-lighter instead of Sisal-darker). **Files**: `src/app/(dev)/playground/animations/chrome-morph/_components/variant-surface.tsx` (~6 LOC delta — 2 shadow class removals, 2 bg color swaps, comment block updates), 4 PNG baselines regenerated. **Verification**: `pnpm tsc --noEmit` clean; `pnpm lint` 0 errors; `pnpm exec playwright test e2e/playground.spec.ts` 11/11 pass. **If user comes back and says the rounded panel should actually be lighter (paper) — i.e. they realized they meant the opposite identity** — the fix is a one-line color swap on the outer container + the panel; baselines regenerate cleanly. |
| B.PT129 | Chrome-morph lab — panel inset + radius parity with Figma (12px cream gap, rounded-18 normal, rounded-25 preview, list border cleanup) | SHIPPED | `8ea8ac7` | User reopened the styling fidelity audit after B.PT128 with `/Users/santiagofuentes/Desktop/trpc-lab/design.fig` (zip: kiwi `canvas.fig` 115KB + `meta.json` + 4KB `thumbnail.png` + `images/`). Couldn't parse the binary directly, but the saved `get_design_context` output from the earlier MCP call (12:106 normal frame) gave the precise deltas. **Findings**: (1) Inner panel position — Figma's NORMAL has `oh-host-content` at `(192, 52) → (2548, 1259)` inside `main.relative` `(192, 40) → (2560, 1271)`, which means the panel sits 12px below the bar bottom + 12px from frame right + 12px from frame bottom. LEFT abuts sidebar at x=192 (no gap there). The 12px L-shape on top/right/bottom is the cream `--oh-frame` showing through — what makes the chrome read as floating over a frame instead of welded to the panel. B.PT128 had panel position `top=barH, left=sideW, right=0, bottom=0` (flush against everything, sharp transition between cream chrome and paper panel). (2) Border-radius — Figma's normal panel has `rounded-[18px]`, preview is `rounded-[25px]`. B.PT128 animated from `0` → `18px` (card) / `25px` (fullscreen) which produced sharp corners in the resting normal state — exactly what the user flagged as "the borders are sharp the design doesnt even have borders". (3) Card vs fullscreen radius — B.PT128 had asymmetric preview radius (18 card / 25 fullscreen). Figma uses 25 in preview regardless of viewport. (4) Booking list extra top border — `<ul>` had `border-y` which produced a hairline above the first row that Figma doesn't have (per-li `border-b` on rows 1-11 only). (5) Header gap — booking row `<header>` had `gap-2` (8px). Figma uses `gap-[0px]` (resolved from a 0-valued variable). **Fix**: re-shape the `DIMS` table to carry `normalGap` (12 for both modes) + `previewFrame` (12 card / 15 fullscreen — matches Figma's `p-[15px]`). Add `NORMAL_PANEL_RADIUS = 18` + `PREVIEW_PANEL_RADIUS = 25` constants. Recompute panel position: `panelTop = isPreview ? previewFrame : barH + normalGap`, `panelLeft = isPreview ? previewFrame : sideW`, `panelRight/Bottom = isPreview ? previewFrame : normalGap`. Remove `border-y` from the booking `<ul>`; keep `divide-y` only (matches Figma's per-li `border-b` on rows 1-11 with no rule above the first row). Remove `gap-2` from the row header (Figma 0). **None of these touch the morph mechanism (B.PT126 stays); the bar + sidebar transparency + drop-shadow + chrome typography from B.PT127/128 stay**. The animation now interpolates radius `18 → 25` and inset `(barH+12, sideW, 12, 12) → (previewFrame, previewFrame, previewFrame, previewFrame)` — soft on both sides, never sharp. **Files**: `src/app/(dev)/playground/animations/chrome-morph/_components/variant-surface.tsx` (~25 LOC delta — DIMS shape, panel position calc, panel borderRadius, ul classes, header classes), 4 PNG baselines regenerated. **Verification**: `pnpm tsc --noEmit` clean; `pnpm lint` 0 errors (warnings unchanged at 7); `pnpm exec playwright test e2e/playground.spec.ts` 11/11 pass against new baselines. **Pre-existing test failures noted**: 9 server-side vitest failures (4 in `workspaces-invitations.test.ts`, 5 elsewhere) confirmed pre-existing on the clean tree via `git stash` round-trip — unrelated to this UI-only change in a `(dev)` playground component. |

Audit found four delta classes between the lab and the Figma source: **(1) Background** — Figma has the bar + sidebar TRANSPARENT (the outer cream `--oh-frame` shows through everywhere except the paper panel area). The B.PT127 lab painted them with `bg-[color:var(--oh-bg)]` (paper). Fix: removed `bg-*` from bar + sidebar; they now inherit the outer frame's cream and the only visual depth comes from the existing drop-shadow. **(2) False borders** — Figma's chrome has `border-[transparent]` (0-color borders, no visible lines) but B.PT127 added `border-b border-oh-line` to the bar and `border-r border-oh-line` to the sidebar. Fix: removed both. **(3) Active sidebar item** — Figma's Bookings active row uses `bg-tint` only; the left border is 2px-wide but `transparent`-colored, so no visible ink line. B.PT127 added `border-l-[color:var(--oh-ink)]` (production's pattern). Library section label was on `oh-legend` (11px/800/55) — Figma is 10px Bold tracking-2.5 opacity-70. Tab labels (Upcoming / Past) were on `oh-eyebrow` (10px) — Figma is 11px. Fix: replaced the token classes with explicit `font-mono text-[Npx] font-extrabold/font-bold tracking-[Npx] uppercase opacity-N` arbitrary-value strings to match the Figma vars precisely. None of these touch the morph mechanism (B.PT126 stays). **Files**: `src/app/(dev)/playground/animations/chrome-morph/_components/variant-surface.tsx` (~30 LOC delta), 4 PNG baselines regenerated. **Verification**: tsc clean, lint 0 errors, playwright 11/11 pass against new baselines. |
| B.PT127 | Chrome-morph lab — full Figma visual fidelity (workspace switcher, Library nav, bookings list) | SHIPPED | b74fda6 | User asked the chrome-morph variants to use the SAME classes / styling / layout as the Figma file (nodes 12:106 + 13:1121) — replace the simplified "Mock bar / Library / Bookings/Availability/Profile" lab with the actual dashboard chrome from the Figma source. **Bar**: workspace switcher button "Personal?" + ChevronDown on left; Bookings icon + Settings icon + 24px avatar circle on right; 40px tall; drop-shadow `0_4px_20px_rgb(0_0_0_/_0.25)`; bottom hairline border. **Sidebar**: 192px wide; Bookings active item (6% ink bg + 2px left ink border) on top; "LIBRARY" mono-caps section label; Availability + Profile + Workspaces items below; Collapse-sidebar PanelLeft icon button at footer (36px square). **Panel**: max-w-[760px] centered column; "YOUR BOOKINGS" 32px bold uppercase heading with green emerald-500 live-updates dot, 2px ink bottom border; Upcoming (12) tab with 2px ink underline + divider + Past (00) muted tab; 12-row booking list (lifted verbatim from Figma's preview frame mock data — asafs, Gggg, Jnunij, Nininim, etc.) with hairline dividers, p-4 rows, name h3 + time eyebrow header, date eyebrow, optional quote (Space Grotesk regular 13px) + optional email (mono 12px). All typography uses project tokens — `oh-eyebrow` for mono caps 10px, `oh-legend` for 11px section labels, Space Grotesk for headings via `font-sans`, JetBrains Mono via `font-mono`. **Limitations**: (1) Icons — Figma uses image-based Vector elements with asset URLs that expire in 7 days; ported to lucide-react equivalents (`Calendar`, `Settings`, `Clock`, `User`, `BriefcaseBusiness`, `ChevronDown`, `PanelLeft`) which are semantically identical line icons but not pixel-perfect. (2) Avatar — Figma placeholder image; ported to a paper-tinted circle with hairline border. (3) Live-updates dot — static `bg-emerald-500`, no SSE wiring. (4) Card mode (h-[360px]) clips the booking list via `overflow-hidden`; full-screen mode shows all 12 rows. (5) Animation timing still uses default A=280ms / B=200ms / C=320ms — user to dial in the Figma prototype's actual values. None of these are blockers for the lab; full pixel-fidelity asset port (icons + avatar + live SSE) belongs in the production port commit, not the playground spec. **Mechanism unchanged from B.PT126**: chrome fades in place, panel rides z-10 and animates `top/left/right/bottom + border-radius` over the chrome to claim the freed area. **Files**: `src/app/(dev)/playground/animations/chrome-morph/_components/variant-surface.tsx` (full rewrite — added `BOOKINGS` mock data, `NavItem` sub-component, full chrome layout with lucide icons), 4 PNG baselines regenerated. **Verification**: `pnpm tsc --noEmit` clean; `pnpm lint` 0 errors; `pnpm exec playwright test e2e/playground.spec.ts` 11/11 pass against new baselines. |
| B.PT126 | Chrome-morph mechanism — Figma-spec port (chrome fades, panel translates + grows over it) | SHIPPED | _to be filled by commit_ | User designed the chrome morph in Figma (file `QfWYFD5AHjpkEDUfX94mUG`, nodes `12:106` normal + `13:1121` preview) and asked to port the design exactly. Layout extracted via the official Figma MCP server (`mcp__plugin_figma_figma__get_design_context` + `get_metadata` + `get_variable_defs`). **Findings vs the B.PT125 grid-template approach**: the Figma design intentionally keeps chrome positioned (chrome stays in its bounding boxes; only opacity flips 1 → 0) and has the panel TRANSLATE + GROW over the chrome via animated `top/left/right/bottom`. The panel rides on `z-10` so it overlays the fading chrome during the morph — "content appears in front of what used to be chrome", verbatim from the user's intent. This differs from B.PT125 (grid track interpolation, chrome geometry collapses) — both end up with "panel claims full bleed, chrome gone" but the visual feel is different. The Figma design is now the source of truth. **Background colors** match existing tokens (no new tokens needed): outer `#d3ccbd` = `--oh-frame` (Sisal); inner panel `#eee7d5` = `--oh-paper` (Aths Special). **Border-radius** at the inner panel: 25px in full-screen, 18px in card mode (matching production `--oh-r-window` for the smaller card). **Mechanism details**: bar absolute `top-0 left-0 right-0 h-[44/52]px`, fades opacity. Sidebar absolute `left-0 top-[barH] bottom-0 w-[152/208]px`, fades opacity. Panel absolute `z-10` with animated `top/left/right/bottom` from `barH/sideW/0/0` (normal — flush against chrome) to `framePx/framePx/framePx/framePx` (preview — uniform 12/15px frame revealing `--oh-frame` background underneath). All transitions composite into a single `transition: top X, left X, right X, bottom X, border-radius X, opacity X` with shared duration + easing per variant. Compositor-friendly for opacity (chrome fade); layout-constrained-to-self for panel inset (absolute children don't reflow siblings). **Animation timing**: defaulted to existing variant durations (A=280ms, B=200ms, C=320ms — all using `cubic-bezier(0.16, 1, 0.3, 1)` or `cubic-bezier(0.32, 0.72, 0, 1)` per `variants.ts`). User to dial in the Figma-prototype's actual duration/easing once they grab it from the Figma Prototype panel; default values are reasonable matches per the May-2026 research. **Files**: `src/app/(dev)/playground/animations/chrome-morph/_components/variant-surface.tsx` (full rewrite — drops grid-template + 2x2 grid in favor of absolute-positioned chrome + z-10 panel with inset animation), `src/app/(dev)/playground/animations/chrome-morph/page.tsx` (intro paragraph + comment block updated to describe new mechanism), 4 PNG baselines regenerated. **Verification**: `pnpm tsc --noEmit` clean; `pnpm lint` 0 errors (warnings unchanged at 7); `pnpm exec playwright test e2e/playground.spec.ts` 11/11 pass against new baselines. **Animation timing handoff still pending**: user to provide exact duration/easing from Figma's Prototype panel (or via the Plugin Console JSON dump `JSON.stringify(figma.currentPage.selection[0].reactions, null, 2)`). Once provided, port the matching values to `variants.ts` and the production preview-mode CSS in `globals.css` (replacing the View-Transitions chrome-morph block lines 3260-3499 + the Firefox opt-out). |
| B.PT125 | Chrome-morph mechanism rewrite — grid-template interpolation (panel grows as chrome shrinks; replaces B.PT123 transform-only redesign) | SHIPPED | _to be filled by commit_ | User reviewed the B.PT123 + B.PT124 implementation and reported the shape was wrong. They wanted the **panel to expand into the freed space** while bar + sidebar shrink to 0 simultaneously — a true coordinated layout morph, not "chrome translates off-screen with panel staying static." B.PT123 misread the original B.PT60-71 intent: the choppiness came from the View Transitions implementation, not from the layout-animation pattern itself. The honest fix is to keep the layout animation and pick a smoother technique. **Research summary**: two parallel agents surveyed (1) modern browser perf characteristics for layout-changing animation and (2) production examples from Linear / Cal.com / dub.co / Notion / Figma / Vercel / Stripe. Findings: every production app uses some form of `width` / `flex-basis` / `grid-template` animation on a flex/grid sibling, with the panel growing automatically as a sibling track. None use View Transitions API for chrome collapse. Apple's NSSplitViewController.toggleSidebar() and SwiftUI's `withAnimation { columnVisibility = .detailOnly }` animate the layout primitive directly — not transforms. The Motion.dev "Web Animation Performance Tier List" classifies all layout-changing properties (`grid-template-columns`, `flex-basis`, `width`, `height`) as D-tier (full layout pipeline every frame) — but on 2024+ hardware that's ~3-6ms/frame for a 3-region shell, well inside the 60fps budget. There is no compositor-only path for "panel grows" because the panel growing IS a layout change. Accept the main-thread cost; make it cheap. **Browser support**: `grid-template-columns/rows` interpolation has been 94.05% Baseline since Chrome 107 (2022) / Firefox 66 (2019) / Safari 16 (2022). Cross-browser safe; no Firefox opt-out needed. **`interpolate-size: allow-keywords`** is Chrome-only as of May 2026 (WebKit issue #348 + Mozilla issue #1022 still pending), NOT in Interop 2026 — skip it. **Implementation**: `_components/variants.ts` rewrote `VariantSpec` to carry `duration` + `ease` per variant (data-driven timing). `_components/variant-surface.tsx` rewrote the rendering: shell is a 2x2 grid (`grid-template-rows: <bar-h> 1fr; grid-template-columns: <side-w> 1fr`), bar at row 1 spanning both cols, sidebar at row 2 col 1, panel at row 2 col 2. The shell's grid template values flip from `(44px, 152px)` / `(52px, 212px)` (card / fullScreen) to `(0px, 0px)` on preview entry; CSS `transition: grid-template-columns ${duration} ${ease}, grid-template-rows ${duration} ${ease}` interpolates the track sizes. Bar + sidebar contents `overflow: hidden` so they clip cleanly as their cells shrink. Panel sized via `1fr` so it claims the freed tracks automatically. **Variants differ only in TIMING + EASING** (same DOM, same mechanism): A) Apple split-view — 280ms `cubic-bezier(0.32, 0.72, 0, 1)` (iOS ease-out-quart, the macOS NSSplitViewController curve); B) Snappy iOS — 200ms `cubic-bezier(0.16, 1, 0.3, 1)` (repo's existing sidebar curve, Linear / Cal.com vibe); C) Mac sheet — 320ms `cubic-bezier(0.16, 1, 0.3, 1)` (slower settle for breathing room). User picks by direct A/B/C feel comparison. **Comparison-page description rewritten** to reflect the new mechanism — drops "translate off-screen" / "transform-only" language, adds the `NSSplitViewController.toggleSidebar()` reference. **Visual regression**: all 4 chrome-morph baselines (comparison page + 3 full-screen routes) regenerated. The static "phase=normal" screenshot is identical across A/B/C variants in resting state — they only diverge during interactive play (timing curves don't show in single frames). That's expected; the static spec catches structural regressions, the user catches motion regressions interactively. **Files**: `src/app/(dev)/playground/animations/chrome-morph/_components/variants.ts` (added `duration` + `ease` to `VariantSpec`, replaced variant descriptions), `src/app/(dev)/playground/animations/chrome-morph/_components/variant-surface.tsx` (full rewrite: dropped transform-based logic, replaced with grid-template interpolation), `src/app/(dev)/playground/animations/chrome-morph/page.tsx` (description rewrite — comments + visible intro paragraph), 4 PNG baselines (all regenerated). **Verification**: `pnpm tsc --noEmit` clean; `pnpm lint` 0 errors (warnings unchanged at 7); `pnpm exec playwright test e2e/playground.spec.ts` 11/11 pass against regenerated baselines. **Sources cited in code comments**: bram.us 2025-02-07 (View Transitions UA keyframe issue, why VT API was wrong); Apple Developer Forums on NavigationSplitView animations; Motion.dev Web Animation Performance Tier List; caniuse.com `grid-template-columns_animation`. **Production port**: still deferred. Once the user picks A/B/C, a follow-up commit ports the winning timing to production preview-mode (`(host)/preview/[handle]`) and removes the View-Transitions-based chrome-morph block from `globals.css` (lines 3260-3499) along with the Firefox opt-out. |
| B.PT124 | Chrome-morph variant full-screen routes (per-variant production-scale read) | SHIPPED | _to be filled by commit_ | User asked for a way to view each B.PT123 variant at full screen rather than only inside the 360px-tall comparison card. The comparison page is great for picking the feel side-by-side, but production scale matters — the morph reads differently at viewport height than at card height. **Implementation**: extracted shared chrome-morph code to `_components/` (underscore-prefix keeps it out of Next's route resolver). **Critical split**: data + types live in `_components/variants.ts` (NO `"use client"` directive), the React component lives in `_components/variant-surface.tsx` (`"use client"`). The split is required because the server entry at `[variant]/page.tsx` calls `VARIANTS.map(...)` in `generateStaticParams` — a Server Component importing a `const` from a `"use client"` module receives an opaque module reference, not the array, and `.map` throws `is not a function`. Caught DURING implementation by the user's running dev server; fixed by lifting the data into a plain TS module. The component file still re-exports the type aliases so existing callsites that import types from it keep working. `VariantSurface` gained a `fullScreen` boolean prop — when true: container is `min-h-dvh` instead of `h-[360px]` with no border, sidebar bumps from `w-36` (144px) to `w-52` (208px) so it reads at production scale. Added dynamic route `[variant]/page.tsx` (server entry) that validates the param against the canonical `VARIANTS` list (case-insensitive) and 404s on anything else. `generateStaticParams` returns `[{variant: 'a'}, {variant: 'b'}, {variant: 'c'}]` so Next pre-renders the three keys in non-prod. Client UI lives in `[variant]/full-screen-lab.tsx` — the variant surface fills the viewport with two floating affordances at z-50: top-left has a backdrop-blur card with a back-link to the comparison page + variant identity (key + title); bottom-right has the phase toggle (same shape as the comparison page's toggle so muscle memory carries between routes). **Comparison page updated**: each variant card now shows a small `Open full-screen` link with a `MaximizeIcon` next to the variant title, linking to `/playground/animations/chrome-morph/<key-lowercased>`. **Visual regression**: 3 new routes added to `e2e/playground.spec.ts`'s `PLAYGROUND_ROUTES` manifest; baselines captured in the same `--update-snapshots` pass. `chrome-morph` (the comparison page) baseline also regenerated because the page gained the per-card "Open full-screen" links. Total spec count: 11/11 (was 8/8). **Files**: `src/app/(dev)/playground/animations/chrome-morph/_components/variants.ts` (NEW — data + types, plain TS module), `src/app/(dev)/playground/animations/chrome-morph/_components/variant-surface.tsx` (NEW — `"use client"` component, imports types from `variants.ts`, re-exports them for back-compat), `src/app/(dev)/playground/animations/chrome-morph/page.tsx` (modified — imports `VARIANTS`/`Phase` from data, `VariantSurface` from component; adds per-card `Open full-screen` link with `MaximizeIcon`), `src/app/(dev)/playground/animations/chrome-morph/[variant]/page.tsx` (NEW — server entry + param validation + generateStaticParams), `src/app/(dev)/playground/animations/chrome-morph/[variant]/full-screen-lab.tsx` (NEW — client UI with floating top-left back-link card + bottom-right toggle), `e2e/playground.spec.ts` (added 3 routes to `PLAYGROUND_ROUTES`), 4 PNG baselines (3 new + 1 regenerated chrome-morph). **Verification**: `pnpm tsc --noEmit` clean; `pnpm lint` 0 errors (warnings unchanged at 7); `pnpm exec playwright test e2e/playground.spec.ts` 11/11 pass against new + regenerated baselines. |
| B.PT123 | Chrome-morph lab — A/B/C variant compare (transform-only, no View Transitions API) | SHIPPED | _to be filled by commit_ | User reported the View-Transitions-driven chrome morph from B.PT121 was choppy on Chrome and degraded on Firefox 144. Research surfaced three root causes: (1) UA keyframes for `::view-transition-group(*)` always include `width` + `height` regardless of dev-supplied CSS, forcing main-thread animation when the morph runs three named groups in parallel (bram.us 2025-02-07 article); Chrome 144's no-op width/height fast-path doesn't apply because the bar genuinely goes to `height: 0` and the sidebar gap to `width: 0`. (2) Collapsing snapshots to zero bounds produces sub-pixel raster artifacts on HiDPI (Jake Archibald's 2024 aspect-ratio article — his `fit-content` mitigation isn't available for collapse-to-nothing). (3) Apple HIG never prescribes "animate to zero bounds" — Stage Manager, iPad Slide Over, iOS Reader Mode all TRANSLATE chrome off-screen using `transform` + `opacity` only. The current implementation fights both the browser AND the design vocabulary. **Decision**: keep the lab as the design spec; redesign it from scratch as a **3-variant comparison** so the user picks the feel before any production port. The lab is dev-only — destructive redesign is fine per user's explicit go-ahead. **Implementation**: each variant is a self-contained mini-dashboard mock (~360px tall card, no production CSS dependency). Panel is full-inset always — no layout animation. Bar is absolute overlay that translates upward off-card on preview entry. Sidebar is absolute overlay that translates leftward. **Variant A** — pure `transform: translate*` + `opacity: 0`. The honest minimum, compositor-only. **Variant B** — A + panel `transform: scale(0.99 → 1)` settle to acknowledge focus shift. **Variant C** — A + chrome `filter: blur(0 → 8px)` for an iOS-y depth feel. All three at 300ms `cubic-bezier(0.16, 1, 0.3, 1)` (project's `--ease-oh`) — within Apple HIG's 250-400ms range. `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce` honored automatically by the global blanket from B.PT119. One toggle button drives all three in lockstep so they morph simultaneously and the user can pick by direct visual comparison. **Why no View Transitions API at all**: per the same research, named-group VT is a great fit for cross-route morphs where elements PERSIST and CHANGE BOUNDS (a card growing to a detail page); it is NOT designed for elements that DISAPPEAR. The chrome-morph use case is "chrome elements exit, panel claims focus" — pure exit animation. Transform-only on absolute-positioned overlays expresses that intent directly. The winning variant gets ported to production preview-mode (`/(host)/preview/[handle]`) in a follow-up commit; the existing `globals.css` chrome-morph block (lines 3260-3499) gets replaced at that time with the matching transform rules. This commit is the spec, not the production port. **Files**: `src/app/(dev)/playground/animations/chrome-morph/page.tsx` (rewrite — ~220 LOC, drops `<SidebarProvider>` / `<Sidebar>` / `<SidebarInset>` imports, drops `document.startViewTransition()`, replaces with three self-contained `<VariantSurface>` components driven by a single phase state). `e2e/playground.spec.ts-snapshots/playground-screenshot-playground-animations-chrome-morph-1-public-darwin.png` regenerated against the new layout. **Verification**: `pnpm tsc --noEmit` clean; `pnpm lint` 0 errors (warnings unchanged at 7); `pnpm exec playwright test e2e/playground.spec.ts` 8/8 pass against the new baseline. **Sources cited in the lab's source comment**: bram.us 2025-02-07 (UA keyframes force main-thread); Jake Archibald 2024 aspect-ratio article; Apple HIG Motion section. |
| B.PT122 | Login password input — `has-[]:` overrides to beat shadcn `InputGroup` default focus specificity | SHIPPED | c6f85eb | User report post-merge: `/login` password field STILL showed shadcn's default focus ring (3px ring-ring/50 + border-ring) on keyboard focus despite the B.PT105 + B.PT114 focus-vocabulary sweeps. Email field above (native `<input class="oh-input">`) was correct; password field (shadcn `<InputGroup>` wrapper for the eye-toggle composition) leaked the default ring. **Root cause**: shadcn's `InputGroup` primitive (`src/components/ui/input-group.tsx:17`) hardcodes three focus rules on its wrapper using the `:has()` pseudo-class — `has-[[data-slot=input-group-control]:focus-visible]:border-ring`, `has-[[data-slot=input-group-control]:focus-visible]:ring-3`, `has-[[data-slot=input-group-control]:focus-visible]:ring-ring/50`. The `:has(... :focus-visible)` selector compiles at CSS specificity **0,3,0** (one class on the host + the inner attribute selector + the `:focus-visible` pseudo). The previous wrapper override at `credentials-form.tsx:94` used `focus-within:[box-shadow:var(--oh-focus-shadow-input)]` which is only **0,2,0** (one class + one pseudo). The default WON the cascade; shadcn's blue/grey ring leaked through on every keyboard focus. The B.PT105 sweep migrated 16 callsites + the `.oh-input` rule but didn't audit consumers of `InputGroup` because the audit grep targeted the legacy `outline:` and Tailwind `ring-3 border-ring` shorthand utilities, not the `:has()` form. **Fix**: replace `focus-within:[box-shadow:...]` on the wrapper with three matching `has-[[data-slot=input-group-control]:focus-visible]:` overrides at the SAME 0,3,0 specificity — `has-[...]:border-[color:var(--oh-ink)]` (counters `border-ring`), `has-[...]:ring-0` (counters `ring-3` + `ring-ring/50`), `has-[...]:[box-shadow:var(--oh-focus-shadow-input)]` (paints the soft halo+ring vocabulary on the same focus-visible selector that drives the rest of the app — matches B.PT105's "keyboard-only focus signal" decision). The `bg-lift` stays on `focus-within:` (0,2,0) so mouse clicks still get the +8% bg cue — that's not competing with the shadcn defaults, only the ring + border are. **Lesson for future audits**: any consumer of shadcn `InputGroup` (or any wrapper that ships `:has()` defaults) needs the override at matching `:has()` specificity. Tailwind's standard `focus-within:` / `focus-visible:` overrides at 0,2,0 lose silently. Code is in tree as of merge `c6f85eb` (the fix landed during conflict resolution merging `fix/mobile-back-to-app-flash`'s wrapper override against parallel-agent commit `0bc411d`'s tiered-states refactor on `feat/ui-playground`). **Files**: `src/app/login/credentials-form.tsx` (the password wrapper at line 118 + the comment block at lines 78-117 documenting the specificity calculation). **Verification**: tsc clean; lint 0 errors; manual keyboard-Tab smoke on `/login` confirms the password field now paints the same `--oh-focus-shadow-input` (1px paper halo + ink frame thickening + soft outer ring) as the email field above. |
| B.PT121 | Fix B.PT118 lab regressions — chrome-morph layout + bookings-tabs missing `<Tabs>` + proxy.ts blocked all playground routes (originally B.PT120 on `feat/ui-playground`; renumbered on merge into feat/ui-expose due to the B.PT115 ID collision with the mobile-back-to-app-flash workstream) | SHIPPED | _to be filled by commit_ | Three regressions surfaced post-ship of B.PT116-120 (originally B.PT115-119 on `feat/ui-playground`). **(1) Proxy.ts redirected `/playground/*` to `/login`** — the auth proxy in `src/proxy.ts` defaults to "redirect to /login if not isPublic". The `isPublic` check covered `/login`, `/register`, `/h/<handle>`, `/invitations/<token>`, `/embed/...` — but NOT `/playground/*`. Every playground route 302'd to login during the B.PT120 baseline capture, which means the original 8 PNG baselines all rendered the same /login page (md5-verified after the fact: every PNG was byte-identical, 33,073 bytes). The visual regression spec was passing against /login screenshots for routes the user never actually saw. **Fix**: add `isPlayground` (`pathname === "/playground" || pathname.startsWith("/playground/")`) to the `isPublic` check in `src/proxy.ts`. Production behavior is unchanged because `(dev)/layout.tsx` calls `notFound()` when `NODE_ENV === "production"` — the route still 404s in prod, this only unblocks the dev-only debug surface. **(2) Chrome-morph lab broken visually** — the hand-crafted mini-shell in `chrome-morph/page.tsx` placed `[data-slot="sidebar-gap"]` + `[data-slot="sidebar-container"]` BOTH in flex flow with the sidebar-container set to `position: relative`. Production's `.oh-app [data-slot="sidebar-container"]` rule (`globals.css:901`) expects `position: fixed` (set by the shadcn `Sidebar` primitive), with the gap reserving in-flow space and the container floating over. Stacking both as relative-positioned flex children double-counted the 200px. The mini-shell also constrained `.oh-host-content` to a 480px fixed-height card, but production CSS has `height: calc(100svh - 24px - var(--oh-dashboard-bar-block))` (`globals.css:3164`) — viewport-anchored, bullies any constraining card into broken layout. **Fix**: drop the hand-crafted mini-shell, render the **real `<SidebarProvider>` + `<Sidebar>` + `<SidebarInset>` primitives** at full viewport so the production CSS targets the same elements with the same selectors. Lab title + description + back-link move into the panel content. Toggle becomes a fixed-position floating button at bottom-right with shadow + hover invert — always in view across the morph. **(3) Bookings-tabs lab threw at render** — `Base UI: TabsRootContext is missing. Tabs parts must be placed within <Tabs.Root>`. `BookingsTabBar` uses `<TabsList>` (Base UI primitive) which requires `<Tabs.Root>` ancestor. Production's `BookingsList` (`bookings-list.tsx:77`) wraps `<BookingsTabBar>` in `<Tabs value={activeTab} onValueChange>`; the lab missed the wrapper. **Fix**: wrap in `<Tabs value={activeTab} onValueChange>` from `@/components/ui/tabs` with a synchronous setState handler (no router push — lab isn't behind a real route). **Baselines re-captured** for ALL 8 routes via `pnpm exec playwright test e2e/playground.spec.ts --update-snapshots` — every previous baseline was a /login redirect screenshot, so the regen pass produced 8 distinct PNGs (md5-verified). **Files**: `src/proxy.ts` (one-line `isPublic` extension + comment), `src/app/(dev)/playground/animations/chrome-morph/page.tsx` (rewrite using real primitives), `src/app/(dev)/playground/animations/bookings-tabs/page.tsx` (Tabs wrapper), `e2e/playground.spec.ts-snapshots/*.png` (all 8 regenerated). **Verification**: `pnpm tsc --noEmit` clean; `pnpm lint` 0 errors (warnings unchanged); `pnpm exec playwright test e2e/playground.spec.ts` 8/8 pass against new baselines. |

The toggle button drives the morph synchronously via `document.startViewTransition(() => setPreview(p => !p))`. | B.PT115 | Mobile sidebar — freeze nav groups during exit animation to kill the "main app sidebar snapping into place" flash (originally B.PT104 on `fix/mobile-back-to-app-flash`; renumbered on merge into feat/ui-expose due to ID collision with the layout-consistency workstream's B.PT104) | SHIPPED | 4178925 | User report (mobile QA): on mobile, sitting on `/settings/general` with the sidebar drawer open + tapping any link that leaves the settings tree (e.g. "Bookings") plays the drawer's reverse animation correctly, but FOR ABOUT 200ms during the exit animation the drawer renders the DESTINATION page's nav groups (MAIN_NAV_GROUPS) instead of the SETTINGS nav the user was looking at. Reads as the "main app sidebar snapping into place" before the route content paints. **Root cause** (verified by tracing render sequence in `oh-app-sidebar.tsx:174-177`): `MobileNavContent` reads `usePathname()` + computes `navGroupsForPath(pathname)` on every render. Render sequence on link tap: (1) Link click triggers `router.push` — pathname updates synchronously to destination; (2) `MobileNavContent` re-renders with the NEW pathname → groups recompute SETTINGS_NAV_GROUPS → MAIN_NAV_GROUPS while the drawer is still on screen; (3) `ContentSlot`'s pathname-effect fires `setOpenMobile(false)` → `closing={true}` → exit animation begins; (4) reverse-stagger plays for ~200ms — and during all 200ms the drawer is rendering the WRONG (destination) nav. **Reference codebases**: dub `apps/web/ui/layout/sidebar/sidebar-nav.tsx:517 (Area)` keeps EVERY area mounted at all times and CSS-toggles visibility per `currentArea` — same principle (don't swap content mid-transition) but heavier architecture. cal.com `apps/web/modules/shell/navigation/Navigation.tsx:108 (MobileNavigation)` uses a fixed bottom-nav bar (no drawer at all). Lab's content-slot architecture (B.PT49) sits between those; freeze-at-mount is the minimal fix that matches the architecture without rewriting it. **Fix**: 3-line change in `MobileNavContent` — replace `const groups = navGroupsForPath(pathname)` with `const [groups] = useState(() => navGroupsForPath(pathname))`. Snapshot at MOUNT means re-renders during the exit window keep showing the source page's nav. The drawer's lifecycle is short-lived (mounted only while the user has it open + during the ~200ms exit) so re-opening on a new route = fresh mount = fresh groups; freezing at mount doesn't stick the value stale. **Why not freeze on `closing`-flip instead**: by the time `closing` flips true, the wrong-groups render already happened in render B (closing was still false when pathname updated). Freezing at mount catches the bug in render B; freezing at closing-flip catches it only in render C (one render too late). **Sweep for similar patterns** (per user request): scanned every component reading `usePathname()` + every component with an exit-animation pattern (`closing` / `onExitComplete`). Only `MobileNavContent` matches the failure shape — kept-mounted-during-exit AND reads pathname-derived state directly in render. Other pathname consumers (`OhAppSidebar` desktop, `OhDashboardLayout`, `OhDashboardBar`) are always-rendered (no exit window) or don't compute route-dependent UI content. The `ResponsiveModal` family doesn't read pathname. View Transitions snapshots have the OPPOSITE problem (over-stale snapshot) handled separately by VT API mechanics. **Files**: `src/components/oh/oh-app-sidebar.tsx` (1 line `useState` import + 1 line state replacing the const + comment block citing the dub/cal references). **Verification**: tsc clean, lint 0 errors. No new test (the bug is a 200ms visual flash; vitest can't observe it; manual mobile-browser smoke is the canonical verification — flow: open mobile drawer on `/settings/general` → tap "Bookings" → drawer animates out showing settings nav the entire time, no main-nav flash). **Worktree provenance**: shipped from `fix/mobile-back-to-app-flash` worktree off `feat/ui-expose@cd29054`. The original commit `4178925` body still names "B.PT104" — that reference now points at the layout-consistency row, not this one; the renumber is documented in the source comment at `oh-app-sidebar.tsx:177` and in the merge commit body. |

Each carried the legacy `outline: 2px solid var(--oh-ink); outline-offset: 2px` pattern: `.oh-dashboard-bar-trigger`, `.oh-time-picker-trigger`, `.oh-time-picker-period-cell` (negative-offset variant — mapped to inset shadow with same halo+ring math), `.oh-post-delete` + `.oh-seg button` + `.oh-tweaks-head button` (combined selector), `.oh-post` (negative-offset card variant — mapped to inset shadow), `.oh-textarea` (`:focus` + `:focus-visible` mirror), `.oh-view-toggle`, `.oh-day-strip-slide`, `.oh-slot-chip`, `.oh-dev-checklist-box`. Pluralization handled by ICU instead of the inline ternary, so es renders `2 anfitriones, 5 reservas` instead of literal English. **New `EventTypes` namespace** (en + es): 41 keys across panel + create-dialog + edit-dialog + host-pool-dialog. The i18n-coverage test only flags JSX text + visible attrs, not zod input message strings, so they don't fail the CLEAN_DIRS gate. Translating them properly requires either a custom `zodResolver` that maps `ZodError.code` to a translation key, or upgrading to zod 4's `errorMap` localization API. Defer to a separate B.PT## row when a non-English user actually trips one of these (rate-limited surface — most users never hit `30 characters max`). **Files**: `messages/en.json` (+41 keys), `messages/es.json` (mirror), 4 × `event-types/components/*.tsx`, `src/lib/__tests__/i18n-coverage.test.ts` (+1 CLEAN_DIRS entry). **Verification**: `pnpm tsc --noEmit` clean (after clearing stale `.next/dev/types`); `pnpm lint` 0 errors (warnings unchanged at 7); `pnpm test:run src/lib/__tests__/i18n-coverage.test.ts` 17/17 pass — the new event-types CLEAN_DIRS contract passes alongside the 5 existing CLEAN_DIRS + 7 CLEAN_FILES contracts. **Defers**: zod schema message i18n (queued for when a non-English user hits one). | B.PT101 | i18n — sidebar nav labels + calendar/booking-confirmation date formatters (closes user-reported `M/T/W` + `MAY 2026` + sidebar gaps) | SHIPPED | _to be filled by commit_ | User report mid-pass on B.PT91-100 verification: (1) the booking-form day strip's weekday narrows (`M T W ...`) stayed English on `oh_locale=es`, (2) the month-year banner (`MAY 2026`) stayed English, (3) the dashboard sidebar was wholly un-translated. **Root cause #1 — dates**: 7 calendar callsites + 3 in `booking-confirmation.tsx` used `Date.prototype.toLocaleDateString(undefined, …)` / `toLocaleTimeString(undefined, …)`. With `undefined` as the locale arg the JS runtime falls back to the BROWSER locale (Intl.DateTimeFormat()'s default), bypassing next-intl's `oh_locale` cookie entirely. Pre-B.PT101 the only locale-aware date callsite was the month-drawer's weekday-narrow row, which read explicit `t("weekdayNarrowSun")` etc — but the day-strip + the rest used the browser fallback. **Fix**: swap every site to `useFormatter().dateTime(date, { … })` from `next-intl`. The formatter respects the active locale (`getLocale()` server-side, the `NextIntlClientProvider locale=` prop client-side). Affected files: `day-strip.tsx`, `availability-drawer.tsx` (drops `fmtMonthYear` helper, computes label inside the component), `trigger-card.tsx` (drops `fmtDate`/`fmtTime` helpers, computes inside component), `booking-drawer.tsx` (drops `fmtSlot` helper, computes inside component), `booking-form.tsx` (refactors `fmtSlot` to take a `format` arg — used in TWO sub-forms so kept as a helper), `day-slots.tsx` (formatter on `SlotChip`'s timeLabel), `booking-confirmation.tsx` (refactors `fmtTime` to take a `format` arg). **Root cause #2 — sidebar**: `src/components/oh/oh-app-sidebar.tsx` had ZERO `useTranslations` calls. Group labels (`LIBRARY`, `ACCOUNT`, `WORKSPACE`, `DANGER`) and item labels (`Bookings`, `Availability`, `Profile`, `Workspaces`, `Back to app`, `General`, `Workflows`, `Calendars`, `Developer`, `Billing`, `Delete account`) were hardcoded English literals embedded in `MAIN_NAV_GROUPS` / `SETTINGS_NAV_GROUPS` constants in `src/lib/brutalist.ts`. Plus the `<nav aria-label="Main">` and the collapse-trigger's `aria-label={state === "expanded" ? "Collapse sidebar" : "Expand sidebar"}` — three aria-labels never translated. **Fix**: refactor `brutalist.ts`'s `NavItem` / `NavGroup` types to carry `labelKey: string` instead of `label: string`. Renderer (`oh-app-sidebar.tsx`) resolves keys against `useTranslations("Sidebar")` for both desktop `<NavGroupRender>` AND mobile `<MobileNavContent />` paths, plus translated nav-aria + collapse-trigger aria-label. Translation keys mirror the lowercased item / group identifiers. **New `Sidebar` namespace** (en + es): `mainNavAria`, `collapseSidebar`, `expandSidebar`, group labels (`library`, `account`, `workspace`, `danger`), item labels (`bookings`, `availability`, `profile`, `workspaces`, `backToApp`, `general`, `workflows`, `calendars`, `developer`, `billing`, `deleteAccount`). **New `BookingConfirmation` namespace** (en + es): the visitor receipt at `/h/<handle>/booked/<uid>` was riddled with hardcoded English (`Booked`, `with {host}`, `Add to calendar`, `Share` / `Shared` / `Copied`, `Reschedule`, the confirm dialog title/desc/cta/cancel, `Receipt {stamp}`, `LOCAL TIME`, `Office hours with {host}`, `Reference {ref}`, header home aria) — all routed through `useTranslations("BookingConfirmation")`. Brand line `Officehours` left as-is (allow-listed via the existing brand pattern in `i18n-coverage.test.ts`). `getTimeZoneLabel()` refactored to take the locale-aware fallback string as an arg. **Test ratchet**: `i18n-coverage.test.ts` `CLEAN_FILES` extended with `src/components/oh/oh-app-sidebar.tsx` + `src/app/h/[handle]/booked/[bookingUid]/booking-confirmation.tsx` so any future hardcoded English in either file fails CI. **Files**: `messages/en.json` (+38 keys), `messages/es.json` (mirror), `src/lib/brutalist.ts` (label → labelKey), `src/components/oh/oh-app-sidebar.tsx` (3× useTranslations), 6 calendar `.tsx` files, `src/app/h/[handle]/booked/[bookingUid]/booking-confirmation.tsx`, `src/lib/__tests__/i18n-coverage.test.ts` (+2 CLEAN_FILES). **Verification**: `pnpm tsc --noEmit` clean; `pnpm lint` 0 errors (warnings unchanged at 7); `pnpm test:run` 422 pass / 5 pre-existing failures (workspaces-invitations + webhooks — verified via `git stash` replay, identical failures on prior HEAD, unrelated to this commit's surface — flagged as B.PT43-line follow-up); `pnpm test:run src/lib/__tests__/i18n-coverage.test.ts` clean (the two new CLEAN_FILES contracts pass). Playwright: hydration-stress on `/bookings` flagged a base-ui `useId` mismatch on the Tabs `tabpanel` — this is a known third-party id-counter drift (B.PT48-50 family), already documented in `playwright.config.ts`'s `workers: 1` justification, NOT introduced by this commit (verified via stash replay — same auth-setup-flaky behavior). **Defers**: none. The QA-2 follow-on rows from B.PT96 (B.PT97 calendar.set-selected optimism, B.PT98 destructive toast-undo, B.PT99 role-flip optimism) remain queued. |

| B.PT96 | Optimistic UI audit + workflow toggle/delete reference impls (closes QA-2 from `docs/qa-pass-2026-05-03.md`) | SHIPPED | 3061eb3 | QA-2: user loved B.PT84's workspace-switcher optimism (trigger label flips before cookie write resolves), asked where else in the app the same pattern would benefit + which sibling behaviors related to optimism we could ship as polish. Approach per qa-pass acceptance: (1) full audit doc covering every mutation hook, (2) 1-2 highest-ROI reference implementations to validate the pattern. **New `docs/optimistic-ui-audit.md`**: walks all 35 hooks under `src/lib/mutations/`, classifies each by optimism candidacy (NAVIGATION → no value; FORM SAVE → low value; CREATE → medium; TOGGLE → HIGH; DELETE → HIGH with toast-undo extension; SPECIAL → defer). For HIGH-priority items, ranks them into 4 implementation waves with concrete `B.PT##` reservation. Documents 4 distinct optimism patterns at our disposal (`useTransition`, React Query `onMutate`+`setQueryData`, React 19 `useOptimistic`, toast-undo) with a trade-off table picking which fits which surface shape. Documents 4 sibling-pattern behaviors (skeleton loading, optimistic navigation, useOptimistic for Server Actions, SWR + background prefetch) the user would feel even without per-mutation optimism. Documents 5 anti-patterns (don't optimistically render irreversible operations, etc) with reasoning. Closes with a "what the user will feel" cumulative-impact summary across all 4 waves. **Reference impls** (this commit, validating Tanstack canonical shape): `use-update-workflow.ts` (workflow.active toggle — Switch in `/settings → workflows` flips instantly) + `use-delete-workflow.ts` (workflow row removal — list.filter immediately). Both use the canonical `onMutate` (cancel + snapshot + setQueryData patch) → `onError` (rollback + toast) → `onSuccess` (delegate to caller) → no `onSettled` (the project's `src/trpc/hooks.ts` ships a global `invalidateQueries()` override that reconciles post-success — verified by reading the hooks.ts source; no need to duplicate). Type safety via `inferRouterOutputs<AppRouter>["workflows"]["list"]` rather than hand-rolled shapes — survives schema changes automatically. **Why no caller-onMutate forwarding**: `.claude/rules/mutation-hooks.md` requires onSuccess + onError forwarding only; none of the 34 sibling hooks expose onMutate to callers, so the optimism context shape stays private to each hook file (avoids type-incompatibility burden + matches existing conventions). **Why no test surface in this commit**: testing optimistic mutation rollback in isolation requires a full QueryClient + tRPC mock setup that doesn't exist for any other hook in the project; would 5x the diff. The audit doc explicitly notes "test the rollback path on the next wave once the toast-undo pattern lands and the QueryClient mock fixture is justified by 5+ consumers." **Files**: `docs/optimistic-ui-audit.md` (NEW, 200+ lines), `src/lib/mutations/use-update-workflow.ts` (rewrite to canonical optimistic shape), `src/lib/mutations/use-delete-workflow.ts` (same shape, list-removal patch). **Verification**: tsc clean, lint 0 errors (warnings unchanged), full vitest 413 passed / 0 failed / 2 skipped (matches B.PT93 baseline; no regression). UI smoke skipped — pattern is well-documented Tanstack canon, no consumer-side changes (workflow-fields.tsx already calls these hooks unchanged), the rollback path fires only on server error which can't be reproduced reliably in dev. **Defers** to follow-up B.PT## rows per the audit doc's 4-wave plan: B.PT97 (calendar.set-selected — biggest per-line win, 10+ checkboxes per render), B.PT98 (destructive list operations with toast-undo: cancel-booking, revoke-api-key, revoke-invitation, remove-member), B.PT99 (role-flip optimism: update-invitation-role + set-member-role). |
| B.PT95 | UI — visible disabled state for menu items mid-async-op + closes QA-4 from `docs/qa-pass-2026-05-03.md` (sub-A fix; sub-B WORKING-AS-DESIGNED) | SHIPPED | abf18aa | QA-4 sub-A — user reported they "don't really see anything that behaves like" the disabled menu rows promised by B.PT84's workspace-switcher polish. **Diagnosis** (5-line code grep + CSS audit): `.oh-menu-item` selector in `src/app/globals.css` had ZERO `[data-disabled]` styling. Base UI's `<Menu.Item disabled>` correctly sets the `[data-disabled]` attribute on the rendered element AND blocks interaction (per its ARIA spec implementation), but the project's CSS shipped no visual treatment for that attribute — so the prop was a no-op visually even though it functioned correctly under the hood. **Sub-A fix**: add three small CSS rules to `.oh-menu-item`. (1) `[data-disabled]:not([data-pending])` → `opacity: 0.55` (matches `--oh-content-muted` tier per the project's muted-text scheme), `cursor: wait` (in-progress signal), `pointer-events: none` (defensive — blocks any keyboard-focus hover that slips past Base UI's interaction guard). (2) `[data-disabled]:not([data-pending])[data-highlighted]` → `background: transparent` (kills the highlight bg-swap on disabled rows so a stale focus state from before the disable flip doesn't read as "still hoverable"). (3) `[data-pending]` → `cursor: wait` only — the picked row keeps full opacity because it carries its own spinner glyph (`Loader2`) and dimming it would compete with the spinner for the user's attention. **Sub-B — alt loading patterns brainstorm**: per the user's own note ("I honestly like the approach of right now... what's implemented might be enough"), no additional loading affordance ships. Considered + rejected: full-screen overlay (kills visual continuity — the user EXPLICITLY wants to stay on the current page during the switch), content-cutout / skeleton swap (implies "loading from scratch" which is wrong; data is REPLACING in place). The shipped chrome stack from B.PT84 (optimistic label + spinner glyph + top hairline progress bar + now the visible disabled-row dim from this commit) is the complete UX vocabulary. Closing sub-B as WORKING-AS-DESIGNED with this reasoning so the question doesn't recur in a future pass. **Files**: `src/app/globals.css` (one CSS block, ~16 lines including the doc comment). **Verification**: tsc clean, lint 0 errors. CSS-only change — no test surface affected, full vitest baseline holds (413/0/2). UI smoke skipped intentionally — the change is purely additive CSS with selectors that match exactly the data-attributes Base UI already emits (verified by static grep against `oh-dashboard-bar.tsx` lines 191-218 where `disabled={isPending}` + `data-pending={isPicked}` are wired). **QA-4 status in qa-pass doc**: FIXED with B.PT95. |

Sub-B (coverage tooling) was B.PT94. **What's NOT in this commit** but the user implied in their note ("most of the things aren't translated like the titles of the pages and the dashboard titles either"): we covered every `OhPageHeader` callsite + the two custom-shaped page-level files (`error.tsx` + `availability-form.tsx`). If a future audit surfaces a section header / dialog title / button label still in English that wasn't on the OhPageHeader path, the i18n-coverage test from B.PT94 catches it on the next CLEAN_DIRS expansion. |
| B.PT94 | i18n coverage vitest guard — fail on hardcoded English in localized dirs (resolves QA-5 sub-B from `docs/qa-pass-2026-05-03.md`) | SHIPPED | 8f82121 | QA-5 sub-B coverage tooling — user explicitly asked for "a tool that finds every untranslated user-visible string in the app at pnpm test:run time, so the next time someone adds a new screen they can't ship a hardcoded English label without CI noticing." Manual hunt-and-peck (the prior workflow) doesn't scale — every QA pass surfaces a few new ones, and B.PT26's first sweep missed an entire family of surfaces (visitor calendar, auth forms, page titles) that B.PT91/B.PT92 had to clean up reactively. **Approach decision** (researched options in qa-pass doc): chose **regex-based, line-scoped, narrowly-targeted to clean dirs**. Rejected: (a) AST-based via @babel/parser — heavier dep + slower + most JSX text node detection works fine on regex for the project's Prettier-formatted source; (b) Playwright runtime crawl in en + es — heaviest infra + slowest + couples i18n coverage to live dev server; (c) walk WHOLE app — would surface hundreds of pre-existing violations from un-localized dirs (page titles + section legends across (host)/ — tracked separately as B.PT93), making the test a 500-line allow-list maintenance burden + losing signal in the noise. **Implementation**: new `src/lib/__tests__/i18n-coverage.test.ts`. Walks `*.tsx` files under `CLEAN_DIRS` (`src/components/calendar/` from B.PT91; `src/app/login/` + `src/app/register/` from B.PT26 + B.PT92; `src/app/(host)/availability/` from B.PT26 + B.PT26B + this commit's discovered fix). Three detection passes per file: (1) visible JSX attribute literals — `aria-label`, `title`, `placeholder`, `alt`, `aria-description` — anything in those attrs that's English text fails; (2) same-line JSX text nodes (`<span>Hello</span>`); (3) multi-line JSX text nodes — Prettier formats `<button>\n  Click me\n</button>` so the previous-line + next-line context check is needed (previous non-comment line ends with `>`, next non-comment line starts with `<`, candidate line is pure text). **Allow-list** (12 entries with justifications): brand names (GitHub / Google / Microsoft / Outlook / Stripe / Officehours / Resend), domain prefix chrome ("officehours.app/h/"), neutral name placeholder ("Alex"), pure punctuation / arrows / ellipsis, ALL-CAPS abbreviations of 1–3 chars (TZ, IATA), RFC 2606 example emails (`*@example.com`), numeric / format placeholders, locale codes, css/aria values (true/false/auto/etc). Adding a new allow-list entry is a design decision with a comment-justified `reason`. **Three self-tests** confirm the detector works: catches both attr + JSX-text violations, skips correctly-translated source (curly-brace expressions), respects the allow-list. **Per-clean-dir contract**: each dir under CLEAN_DIRS gets its own `it()` block so a violation in one dir doesn't mask the others' results. Failure surfaces a `file:line: snippet` listing PLUS suggested fix paths (wrap with t(), add to messages JSON, or extend ALLOW_LIST). **Discovery during build**: the test caught a real gap — `src/app/(host)/availability/components/availability-form.tsx:73` had `title="Hours"` plus inline "Weekly availability" legend, "The hours visitors can book from..." description, and Save/Saving/Saved labels. B.PT26B had localized the field-chrome inside `availability-fields.tsx` but missed the wrapper `availability-form.tsx`. Fixed in this commit (extended `Availability` namespace with 6 keys + threaded `useTranslations` through the form wrapper) — the test was the spec that drove the cleanup, exactly as intended. **Files**: `src/lib/__tests__/i18n-coverage.test.ts` (NEW, 290 LOC including allow-list + three detection passes), `src/app/(host)/availability/components/availability-form.tsx` (page wrapper localization caught by the test), `messages/en.json` + `messages/es.json` (6 new Availability keys: pageTitle, weeklyLegend, weeklyDescription, saveLabel, savingLabel, savedLabel). **Defers**: B.PT93 (page-title + section-legend sweep across (host)/ routes — adding those dirs to CLEAN_DIRS comes after their content is localized; this test reports them silent today). **Verification**: tsc clean, lint 0 errors, full vitest 411 passed (was 404; +7 new tests in this file) / 0 failed / 2 skipped. **Pattern reference**: `src/lib/__tests__/no-mid-dot.test.ts` (B.PT27), `i18n-no-duplicate-keys.test.ts` (B.PT90) — same shape, different invariant. Per the edge-case-testing playbook (`docs/edge-case-testing.md`) this is Pattern 2 (file-walk vitest contract). |
| B.PT92 | i18n — auth form sub-components (credentials, magic-link, register) localized via existing `Auth` namespace (resolves QA-5 sub-A auth-form portion from `docs/qa-pass-2026-05-03.md`) | SHIPPED | 9bd32b2 | QA-5 sub-A auth-form portion. B.PT26 translated the `/login` and `/register` PAGE shells (titles, subtitles, GitHub button, link-to-other-page, next-auth ?error= messages) but the FORM sub-components (`credentials-form.tsx`, `magic-link-form.tsx`, `register-form.tsx`) still rendered hardcoded English — the user's report called this out specifically: "things like the 'name' or 'email' things in the buttons like titles and stuff don't work." All three forms had ZERO `useTranslations` calls. **Approach**: extend the existing `Auth` namespace (NOT a new namespace — these are the same auth surface as B.PT26's page-shell strings, and grouping under one namespace keeps the locale catalog coherent). 32 new keys covering: shared field labels (Email / Password / Handle) + placeholders (different per surface — login uses "you@example.com", register uses "you@domain.com" + "8+ characters"), shared show/hide-password aria, login submit pending+default, register submit pending+default, magic-link label + submit pending+default + error + sentTo confirmation (with `{email}` ICU param via `t.rich` for the bolded email span) + use-different reset, register handle-availability badge states (free/taken/3+/!), register handle-help copy variants (default/taken/invalid/error/available). **Files**: `src/app/login/credentials-form.tsx`, `src/app/login/magic-link-form.tsx`, `src/app/register/register-form.tsx`, `messages/en.json`, `messages/es.json`. **Why `t.rich` for `magicLinkSentTo`**: the success message embeds the user's email in a styled `<span>`. ICU plain `t({email})` can't preserve the JSX styling, so `t.rich` with a function-returning-JSX renderer is the canonical next-intl pattern (verified via Context7 `/next-intl/docs` mid-2026). Spec: `t.rich("magicLinkSentTo", { email: () => <span>...</span> })` — translator catalog stores `"Check your email — link sent to {email}"`, the span wrapping is applied at render time. **What this commit does NOT cover**: page titles + section legends across `(host)/` routes (Defers: B.PT93 — separate sweep, separate row), i18n-coverage contract test (Defers: B.PT94 — QA-5 sub-B). **Verification**: tsc clean, lint 0 errors, full vitest 404 passed / 0 failed / 2 skipped (matches B.PT91 baseline; no regression). The B.PT90 i18n-no-duplicate-keys guard confirms `Auth` namespace remains a single block in both locale files. **QA-5 sub-A status in qa-pass doc**: still IN_PROGRESS — booking flow (B.PT91) + auth forms (B.PT92) done, page-titles (B.PT93) pending. |
| B.PT91 | i18n — visitor booking surface (Pick a date, slot picker, booking form, month drawer) localized via new `BookingCalendar` namespace (resolves QA-5 sub-A booking-flow portion from `docs/qa-pass-2026-05-03.md`) | SHIPPED | 6a587c4 | QA-5 sub-A from the 2026-05-03 walkthrough: `/h/<handle>` "Pick a date" trigger + booking form + slot picker were all hardcoded English regardless of `oh_locale`.
