Each component picks up `useTranslations("BookingCalendar")` at the top of its render scope; ICU param substitution used for date / time / count interpolation (e.g. `t("triggerFullAria", { date, time })`). Date / time formatters (`toLocaleDateString(undefined, ...)`) intentionally NOT swapped to next-intl `useFormatter` in this commit — they pick up the browser locale which is acceptable for date/time formatting (separate concern from translation; can be revisited in a future row if a real bug surfaces). **What this commit does NOT cover** (deferred to separate B.PT## rows per the qa-pass doc's "three concrete sweeps" guidance): /login + /register form sub-component labels (Defers: B.PT92), page-title + section-legend sweep across `(host)/` routes (Defers: B.PT93), i18n-coverage contract test that catches future gaps automatically (Defers: B.PT94 — QA-5 sub-B). **Verification**: tsc clean, lint 0 errors (warnings unchanged), full vitest 404 passed / 0 failed / 2 skipped (the 5 pre-existing flakes from main pass cleanly here because the worktree's dev.db has zero contention with a parent dev:full server). **i18n-no-duplicate-keys guard from B.PT90 confirms no top-level namespace collision** — `BookingCalendar` is a fresh namespace, distinct from the existing `Calendar` (settings) one. **QA-5 sub-A status in qa-pass doc**: partially flipped — booking-flow portion FIXED, login + page-titles tracked separately. |
| B.PT90 | i18n vitest guard — fail on duplicate top-level namespace keys in `messages/*.json` | SHIPPED | 958522e | B.PT26 hit a real bug that this guard would have caught at `pnpm test:run` time: I appended a fresh `Invitations` block (only `skeletonEyebrow`) at EOF of `messages/en.json` + `messages/es.json` without noticing the canonical `Invitations` block already higher up in each file. JSON parsers (V8 included, per ECMA-404 §6) keep the LAST value when a key is defined twice — so my smaller later-defined block silently shadowed the original 20-key namespace. Symptom surfaced manually a few sessions later: `MISSING_MESSAGE: Could not resolve 'Invitations.notNow' in messages for locale 'en'` on `/invitations/<token>`. All 20 keys (eyebrow, titleFor, body, accept, accepting, signInToAccept, openWorkspace, expiredHint, status_*, role_*, notNow, etc.) silently missing the moment B.PT26 landed. Fix shipped earlier in `04b1eeb` (merged the two blocks); this commit adds the guard so the bug class fails CI in the future. **Implementation**: new `src/lib/__tests__/i18n-no-duplicate-keys.test.ts` walks `messages/*.json` and scans top-level keys via regex on each line (`^ {2}"([^"]+)":` — Prettier-formatted 2-space indent reliably catches top-level keys without false positives from nested keys at 4-space indent or string contents). For each file: counts occurrences per key, fails with `messages/<file>: "<key>" at lines <l1>, <l2>` listing if any key appears >1 time. Adds a sanity test ("messages/ contains at least one *.json locale file") so a renamed/moved messages dir fails loudly instead of silently passing on zero iterations. Adds a self-test ("detector catches a planted duplicate in synthetic input") so a regex regression that always returns [] doesn't silently pass every catalog file — would've caught my own assumption-flaw mistake from the B.PT26B property test in seconds. **Pattern reference**: `src/lib/__tests__/no-mid-dot.test.ts` (B.PT27) — single focused invariant, file-walk style, surfaces violations with file:line listings. Same shape, different invariant. **Scope (what's NOT covered)**: nested-key duplicates within a namespace. Would catch `{"Settings":{"title":"a","title":"b"}}` requires real JSON parsing which the regex approach skips for simplicity. The B.PT26 regression was top-level; if a real nested-dup bug surfaces, expand the test then. Top-level coverage is the canonical i18n-namespace shape. **Files**: `src/lib/__tests__/i18n-no-duplicate-keys.test.ts` (NEW). **Verification**: tsc clean, lint 0 errors, isolated `pnpm test:run` of the new test + sibling no-mid-dot + i18n.test.ts → 13/13 pass (4 new from this file). **Honest gate note**: the FULL `pnpm test:run` was red at commit time due to environmental DB-lock contention — `pnpm dev:full` was running and held long-lived connections to `dev.db`, causing `prisma.bookingAudit.deleteMany({})` calls in test fixtures' `safeTearDownByHandle` to time out waiting for SQLite's write lock. The 36 failed test files all show "Operation has timed out" on fixture setup, NOT actual contract failures. This commit is purely additive (new file, no edits to existing code, the test reads filesystem JSON only — no DB), so the failures cannot be caused by it. To verify cleanly post-merge: stop `pnpm dev:full`, re-run `pnpm test:run`, expect the prior baseline of 5 pre-existing flakes (workspaces-invitations + webhooks.test.ts:195) + 4 new passing tests → ~399 passed total. |
| B.PT89 | Billing — Customer Portal `subscription_update_confirm` for plan switches + snapshot-compare success toast (Bug 1 + Bug 2) | SHIPPED | f86a0d6 | Two bugs surfaced during the 2026-05-02 billing flow audit. (Bug 3 — Cloudflare Access intercepting `/api/stripe/webhook` — is a Cloudflare Zero Trust policy issue outside the code; tracked in chat with three remediation paths for the operator to pick.) **Bug 1 — architecture**: `billing.startCheckout` always created a `stripe.checkout.sessions.create({ mode: "subscription" })`, even when an active subscription already existed on the workspace's customer. Stripe's canonical pattern for plan switches when a sub is active (`/billing/subscriptions/upgrade-downgrade`) is either `stripe.subscriptions.update` (direct API) or Customer Portal `subscription_update_confirm` flow (hosted page with proration preview + user explicit confirmation). Without that branch, every TEAM → PRO downgrade or PRO → TEAM upgrade minted a SECOND parallel subscription on the same Stripe customer, billing the user for both monthly. **Reference codebase**: dub `apps/web/app/api/workspaces/[idOrSlug]/billing/upgrade/route.ts:60-79` does the same branch with the same `flow_data` shape — verified via direct file read. cal.com routes orgs/teams URL-first so they don't have the same shape. **Fix shape**: `startCheckout` now reads `Subscription.stripeCustomerId` + `stripeSubscriptionId` from the local mirror; if both present, calls `stripe.subscriptions.retrieve` to confirm the sub is actually `active` or `trialing` in Stripe (defensive — webhook events can be missed if Cloudflare Access intercepts the endpoint, leaving the local mirror pointing at a phantom sub). On confirmed-active: mints a Customer Portal session with `flow_data: { type: "subscription_update_confirm", subscription_update_confirm: { subscription, items: [{ id, quantity: 1, price }] } }`. Falls through to Checkout Session when (a) no local mirror, (b) mirror has no Stripe customer/sub ids, or (c) `retrieve` returns canceled / missing — graceful recovery from drift. **Bug 2 — lying success toast**: `<CheckoutReturnSync>` checked `plan !== "FREE"` to declare success. For ANY downgrade-from-paid path (TEAM → PRO starts on TEAM, never goes through FREE) this evaluated true on poll #1 — the toast lied while the actual subscription state never updated. **Fix shape**: snapshot the cached `plan` value on `status="success"` entry into a `useRef`, read with `utils.billing.currentPlan.getData({ slug })` (no extra fetch — the cache was populated by the page's SSR prefetch). Polling success criterion is now `plan !== baseline` when baseline captured, falling back to the prior `plan !== "FREE"` only when no baseline (cold-cache hydration — defensive, preserves the FREE → paid happy path). Baseline cleared after the polling session resolves so subsequent visits get a fresh snapshot. **Tests** (2 new in `billing-procedures.test.ts`): "returns Customer Portal URL when an active subscription exists" — plants stripeCustomerId + stripeSubscriptionId, mocks `subscriptions.retrieve` to return active sub with items[0].id, asserts portal URL returned with correct `flow_data` shape, asserts checkoutCreate NOT called. "falls back to Checkout Session when retrieve says the sub is canceled" — same setup but retrieve returns `status: "canceled"`, asserts checkout URL returned and portalCreate NOT called. Stripe SDK mock extended with `subscriptions.retrieve` (was just `checkout.sessions.create` + `billingPortal.sessions.create`). **Files**: `src/trpc/routers/billing.ts` (branch in startCheckout), `src/app/(host)/settings/billing/components/billing-section.tsx` (CheckoutReturnSync baseline + comparator), `src/trpc/__tests__/billing-procedures.test.ts` (mock + 2 new tests). **No copy changes**: PlanCard already uses neutral `t("upgradeTo", { tier })` → "Switch to {tier}" when current is non-FREE non-target — works for both upgrade and downgrade directions verbatim. **Verification**: tsc clean, lint 0 errors (warnings unchanged), vitest 370 passed (was 368; +2 new) / 5 pre-existing flakes (orthogonal). End-to-end checkout UNTESTED in this commit because the user's Cloudflare Access policy still blocks `/api/stripe/webhook` — once they apply Path A/B/C from the chat, the full FREE → PRO + TEAM → PRO flow works against Stripe live. |
| B.PT88 | Dev tooling Phase 1 — log file aggregation across web/stripe/tunnel + `dev:full` orchestrator | SHIPPED | 8653ec5 | Goal: give Claude Code (and the human dev) one-call access to every running console without context-switching across 3-4 terminals. While debugging billing flows the agent kept asking the human to paste log output from terminals it couldn't see; this fixes that for ~15 minutes of work. **Decision matrix** (full architecture in `docs/dev-tooling-architecture.md`): Phase 1 = log files (this commit), Phase 2 = `StripeEvent.data` + `/admin/debug/billing/[slug]` (planned), Phase 3 = custom local stdio MCP server with `tail_log` / `recent_events` / `query_db` / `app_state` / `stripe_state` / `replay_stripe_event` tools (planned), Phase 4 = pino JSONL sink + `operationId` correlation (planned). **Considered + rejected**: Overmind/Foreman (requires `brew install`, `concurrently` covers 90%); MCP filesystem server (re-exposes `Read`/`Bash` capabilities the agent already has); MCP SQLite server (the future custom MCP scopes SQL to dev.db only + readonly + paired with Stripe state queries in one call — tighter contract); OpenTelemetry/Tempo (overkill for single-host dev). **Files**: `scripts/dev-logged.sh`, `scripts/stripe-logged.sh`, `scripts/tunnel-logged.sh` — each runs the existing command via `script -q /dev/null` (preserves line buffering when output is piped) and `tee`s into `logs/<service>.log`. `script` invocation is the macOS-flavor form (`script -q FILE COMMAND`); contributors on Linux need the `-qfc` form. **package.json**: new scripts `dev:logged`, `stripe:logged`, `tunnel:logged` (per-service wrappers); `dev:full` (concurrently runs all three with prefixed colored output + `--kill-others-on-fail` for fail-loud); `logs:tail` (tail -F all three live); `logs:clear` (rm -f logs/*.log between sessions). `concurrently@9.2.1` added as devDep. **.gitignore**: `/logs/` excluded — local artifact, never committed. **Why per-service shell wrappers (not a single concurrently command)**: preserves the operator's existing terminal-per-service workflow. They can run wrappers individually OR `dev:full` for the all-in-one. Gives Claude Code `Read`-tool access without changing how the human works. **Operator workflow**: `pnpm dev:logged` (Next), `pnpm stripe:logged` (Stripe CLI), `pnpm tunnel:logged` (cloudflared) in three terminals — OR `pnpm dev:full` in one. `pnpm logs:tail` follows all three, `pnpm logs:clear` resets between debug sessions. **Defers: B.PT89** (Phase 2 — admin debug page + StripeEvent.data column + replay endpoint), **B.PT90** (Phase 3 — custom MCP server), **B.PT91** (Phase 4 — JSONL sink + correlation IDs). Verification: tsc clean, lint 0 errors, all three scripts pass `bash -n` syntax check, logs/ dir creates on first run + tee writes work. |
| B.PT87 | Dev checklist — Recent + All features tabs in the FAB modal | SHIPPED | 25086eb | The dev-checklist FAB (B.PT75) renders `docs/features-and-tests.md` as interactive checkboxes — useful but the doc is 700+ lines and hides "what should I check today" inside the full catalog. After the 2026-05-02 QA pass + B.PT26 i18n sweep landed (8 commits, mix of UI + tRPC + i18n + tests), the user asked for a per-batch verification surface that lives next to the canonical doc, not buried in it. **Solution**: two tabs in the same FAB modal. **Tab 1 — Recent** (`docs/recent-changes-checklist.md`, NEW): short-form GFM task-list scoped to the just-shipped batch. Part A is UI-walkthrough (numbered, "open this URL → see this affordance"); Part B is non-UI (`pnpm test:run <file>` recipes). Default tab when both files are present so opening the FAB lands on the freshest list. **Tab 2 — All features** (`docs/features-and-tests.md`, unchanged): the long-form catalog. **Files**: `dev-checklist-mount.tsx` reads BOTH markdown files via `safeRead()` (returns null when a file is missing — different worktree / partial clone — so the launcher gracefully degrades to the available tab). `dev-checklist-launcher.tsx` renders the `Tabs` primitive with `variant="line"` to match the dashboard's segmented-control vocabulary; only mounts the tab UI when BOTH files are present (single-tab fallback when only one resolves). `dev-checklist-content.tsx` accepts a `storageScope` prop and parameterizes both the checked-state and scroll-position localStorage keys (`oh-dev-checklist[-recent]-v1`, `oh-dev-checklist[-recent]-scroll-v1`). **Per-tab storage isolation**: without it, the "Reset all" button on the Recent tab would wipe accumulated progress on the All-features tab; scrolling one tab would re-position the other on next open. The default (unscoped) keys preserve existing user state on the All-features tab so the introduction of tabs doesn't reset prior saved progress. **Why `variant="line"`**: matches the existing booking-list segmented-control vocabulary (per-tab underline, transparent bg) — quieter than the default solid-bg variant, fits inside a modal without competing with its frame. Also tested at the chisel-mode polished bar — pure-CSS `data-active:after:opacity-100` underline transition, no GSAP. **Files touched**: `src/components/oh/dev-checklist-mount.tsx` (read both files, pass both as props), `src/components/oh/dev-checklist-launcher.tsx` (Tabs wrapper, default-to-Recent fallback, single-tab graceful degrade), `src/components/oh/dev-checklist-content.tsx` (storageScope prop, key derivation), `docs/recent-changes-checklist.md` (NEW; covers QA-1..QA-7 + B.PT26 verification). **Verification**: tsc clean, lint 0 errors (warnings unchanged), authed Playwright hydration 6/6 (`/bookings`, `/availability`, `/profile`, `/settings/general`, `/settings/billing` — all routes that mount the FAB). UI smoke from chat: not run (existing dev server stale-lock state); the change is purely additive in the modal body and uses already-tested primitives, but the user should verify the FAB → Recent tab → first-paint default + per-tab scroll preservation manually. |
| B.PT86 | Edge-case discovery playbook — fast-check + Vitest property tests, Playwright invariant flows, mutation-testing decision (resolves QA-7 from `docs/qa-pass-2026-05-02.md`) | SHIPPED | 407fa12 | QA-7: across QA-1 through QA-6 the user observed that manual happy-path testing surfaces obvious issues but misses edge cases (race conditions, post-delete orphans, post-rename URL stability, cache-invalidation timing). Wanted tooling + methodology to find these systematically. **Decision matrix evaluated**: (1) **Property-based testing (`fast-check` + `@fast-check/vitest`)** — best ROI for procedure-heavy contract tests; turns "for any input X, P(X) holds" claims into runnable contracts. ADOPTED. (2) **Cross-cutting Playwright invariant flows** — right shape for "after multi-step user action A, invariant Q holds" claims (e.g. workspace-rename URL stability, post-delete member-list cleanup). DOCUMENTED — already shipped one example via B.PT85 (`embed.spec.ts`); pattern formalized in the playbook for future invariants tagged `@invariant`. (3) **Mutation testing (Stryker)** — DEFERRED with a documented re-evaluation trigger (revisit when project has >500 contract tests AND a recent subtle behavior can't be traced to a specific test asserting it; Stryker's per-mutation re-run cost on the SQLite-backed contract layer is ~30-60s × N mutations, doesn't land yet given the existing suite's catch ratio). Cal.com + dub were both audited: cal has property-based tests under `packages/features/bookings/lib/__tests__/` for date math, dub doesn't use property-based testing at all (their invariants are simpler — slug → URL is tabular). **Reference test shipped**: `src/trpc/__tests__/bookings-create.property.test.ts` extends the canonical idempotency contract from "works for N=1, 2, 3" (the existing example-based tests) to "for any v4 UUID K and any concurrent-submit count N in [2, 8], all N submits return the same publicUid AND exactly one Booking row exists." If the contract ever regresses (e.g. someone moves the inside-tx idempotency lookup outside the transaction — the `c2fe653` change reverted), fast-check shrinks to the minimum N + key shape that exposes the race. Run cost: 25 iterations × N submits + per-iteration DB wipe = ~1s wall time. **Generator choices** (documented inline + in the playbook): `fc.uuid({ version: 4 })` matches `crypto.randomUUID()` runtime shape; `fc.integer({ min: 2, max: 8 })` bounded against the rate limiter (10/min/IP); visitor email re-randomized per iteration so dedup logic doesn't leak between runs. **Playbook**: new `docs/edge-case-testing.md` documents (a) the three-pattern decision matrix, (b) `numRuns` guidance + when to lower (never below 25 — below that you're rolling a 2d8 not testing), (c) anti-patterns (fc.anything() without bounds, multi-invariant property bodies, treating Playwright invariants as unit-test substitutes), (d) tooling status (`fast-check@4.7.0` + `@fast-check/vitest@0.4.1` are devDeps; Stryker NOT installed; Vitest 4.1.5 + Node 22 are tested combinations), (e) the canonical add-a-new-edge-case workflow (open `qa-pass-*.md`, identify the OPEN row, pick a pattern from the matrix, author + commit). **QA-7 status**: flipped to FIXED in `docs/qa-pass-2026-05-02.md` with this commit's SHA. **Verification**: tsc clean, lint 0 errors, vitest 368 passed (was 367; +1 new property test) / 5 failed (same pre-existing flakes — orthogonal). The playbook itself has no test surface; the reference test is the contract. |
| B.PT85 | Embed loader end-to-end smoke + proxy hole-fix for anonymous embed traffic (resolves QA-5 from `docs/qa-pass-2026-05-02.md`) | SHIPPED | 1dc5015 | QA-5: user only opened `/embed/<handle>` directly under an authed session; the anonymous third-party-embed scenario (`<script src="/embed.js" defer>` injecting an iframe into a guest's browser) had never been exercised end-to-end. Audit surfaced **two underlying bugs the manual smoke would have hit**: (1) **`src/proxy.ts` was redirecting `/embed.js` AND `/embed/<handle>` to `/login` for unauthenticated traffic**. The matcher correctly excluded `api`, `_next/static`, `_next/image`, `favicon.ico` — but `embed` was missing from `isPublic`, so anonymous visitors got 302'd to login the moment the loader's iframe tried to load. The whole feature was unreachable for the canonical use case. Cal.com handles the same shape by putting embed routes outside the auth gate (their matcher omits `/embed.*` outright); the equivalent here is adding `pathname === "/embed.js" || pathname.startsWith("/embed/")` to the proxy's `isPublic` clause so the existing attribution-cookie + matcher logic still gets to inspect the route. (2) **`public/embed.js` had a JS-prop / DOM-attribute mismatch**: `mount()` set `host.__ohMounted = true` (JS property) but `mountAll()`'s selector filtered with `:not([data-oh-mounted])` (DOM attribute). The JS-prop guard at the top of `mount()` caught the actual race, so no double-mount happened in practice — but the selector was a no-op pretending to do filtering it didn't. Fixed by also setting the DOM attribute (`host.setAttribute("data-oh-mounted", "true")`) so both layers agree. **Validation tooling (the heart of QA-5)**: built a self-contained tester at `e2e/fixtures/embed-test.html` that loads `embed.js` from a configurable origin, mounts an iframe into a host div, and prints every `originator: "OH"` postMessage to a visible log panel. Two consumption modes: (a) manual smoke — the user opens the file directly, edits the inputs, sees the iframe + the live message stream + the booking surface; (b) automated — `e2e/embed.spec.ts` (NEW Playwright spec, public project) drives the same fixture via `file://` + URL params, asserts the iframe mounts (`iframe[data-oh-handle]` visible), polls for at least one `size` postMessage to land in `window.__OH_TEST_MESSAGES`, drills into the iframe via `frameLocator()` and asserts the booking surface ("Pick a date" trigger button) is visible. **Why `file://` + cross-origin to localhost:3000**: that IS the canonical embed scenario — third-party site at one origin, iframe at another. Verifying postMessage works cross-origin is part of what "the embed works" means. **Why no `frameLocator` booking-flow walkthrough**: the booking-flow contract is already covered by `booking-flow.spec.ts` against the visitor route directly. The embed spec's job is to prove the iframe + postMessage + booking-surface presence, not re-test the booking transaction. **playwright.config.ts**: extended the `public` project's `testMatch` regex from `(?:hydration|booking-flow)\.spec\.ts` to `(?:hydration|booking-flow|embed)\.spec\.ts`. **Files**: `src/proxy.ts` (add `isEmbed` to `isPublic`), `public/embed.js` (set DOM attribute alongside JS prop), `e2e/fixtures/embed-test.html` (NEW manual + automated tester), `e2e/embed.spec.ts` (NEW Playwright spec), `playwright.config.ts` (regex extension). **QA-5 status**: flipped to FIXED in `docs/qa-pass-2026-05-02.md` with this commit's SHA. **Verification**: tsc clean, lint 0 errors, vitest 367 passed / 5 failed (same pre-existing flakes — orthogonal), Playwright 24/24 (was 23; +1 new embed spec, no regressions in hydration/booking-flow/authed). |
| B.PT84 | Workspace switcher pending UI — optimistic label + spinner glyph + top progress bar (resolves QA-3 from `docs/qa-pass-2026-05-02.md`) | SHIPPED | 96d6687 | QA-3: clicking a workspace in the topbar dropdown changed the active workspace, but the page kept showing the OLD workspace's data for ~1s while broad invalidation + `router.refresh()` resolved. No "switch in progress" affordance — read as the data being briefly *wrong* rather than briefly *loading*. Audited reference codebases: dub puts the workspace slug IN the URL (`/[slug]/links`) so a switch is just a `<Link>` navigation — Next handles the route transition + the data is SWR-cached per-slug, so they never see stale-prior-workspace data. Their only switcher polish is a 75ms transition class on the trigger button itself (`workspace-dropdown.tsx:96`) and a guard against the slug going empty mid-nav animation (line 31). Cal.com routes orgs/teams URL-first (`/team/<slug>/...`) so they don't have the same shape — no first-class workspace switcher polish to lift. Lab is hybrid: workspace is COOKIE-keyed (B.PT6, `oh_active_workspace`), not URL-keyed, so a switch can't piggyback on Next's route transition — there's no route transition. The cookie change → `utils.invalidate()` → `router.refresh()` chain is what creates the perceived choppiness. **Decision**: keep the cookie-keyed architecture (the alternative — slug-keying every URL — would touch hundreds of links / fetches / notFound checks and is the QA-1-via-IDs problem), but fix the perception with React 19's `useTransition` + a top-progress affordance. **Implementation**: (1) Capture `isPending` from the existing `useTransition` (line 60 — was discarded with `[, startTransition]`). Track `pendingSlug` alongside isPending so the trigger label can render OPTIMISTICALLY — the moment the user clicks, the trigger shows the picked workspace's name, not the still-active one. Optimism is safe because the picked row came from the membership-gated dropdown — server-side rejection in `setActiveWorkspace` is the only failure mode and it surfaces as a toast with the pendingSlug clearing in the `finally`. (2) Trigger spinner: replace the `ChevronDown` glyph with `Loader2` (lucide, animate-spin) while pending. `aria-busy` + `data-pending` for AT users + CSS hooks. (3) New `<OhTopProgressBar visible={isPending} />` rendered as the LAST child of the dashboard bar — fixed-position 2px bar at the very top of the viewport (z-index 60, above the dashboard bar's 10), with an indeterminate shimmer keyframe (`oh-progress-shimmer`, 1.4s ease-in-out infinite, scaleX(0.4)→0.6→0.4 traveling left→right). Pure presentational, mounts only when visible. `role="progressbar" aria-live="polite" aria-label="Switching workspace"` — screen readers hear it. Reduced-motion mode parks the bar at scaleX(0.5) opacity 0.55 instead of animating. (4) Menu items get `disabled={isPending}` (block re-entry — without this, stacked picks race-condition the optimistic label) + the picked row gets a tiny `Loader2` glyph in its check-position so the user sees which row "took" the click. (5) Early-return when picking the already-active workspace (the click did nothing meaningful — without the guard, the bar would flash for ~50ms). **Why a top progress bar and not an overlay or skeleton**: an overlay over the content destroys visual continuity (the user just confirmed they want to STAY on this page, the data is what's in flight, not the shell), and a skeleton would imply "loading from scratch" which is also wrong. The bar mirrors the convention every dashboard product (vercel, dub-via-nprogress, github, linear) trained users to read as "page is doing async work in the background." **Why no test**: the affordance is a transition state — `isPending` flickers in <100ms in unit tests without a fake delay, and the e2e `workers=1` cost doesn't justify a fragile visual-timing assertion. The transition itself (cookie write → invalidate → refresh) is already covered by `active-workspace.test.ts` + the broad-invalidation contract in `B.PT17`. **Files**: `src/components/oh/oh-dashboard-bar.tsx` (wire isPending + pendingSlug + optimistic label + Loader2 glyph + render OhTopProgressBar), `src/components/oh/oh-top-progress-bar.tsx` (new presentational), `src/app/globals.css` (add `.oh-progress-bar` rule + `oh-progress-shimmer` keyframe + reduced-motion fallback). **QA-3 status**: flipped to FIXED in `docs/qa-pass-2026-05-02.md` with this commit's SHA. **Verification**: tsc clean, lint 0 errors (warnings unchanged), vitest 367 passed / 5 failed (same pre-existing `workspaces-invitations` + `webhooks.test.ts:195` flakes — orthogonal). UI smoke: dev server already running on port 3000 with HMR — Next's per-route compile would have logged any compile error in the dev log; the change is purely additive (new presentational component + state hooks; existing logic unchanged). |
| B.PT83 | Explicit cascade cleanup on `users.deleteAccount` (libsql FK quirk; resolves QA-6 from `docs/qa-pass-2026-05-02.md`) | SHIPPED | 5a64d0f | QA-6: a user deleted their own account via `users.deleteAccount`; the workspace-owner of a separate workspace where the deleted user was a MEMBER (not owner) still saw the deleted user lingering in `listMembers`. Diagnosis: schema cascade is configured correctly (`Membership.user @relation(... onDelete: Cascade)`), but `@prisma/adapter-libsql` doesn't reliably honor SQLite FK cascades — explicitly noted in `.claude/rules/testing.md` *Seed requirements* + `e2e/seed-test-user.ts:10-15` ("`@prisma/adapter-libsql` doesn't always honor SQLite FK cascades reliably — explicit `deleteMany` is the only reliable cleanup"). The procedure trusted cascades and got bitten. **Fix**: wrapped the deletion in `prisma.$transaction` with explicit cleanup in dependency order. (1) `prisma.workspace.deleteMany({ where: { ownerId: ctx.user.id } })` — drops workspaces this user owns FIRST. The Workspace cascade picks up its own children (memberships, invitations, bookings, api keys, webhooks, audit, slug history, subscription); cascade reliability from Workspace down is more reliable than from User down because the level is shallower. (2) `prisma.membership.deleteMany({ where: { userId: ctx.user.id } })` — drops the user's Memberships in workspaces they DON'T own (the QA-6 visible bug). (3) `prisma.user.delete(...)` — remaining direct children (Account, Session, Authenticator, AvailabilityRange, EventTypeHost, UserFeatures, CalendarCredential, Booking-as-host) cascade from the User row; those have always worked because the typical access path runs through them in the moment, exposing any failure quickly. **Why not also explicit-deleteMany those tables**: scope creep without evidence. The user's bug was Membership; the listed cascades have working test coverage in the existing "cascades the user row + every Cascade relation" case. If a future bug surfaces an orphan in any of those tables, add it to the transaction then. **New test** (`src/trpc/__tests__/account-deletion.test.ts`): "removes the user from OTHER workspaces' member lists (B.PT83 — QA-6)" — provisions a second user owning a different workspace, makes the host a member of that workspace, runs `deleteAccount` on the host, asserts `Membership.findFirst({ workspaceId, userId: host.id })` returns null AND that the OTHER workspace itself survives (only the deleted user's owned workspaces should cascade away). Cleanup tears down the second user + their workspace. **Why use `prisma.membership.create` directly in the test instead of going through `workspaces.invite` + `accept`**: bypasses the multi-step invite/accept flow — we're testing membership cleanup, not the invite contract. Direct insert keeps the test focused. **QA-6 status**: flipped to FIXED in `docs/qa-pass-2026-05-02.md` with this commit's SHA. **Verification**: tsc clean, lint 0 errors, vitest 367 passed (was 366; +1 new pass) / 5 failed (same pre-existing flakes). |
| B.PT82 | Workspace slug-history aliases — back-stack URLs after rename redirect instead of 404 (resolves QA-1 from `docs/qa-pass-2026-05-02.md`) | SHIPPED | _to be filled by commit_ | QA-1: renaming a workspace from its detail page invalidated any back-stack browser entry holding the prior slug — pressing browser back returned the user to a 404. The post-rename `router.replace` in `general-section.tsx:67` only replaces the CURRENT history entry, not earlier ones. Audited reference codebases: cal.com sidesteps via stable IDs in URLs (`/event-types/${eventType.id}`); dub keeps slugs but doesn't surface a redirect for renamed slugs (the issue is real there too). Lab uses slugs everywhere — switching to IDs would touch every Link / fetch / notFound check. Alias-table approach is less invasive. **Schema**: new `WorkspaceSlugHistory` model with `oldSlug @unique` + cascade-delete. Migration `20260502195032_workspace_slug_history` is purely additive. **Procedure** (`workspaces.update`): wrapped slug update in `prisma.$transaction` so slug update + history write commit atomically. When `newSlug !== input.slug`: (1) delete any existing history rows whose `oldSlug == newSlug` (defensive — new owner takes precedence over prior tenant's redirect); (2) upsert a row keyed by `oldSlug = input.slug` mapping to this workspaceId. Upsert (not pure create) handles rotate-back path (rename A→B→A→B). **Helper** (`src/lib/workspaces-server.ts`): new `redirectIfAliasedSlug(slug, subPath)`. Checks `Workspace.findUnique({where: {slug}})` first — if current owner exists, early return (one query in the happy path). Otherwise checks `WorkspaceSlugHistory.findUnique({where: {oldSlug}})` — if found, calls Next's `redirect()` to the current slug's URL preserving the user's intended sub-path. Throws via `NEXT_REDIRECT` per Next.js App Router contract. **Pages wired** (3): `members`, `settings`, `event-types` `page.tsx` each call `await redirectIfAliasedSlug(slug, "<sub>")` at top. Cost: one extra DB query per workspace-scoped page load. **Why per-page wrapper, not middleware**: middleware-level resolution adds a network hop on every dashboard route regardless. The page-level wrapper is scoped to the three routes that take `[slug]`. **Why not layout-level**: there's no `[slug]/layout.tsx` and adding one for one query is heavier than calling a helper. **Tests** (4 new cases in `workspace-lifecycle.test.ts`): records the prior slug on rename; name-only update does NOT write a history row; rotating A→B→A→B keeps a single history row (upsert path); a different workspace claiming a former slug clears the prior history. **QA-1 status**: flipped to FIXED in `docs/qa-pass-2026-05-02.md` with this commit's SHA. **Verification**: tsc clean, lint 0 errors, vitest 366 passed (was 362; +4 new pass) / 5 failed (same pre-existing `workspaces-invitations` + `webhooks.test.ts:195` flakes). |
| B.PT81 | Skeleton `loading.tsx` for `/invitations/<token>` — remove hydration flash (resolves QA-2 from `docs/qa-pass-2026-05-02.md`) | SHIPPED | _to be filled by commit_ | QA-2 had two sub-issues: (a) suspected role mismatch (sent as ADMIN, displayed as MEMBER), (b) confirmed hydration flash on the invitation accept page. **Sub-issue (a) — role round-trip audit**: traced the path end-to-end. `<OhSelect {...roleField}>` in `invite-member-dialog.tsx:188-198` correctly forwards react-hook-form's controller field (native select w/ value+onChange spread); `inviteMany` procedure at `workspaces.ts:747` stores `inv.role` verbatim into `Invitation.role`; `invitations.preview` at `invitations.ts:15` selects `role: true`; UI renders `t("role_${role}")`. The path is correct end-to-end with no transformation. Most likely scenario: user left the default MEMBER instead of picking ADMIN (the form's `defaultRow()` in `invite-member-dialog.tsx:51-54` defaults to MEMBER). Documented; no code fix shipped — if user reproduces with a deliberate ADMIN selection that still arrives as MEMBER, file a separate item. **Sub-issue (b) — hydration flash, fix shipped**: confirmed cause. `/invitations/[token]/page.tsx` is async server, awaits `invitations.preview.fetch()` server-side. Before this commit there was no `loading.tsx` in the route. On a tunneled dev server with Turbopack, server-render TTFB is visibly long — without an explicit loading boundary, the user saw a blank/flash until the rendered HTML arrived. Other authenticated routes like `/bookings` ALSO server-render but the user already has the dashboard chrome painted from `(host)/layout.tsx`'s prefetch (B.PT41), so the swap inside the panel reads as instant. The invitation route is anonymous (no parent shell prefetched); the entire viewport is empty until SSR resolves. **Implementation**: added `src/app/invitations/[token]/loading.tsx` rendering a skeleton that mirrors the final layout one-for-one — same `min-h-screen bg-oh-bg` shell, same `max-w-md` column, same eyebrow + title + dl + button shape. `<SkeletonBar>` helper uses `animate-pulse` + `bg-[color:var(--oh-tint)]` (~6% ink, the project's already-defined quiet placeholder color so it doesn't compete with real chrome). Layout matches `components/invitation-accept.tsx` exactly so the swap to real content has zero CLS — same widths, same heights, same gaps. Pattern reference: cal.com + dub both render route-level `loading.tsx` for routes performing server fetches; this aligns the public invitation route with that convention. **Why a skeleton vs `<Suspense>` boundary inside the page**: Next.js's App Router treats `loading.tsx` as the canonical fallback for the route segment's async work. Suspense inside the page would require splitting render-paths and is the right call when only PART of a route is async; here the whole page depends on the preview fetch, so the segment-level loader is the cleaner abstraction. **Why no test added**: this is a UI-only loading state; it doesn't change any procedure semantics, no public API surface, no DB write. The existing `invitations.preview` test coverage already ensures the procedure returns the right role. **QA-2 status**: flipped to FIXED in `docs/qa-pass-2026-05-02.md` with this commit's SHA. **Verification**: tsc clean, lint 0 errors. (Skipped vitest — no procedure changes; same pre-existing flake situation as B.PT80.) |
| B.PT80 | Sync billing UI after Stripe Checkout success — invalidate + poll for plan change (resolves QA-4 from `docs/qa-pass-2026-05-02.md`) | SHIPPED | _to be filled by commit_ | Field observation from the 2026-05-02 QA pass: completing Stripe Checkout returned the user to `/settings/billing?billing=success` but the page still rendered the old (FREE) plan badge until manual reload. Webhook ran fine — DB was correct — but the React Query cache wasn't invalidated on land. Audited cal.com (their post-checkout flow does a `success_url` → `/api/teams/api/create?session_id={CHECKOUT_SESSION_ID}` server route that processes the session before redirecting, so the cache is fresh by the time the user sees the page) and dub (`apps/web/ui/modals/upgraded-modal.tsx` + `modal-provider` — checks `?upgraded=true` → opens a confirmation modal → `mutateWorkspace()` invalidates SWR on close). Lab's pattern most closely matches dub's: react to a query param flip on the existing client component. Direct port. **Implementation** (`src/app/(host)/settings/billing/components/billing-section.tsx`): added a sibling client component `<CheckoutReturnSync />` mounted inside `BillingSection` between the page header and the `<BillingFields />` body. Reads `?billing=success` via `useSearchParams`. On match: (a) invalidates `trpc.billing.currentPlan` + `trpc.workspaces.list` (latter affects the topbar plan badge so the chrome reads honest), (b) polls every 2s for up to 10s waiting for the plan to flip away from FREE — handles the webhook race (Stripe webhook may not have processed yet when the user lands), (c) on success: success toast + `router.replace("/settings/billing")` strips the query param so reload doesn't re-fire, (d) on poll-budget timeout: info toast ("payment received, refresh in a moment") + same query strip — surfaces the rare slow-webhook case explicitly instead of silently giving up. Cancelled path (`?billing=cancelled`) similarly toasts + strips. **Why polling instead of one-shot invalidate**: a single invalidate races the webhook. dub.co's modal flow buys time via a celebration modal the user reads through — we don't have that; the toast is shown immediately, so the underlying data must be authoritative by then. 10s budget covers the 99% case; past that, the info toast is honest about uncertainty. **Why not pass `session_id` through and verify server-side** (cal.com pattern): heavier — a new route, a new procedure, a server-side fetch back to Stripe to confirm the session. The webhook is already authoritative; the client just needs to wait for it. Keep the procedure surface minimal. **Session guard ref**: a `sessionStartedRef` ensures one polling session per `?billing=success` arrival — without it, `router.replace` triggers a re-render that flips `searchParams` to null which would cleanup-and-relaunch on the next mount. The ref-flip on `status === "success"` re-entry covers users who Cmd+Click the checkout button + come back via browser back. **Locale**: new `Billing.checkoutSuccessToast` / `checkoutPendingToast` / `checkoutCancelledToast` keys in `messages/en.json` + `messages/es.json`. **QA-4 status**: flipped to FIXED in `docs/qa-pass-2026-05-02.md` with this commit's SHA. **Verification**: tsc clean, lint 0 errors, vitest 362 passed / 5 failed / 2 skipped — the 5 failures (4 `workspaces-invitations.test.ts` + 1 `webhooks.test.ts:195`) are pre-existing flakes documented in B.PT58 + B.PT77 (test isolation issues from `findFirst()` calls without orderBy / scope + accumulated dev DB state from manual UI testing); orthogonal to this commit which is UI client-side effect only, no procedure or schema changes. |
| B.PT79 | Floating admin dev-notes launcher next to the checklist FAB (debounced auto-save) | SHIPPED | _to be filled by commit_ | User wanted a quick scratchpad in-app to jot what they notice while walking the features-and-tests checklist (B.PT75) — observations, bugs, "this looks weird" thoughts. Asked for a second FAB next to the existing one, with an auto-save behavior: only save after typing pauses, and every new keystroke restarts the timer (no save mid-burst). **Files**: `src/components/oh/dev-notes-mount.tsx` (server — admin-gates via `isAdminHandle`, returns null for non-admins, mirrors `DevChecklistMount`'s shape one-for-one); `src/components/oh/dev-notes-launcher.tsx` (client — fixed FAB at `top-14 left-20` (16px gap after the checklist FAB which is at `left-4` size-12) opens `<ResponsiveModal>` with a tall textarea + "Saved" pill + Copy + Clear buttons). **Mount**: `<DevNotesMount />` placed alongside `<DevChecklistMount />` in `(host)/layout.tsx` inside the existing HydrationBoundary. **Storage**: `localStorage` key `oh-dev-notes-v1`. Single freeform string — append-style notes work fine in plain text, no row schema needed; if the host wants structure they can use Markdown headings / bullets in the textarea and the Copy button dumps the buffer for paste-into-real-bug-tracker. **Debounce shape**: `useEffect(() => { clearTimeout(prev); setTimeout(save, 1500ms) }, [notes])` — every keystroke triggers the effect, the cleanup clears the prior timer, the new effect schedules a fresh 1500ms save. Only the trailing pause persists. Timer reset behavior matches the user's exact ask: "every time types it resets the timer." **"Saved" pill**: separate `showSaved` boolean state, flipped true when the timer fires, faded back to false after 1.5s by a second `useEffect`. The pill flashes briefly to confirm the write landed; subsequent edits clear it (visible "writing pending" signal). Used a separate boolean instead of computing `Date.now() - savedAt < 1500` because React 19's compiler ESLint rule (`react-hooks/no-impure-call`) treats `Date.now()` during render as impure and flagged it as an error. State + effect is the canonical refactor — same pattern `<RevealedToken>` uses for the "Copied" pill in the api-key-create-dialog. **Why localStorage and not server-side**: notes are personal scratch, scoped to the host's machine + browser. No collaboration story (admin-only feature, single user). Adding a server schema would mean a Prisma model + tRPC procedures + sync logic for a feature whose value is "I want this for the next 30 minutes while testing." localStorage is the right shape; the Copy button is the off-ramp when the host actually wants to commit notes elsewhere. **Why `<textarea>` not a markdown editor**: keystrokes-to-text is the fastest mental model for jotting; visual rendering would slow capture. The dev checklist is markdown-rendered because it's READ-only consumption; notes are write-mostly so plain text wins. **Position math** (initial cut used Tailwind v4 dynamic spacing tokens `left-20` / `left-22` — those produced overlap on first paint before HMR caught up to the JIT class generation; switched to arbitrary `[5rem]`/`[6rem]` values so the JIT generates them as ad-hoc classes always present in the bundle): mobile `left-4` (16) + size-12 (48) = 64; notes at `left-[5rem]` (80) → 16px gap. sm+ `left-6` (24) + 48 = 72; notes at `left-[6rem]` (96) → 24px gap. **Verification**: tsc clean, lint 0 errors, vitest 366 passed / 1 failed / 2 skipped (same pre-existing `webhooks.test.ts:195` flake). |
| B.PT78 | Persist + surface HTTP response status code on webhook deliveries | SHIPPED | _to be filled by commit_ | User report after B.PT77: webhook.site set to return 400 but the booking detail row still showed "Delivered ✓" — couldn't tell whether the receiver actually accepted the request or whether the cron got something else. Confusion came from two places: (a) webhook.site's default response IS 200 OK unless explicitly configured per-page (easy miss — many users miss the "Edit" button), (b) our row chrome showed only success/fail buckets, never the actual HTTP status. **Researched** how cal.com + dub surface this: cal.com keeps task rows minimal (`packages/features/tasker/tasks/webhookDelivery.ts` — no status code persisted, they rely on logs); dub.co stores every webhook event in **Tinybird** with explicit `http_status`, `request_body`, `response_body` fields per event (`apps/web/lib/zod/schemas/webhooks.ts:37-50` — `webhookEventSchemaTB`). Dub's pattern is the modern shape; we're aligning with it scaled-down (no Tinybird; SQLite Task table). **Schema change**: added `lastResponseStatus Int?` to `Task` (`prisma/schema.prisma:418-426`). Migration `20260502063636_task_last_response_status` is purely additive (`ALTER TABLE "Task" ADD COLUMN`), no backfill needed (existing rows show null and skip the badge). **Cron processor** (`src/app/api/cron/process-tasks/route.ts`): success branch (2xx) writes `lastResponseStatus: response.status` alongside `succeededAt`. Both `markFailed` and `markPermanentlyFailed` now accept an optional `responseStatus` argument (undefined for non-network task types — emailSend, calendarWrite — keeps their column null via the conditional spread `...(typeof responseStatus === "number" ? { lastResponseStatus: responseStatus } : {})`). Network-error path (DNS / timeout / abort, no response object) leaves the field untouched since there's no code to capture. **Procedure** (`src/trpc/routers/bookings.ts`): `getDetail` selects `lastResponseStatus` on both `pendingTasks` (failed/retrying) and `deliveries` (succeeded) so the UI has it for every row. **UI** (`src/app/(host)/bookings/[publicUid]/components/booking-detail.tsx`): the status code renders as a mono-caps tabular-num badge next to the row's status pill — green for 2xx successes (`✓ 200`), destructive-red for 4xx/5xx failures (`✗ 400 Permanently failed`). aria-label `HTTP <code>` so a screen reader announces it as a status code rather than a free-floating number. **Why not also store the response body**: dub does it via Tinybird, where retention + bloat are non-issues; SQLite is column-storage with no native compression and a webhook body can be 50KB+. Adding a body column to Task would balloon DB size for diagnostic data that's rarely read. If we ever need it, a separate `WebhookDeliveryAttempt` table with a retention cron is the right shape — out of scope for this commit. **Why dub-style instead of cal.com-style** (no field at all): user's confusion was specifically "what was the actual response" — we need the data on the row. Cal.com's "ask the receiver's logs" answer doesn't fit a small-team self-service product. **Why optional argument with conditional spread instead of always-write**: emailSend's `sendEmail` returns `{ ok, id, reason, error }` — no HTTP response code in the same shape. calendarWrite's adapter throws on failure — also no code surface. Passing them an integer would require synthesizing one (e.g. 0 for "no code") which is data lying. Conditional spread keeps the column accurately null for non-HTTP task types. **Verification**: ran `pnpm prisma migrate dev` (created + applied additive migration), `pnpm prisma generate` (regenerated typed client — required per AGENTS.md "Always do" — restarted dev server post-gen on port 3001). tsc clean, lint 0 errors, vitest 366 passed / 1 failed / 2 skipped (same pre-existing `webhooks.test.ts:195` flake from B.PT77 — `findFirst()` w/o orderBy + user-created subscriptions polluting DB; orthogonal to this commit). |
| B.PT77 | Booking detail webhook deliveries — explicit Failed section with status glyphs | SHIPPED | _to be filled by commit_ | User report after B.PT74 + step-8 manual test: when a webhook receiver returns 5xx/4xx, the booking detail page surfaces the row but doesn't visibly distinguish "delivered with 2xx" from "scheduled, retrying" from "permanently dead." All states read as "Delivered" or live under an ambiguous heading. Diagnosis (`src/trpc/routers/bookings.ts:687-724` + `src/app/(host)/bookings/[publicUid]/components/booking-detail.tsx:536-588`): the procedure splits Task rows for this booking into two buckets — `pendingTasks` (`succeededAt: null`) and `deliveries` (`succeededAt: { not: null }`). The cron processor's success branch is the ONLY thing that writes `succeededAt`; markFailed and markPermanentlyFailed both leave it null. So a permanently-failed delivery (`attempts >= maxAttempts`) lives in `pendingTasks` indistinguishable from in-flight retries. The UI section labels "Scheduled" + "Delivered" don't communicate this and there's no error surface. **Fix**: add `maxAttempts` to the `pendingTasks` select; client-side split into "still retrying" (`attempts < maxAttempts`) and "permanently failed" (`attempts >= maxAttempts`). New "Failed" section with destructive color, ✗ glyph, the row's `lastError` truncated to 3 lines via `line-clamp-3`, and a "Permanently failed" status pill. "Scheduled" section gets a ⟳ glyph. "Delivered" section gets a green ✓ glyph. The host now sees three visually distinct states at a glance. **Why client-side split rather than a third query**: data is already on the wire; splitting on the array avoids a round-trip and a new procedure surface. The `maxAttempts` field was the only missing piece — schema already had it (`Task.maxAttempts Int @default(3)`). **Why no audit row writes**: state is observable from the existing Task table; auditing is for booking state, not delivery infra. The task row's `lastError` + `attempts` + `lastFailedAttemptAt` are the authoritative trail. **Locale**: new `failed` (section title) + `failedStatus` ("Permanently failed" pill text) keys in `messages/en.json` + `messages/es.json`. Other strings (`scheduled`, `delivered`, `retriedAttempts`) reused. **Verification**: tsc clean, lint 0 errors, vitest 366 passed / 1 failed / 2 skipped — the 1 failure (`webhooks.test.ts:195` "writes a Task row when an active matching webhook exists") is a pre-existing test isolation flake, NOT introduced by this commit. The test uses `prisma.webhookSubscription.findFirst()` without an `orderBy` or workspace scope; in this session I exercised the new B.PT74 webhooks UI to create real subscriptions (manually verifying step 8 of features-and-tests.md), leaving extra rows in `dev.db`. The test's `findFirst()` sometimes returns one of my UI-created rows instead of the one the test just created. My change doesn't touch the webhooks router, the bookings.create webhook scheduling, or the Task referenceUid format. Stash-based isolation experiment confirmed: removing my changes doesn't fix the test (it just shifts to a different failure mode because the unrelated `booking-detail.tsx` then has a TS reference to `maxAttempts` that doesn't exist on the un-edited procedure shape). The proper fix for the test belongs in a separate commit that scopes `findFirst({ where: { workspaceId } })` or adds a `beforeEach` cleanup; orthogonal to this UI fix. |
| B.PT76 | Dev checklist scroll-position persistence + move FAB to top-left | SHIPPED | _to be filled by commit_ | User feedback after B.PT75: (a) reopening the modal always landed at the top of the 729-line markdown — annoying when stepping through items 30+; (b) FAB at bottom-right collided with their natural one-handed mobile reach pattern, asked for top-left. **Scroll fix**: moved the `max-h-[75vh] overflow-y-auto overscroll-contain` from the launcher's wrapper div into `<DevChecklistContent>`'s own `<article>` element so the scroll container is colocated with the storage logic. Two effects added: (1) on-mount restore reads `oh-dev-checklist-scroll-v1` from localStorage and assigns `node.scrollTop` inside `requestAnimationFrame` — without the rAF, the assignment lands BEFORE the markdown children have rendered their heights and the browser clamps the value to 0; rAF defers until the first paint settles. (2) `onScroll` handler throttled via a `requestAnimationFrame` ref-flag — at most one localStorage write per paint frame, preventing 60×/sec writes during a fast wheel burst. **FAB position**: `fixed bottom-4 right-4 ... sm:bottom-6 sm:right-6` → `fixed top-14 left-4 ... sm:top-16 sm:left-6`. `top-14` (56px) clears the dashboard bar's `--oh-dashboard-bar-block` (~40px) plus a comfortable gap; on iOS where the notch shifts the bar down, the gap stays positive. On desktop the FAB sits over the sidebar's footer area (sparse by design) — visually a deliberate utility, not an accidental overlap. **Why not `useEffect` w/ setState**: the React 19 `react-hooks/set-state-in-effect` rule is the project guard; this commit only mutates DOM (`node.scrollTop = ...`) and writes to localStorage inside the effect — no setState, so the rule passes. Same shape `useMounted()` would NOT help because we need the actual DOM ref, which is by definition only available post-mount. **Edge case** (intentional): if the user scrolls to position X, closes the modal, deletes the markdown file, and reopens, the restore writes scrollTop=X but the document height is shorter — browser clamps to max-scroll automatically; no visual artifact. Same for marking many items done (height shrinks slightly because `<li>` doesn't actually change height on check, just background — height unchanged). **Storage** keys are now: `oh-dev-checklist-v1` (per-row checked map, B.PT75) + `oh-dev-checklist-scroll-v1` (scrollTop number, this commit). Keys are versioned (`-v1` suffix) so future schema migrations can detect + drop stale data. **Files**: `src/components/oh/dev-checklist-launcher.tsx` (FAB position class swap; scroll wrapper removed in favor of in-content placement); `src/components/oh/dev-checklist-content.tsx` (added `useRef`/`useEffect`/`useCallback` imports + `SCROLL_KEY` const + restore effect + rAF-throttled save + scroll-container styles on the `<article>`). Gates: tsc clean, lint 0 errors. |
| B.PT75 | Floating admin dev checklist — renders `docs/features-and-tests.md` with persistent task-list state | SHIPPED | _to be filled by commit_ | User asked for an in-app way to walk the features-and-tests checklist on mobile without flipping between an editor and the running app. Wanted (a) a fixed/floating launcher accessible from any dashboard route, (b) the markdown rendered with interactive checkboxes, (c) state preserved across sessions/reloads. Researched options: `react-markdown@^10` + `remark-gfm@^4` is the canonical pair for GFM task lists (the `- [x] thing` syntax) — `remark-gfm` is what enables checkbox parsing in the first place; without it task items render as plain hyphenated text. cal.com + dub don't ship a comparable in-app dev checklist (they rely on Linear / Storybook for ops UX), so no reference codebase pattern to mirror — built from the canonical react-markdown docs. **Files**: `src/components/oh/dev-checklist-mount.tsx` (server — admin-gates via `isAdminHandle`, reads `docs/features-and-tests.md` via `fs.readFileSync` at request time, returns null for non-admins so neither the markdown blob nor the launcher chrome ship to non-admin clients), `src/components/oh/dev-checklist-launcher.tsx` (client — fixed bottom-right FAB at `z-30` opens `<ResponsiveModal>` (Vaul drawer at <md, Base UI dialog at md+ via the existing primitive); content is lazy-loaded via `React.lazy(() => import('./dev-checklist-content'))` so the ~50KB react-markdown + remark-gfm bundle only hits the wire when the panel opens — non-admin pages pay zero), `src/components/oh/dev-checklist-content.tsx` (client — `<ReactMarkdown remarkPlugins={[remarkGfm]} components={{ input }}>` swaps the GFM-emitted `<input type="checkbox">` for an interactive control bound to localStorage state, "Reset all" button at the top, completed count in `oh-eyebrow tabular-nums`), `src/app/(host)/layout.tsx` (mounts `<DevChecklistMount />` inside the existing HydrationBoundary so the FAB is available on every authenticated host route alongside `{modal}`), `src/app/globals.css` (new prose styling block — `oh-dev-checklist-prose` with H1/H2/H3 sizes, `code`/`pre` chrome, GFM table styling, and `oh-dev-checklist-box` for the interactive checkbox using `appearance: none` + a CSS-drawn checkmark via `::after` `border-left+border-bottom rotate(-45deg)`). **Storage shape**: `Record<lineHash, boolean>` under localStorage key `oh-dev-checklist-v1`. Per-checkbox key is a djb2 hash of the surrounding line's raw markdown text so an unrelated edit further down the file doesn't shuffle saved checks; only edits to the literal task line invalidate that one row. Hash is non-cryptographic (collisions theoretically possible) but the file is ~150 task items long; collision risk is well under any practical concern. **Why lazy state init instead of `useEffect(() => setChecked(read()))`**: React 19's compiler ESLint rule (`react-hooks/set-state-in-effect`) blocks the latter pattern per the project's "Never do" list. The lazy `useState(() => readStorage())` form runs once on first render. Safe across the SSR boundary because the panel content is only rendered after the modal opens (purely client-side render path) — the launcher's closed-state SSR output never includes the checklist. **Why admin-gated**: the doc references internal commands, dev secrets, and ops procedures. Shipping it to all signed-in hosts would leak operational notes. Same `OFFICEHOURS_ADMIN_HANDLES` env that governs `/admin/*` (B.PT19 era) — one source of truth for "is this user an operator." **Defers**: none. **What this commit does NOT do**: render the markdown server-side (lazy-load was the right call given bundle size); add a search/filter inside the panel (700+ lines is fine to scroll on a 13px font; revisit if a complaint surfaces); fall back gracefully if the markdown file is genuinely missing (returns null silently — the launcher just doesn't appear, which is the right behavior for a partial clone). Gates: tsc clean, lint 0 errors, vitest 367 passed / 2 skipped. |
