| B.PT73 | `EMAIL_DEV_REDIRECT` env override so dev emails arrive in a real inbox instead of `delivered@resend.dev` | SHIPPED | _to be filled by commit_ | User feedback during the features-and-tests walk-through (item 7, visitor reminder email): every email landed at Resend's `delivered@resend.dev` dev sink, never in the user's actual inbox, making it hard to verify rendered content / click links during manual testing. **Audit of cited reference codebases** (`~/Desktop/cal.com/`, `~/Desktop/dub/`): cal.com runs **Mailhog** for E2E (SMTP catcher daemon at localhost — `IS_MAILHOG_ENABLED` flag in `packages/lib/constants.ts:148`). Dub uses the **exact line we copied** — `to: isProdEnv ? to : "delivered@resend.dev"` (`packages/email/src/send-via-resend.ts:30`). Mailhog is heavyweight for a single-developer testing flow; dub's sink is what we already do. Neither offers an inbox-redirect knob. **Decision**: add an opt-in `EMAIL_DEV_REDIRECT` env that, when set, routes every dev send to that address instead of the Resend sink. Default unset → behavior unchanged from B.PT57-era (preserves dub's safety net so a wandering test booking doesn't email a real visitor). **Files**: `src/env.ts` (new schema entry — `z.string().email().optional()`, validates address shape on boot), `src/lib/email/index.ts` (`resolveRecipient` checks `process.env.EMAIL_DEV_REDIRECT` after the prod-bypass guard, returns it if set, else falls through to the existing `delivered@resend.dev` sink), `.env.example` (commented entry under `EMAIL_FROM` so a fresh clone sees the option). **Why this isn't reading directly from t3-env's `env`**: `resolveRecipient` runs inside the cron processor route which currently reads `process.env.CRON_SECRET` directly (per the comment in `src/app/api/cron/process-tasks/route.ts:42-43` — t3-env snapshots at module load and tests mutate env post-import). Same pattern here for consistency. **Why not via `EMAIL_FROM`-style mapping**: that's the sender, not the recipient. Different concept; conflating would muddle the mental model. **Why not Mailhog**: requires a daemon (`brew install mailhog && mailhog`), changes the transport (Resend → SMTP), and pulls dev away from the production-shaped path. Opt-in env gives 90% of the value with 5% of the setup. **Production safety**: the `VERCEL_ENV === "production"` short-circuit at the top of `resolveRecipient` runs FIRST — even if a misconfigured prod env had `EMAIL_DEV_REDIRECT` set, prod sends still use the real recipient. **Tested**: tsc clean, lint 0 errors, vitest 367 passed / 2 skipped. **Why magic link "works" perceptually**: NextAuth's magic-link provider uses the same `sendEmail` wrapper (`src/auth.ts:43-48`) → same dev sink. Pre-B.PT73 the user was likely seeing magic links in the Resend dashboard logs (where `delivered@resend.dev` sends show up) and considering them "received." Post-B.PT73, setting `EMAIL_DEV_REDIRECT` makes them land in the actual inbox. |
| B.PT72 | Dev-only Task queue auto-drain (`pnpm dev:cron`) so calendar writes propagate without manual curl | SHIPPED | _to be filled by commit_ | User feedback during the calendar two-way write end-to-end test (post-B.PT71): cancel + reschedule flows correctly enqueue a `calendarWrite` Task row but in dev there's no cron firing the `/api/cron/process-tasks` endpoint, so the row sits in the queue until the developer manually `curl`s it with the bearer token. Production (Vercel Cron, every 5 min per `vercel.json`) handles this for free. **Decision (cal.com vs queue audit)**: kept the queue. A sub-agent audit (logged in chat, not in a separate doc) walked the cal.com source at `~/Desktop/cal.com/` — `RegularBookingService.ts:2052` shows cal.com awaits `eventManager.create()` synchronously inside the booking handler with no retry queue, no rollback on Google API failure, just log-and-continue. That trade-off (request blocks 500ms-2s on Google + no recovery from outage) is fine at cal.com's scale but the lab's queued architecture (B.PT58-era §10.1 production primitives) is a defensibility strength, not a regression to walk back from. The queue catches transient Google failures with exponential backoff (5m → 60m capped, 3 attempts) and decouples booking commits from external API health. Dev annoyance is solved with a tiny poller, not an architectural rewrite. **Files**: `scripts/dev-cron.mjs` (new ESM script — Node built-in `fetch`, no deps; reads `CRON_SECRET` via tiny `.env` parser to avoid pulling in `dotenv`; defaults to `http://localhost:3001` and 10s interval, both env-overridable via `PORT` + `CRON_INTERVAL_MS`; suppresses log on `processed=0` ticks to avoid spam; exponential-backs-off on connection-refused so a `pnpm dev` restart doesn't spew errors; clean shutdown on `SIGINT`/`SIGTERM`); `package.json` (new `dev:cron` script, runs `node scripts/dev-cron.mjs`). **Workflow**: developer runs `pnpm dev` in one terminal + `pnpm dev:cron` in another. Booking → cancel → reschedule all reflect on Google Calendar within ≤10s without any manual curl. **Why not bundle into `pnpm dev`** (no `concurrently` add): `pnpm dev` running both processes interleaves their stdout streams and complicates Ctrl+C semantics; a separate terminal keeps the cron logs scannable on their own and lets the developer kill / restart the poller without tearing down Next. **What this commit does NOT do**: doesn't change `bookings.create / .cancel / .reschedule` procedures (already correct per B.PT58 §10.1 audit), doesn't touch `vercel.json` (production cron unchanged), doesn't add any prod runtime path (script is dev-only and never imported by app code). Verification: tsc clean, lint 0 errors, vitest 367 passed / 2 skipped (the 4 pre-existing `workspaces-invitations` failures from prior runs were a stale-DB artifact — gone after the recent `migrate reset`), smoke test confirmed the script boots, polls, and shuts down cleanly. |
| B.PT71 | Preview chrome-morph panel twitch on shrink — fix snapshot aspect ratio + drop sidebar-gap VT participant | SHIPPED | _to be filled by commit_ | User report after B.PT70: chrome morph in Chrome (after the architecture stabilized in B.PT65-B.PT68) still shows a "twitch" when shrinking from preview back to dashboard, and a perceived "left side closes faster than top" feel. **Diagnosis** (verified via Jake Archibald's canonical write-up at jakearchibald.com/2024/view-transitions-handling-aspect-ratio-changes/ + Chrome for Developers same-document VT docs): two stacked effects. **(1) Snapshot aspect-ratio distortion** (the twitch): per the VT API spec, `::view-transition-old/new` snapshots ship default `width: 100%; height: auto;` — they fill the morphing group's WIDTH but preserve their intrinsic aspect ratio (HEIGHT). When the panel morphs from full-bleed (one aspect ratio) to dashboard-shape (different aspect ratio), the snapshots' fixed aspect doesn't match the morphing group bounds — vertical gap or overflow appears at the boundary, the eye reads it as a frame-level twitch. **(2) Geometric speed asymmetry** (the "left closes faster"): the panel's left edge moves ~180px (sidebar reveal), top moves ~40px (bar reveal), right + bottom don't move. Same 380ms duration → left edge moves ~4× faster in pixels-per-ms. Pure math, not a bug. **Fix for the twitch** (Jake Archibald's exact recommendation): force `height: 100%` on `::view-transition-old(oh-chrome-panel)` + `::view-transition-new(oh-chrome-panel)`. Snapshots fill the group's morphing height; combined with the existing `overflow: hidden` on the group (B.PT63), any aspect-ratio distortion is contained within the rounded clip. Content slightly stretches but the morph reads as continuous, not jittery. **Bonus simplification** — dropped `view-transition-name: oh-chrome-sidebar-gap` from `[data-slot="sidebar-gap"]`. Sidebar-gap is a transparent flex placeholder; its width morph (0 ↔ 192px) has no visual content. Snapshotting it added an adjacent VT group whose right edge sat against the panel group's left edge during the morph — sub-pixel rounding between two independent groups produced a 1px boundary jitter that compounded the panel's aspect-ratio twitch. Removing the VT name lets the gap width snap instantly in real DOM (one-time layout, no thrash because nothing CSS-transitions on it; the visible area is the panel's VT snapshot which morphs correctly because its NEW group bounds are computed AFTER the DOM update — flex layout has reflowed by then). Cleaner system, fewer participants, less surface for sub-pixel artifacts. **Geometric asymmetry — left closes faster**: this is mathematical reality given the chrome's layout (sidebar on left only, bar on top only). Re-architecting to fix would need either (a) a square chrome layout (no left-only sidebar, no top-only bar) — destroys the dashboard identity; or (b) per-edge keyframe control via custom `::view-transition-group(name) { animation: ... }` overrides — possible but complex (would need a custom @keyframes that uses different timing functions per side, which CSS doesn't support directly; would need separate VT names per side, very invasive). Decision: accept the asymmetry as inherent to the chrome layout. The `var(--ease-oh)` curve (cubic-bezier 0.16, 1, 0.3, 1) already decelerates at the end so the fast left edge eases gently into its destination — softens the perception. **Diagnostic methodology used** (logged for future): WebSearch + WebFetch on Jake Archibald's article (the canonical reference for VT aspect-ratio quirks), MDN spec sections, Chrome for Developers docs. The aspect-ratio twitch IS a known quirk with a documented fix. The geometric asymmetry is layout-fundamental, no library-side fix exists. Gates: tsc clean, lint 0 errors. References: jakearchibald.com/2024/view-transitions-handling-aspect-ratio-changes, drafts.csswg.org/css-view-transitions-1, developer.chrome.com/docs/web-platform/view-transitions. |
| B.PT70 | Firefox fallback CSS transition runs invisibly behind root snapshot — opt root out of VT | SHIPPED | _to be filled by commit_ | User report after B.PT69: chrome-morph fallback in Firefox still snaps — animation effectively invisible. **Root cause** (verified via MDN View Transitions API spec, drafts.csswg.org/css-view-transitions): when a View Transition is active, "elements in a Document captured in a view transition and its element contents, except transition root pseudo-element's inclusive descendants, are not painted (as if they had `visibility: hidden`)." The `<html>` root element has an implicit `view-transition-name: root` per spec, so its snapshot covers the entire page during the cross-fade window (~250ms default). The B.PT69 fallback added `transition: margin 380ms` on `.oh-host-content`, but that transition was running on the REAL DOM behind the root snapshot — invisible. By the time the snapshot is removed (~250ms in), the CSS transition has only ~130ms left to play visibly; user sees the tail end as a "snap." `next-view-transitions` always wraps router.push in `document.startViewTransition()`, so even though our chrome elements opt out via `view-transition-name: none`, the ROOT still participates and shows its snapshot. **Fix**: also set `view-transition-name: none !important` on `:root.oh-firefox`. With root excluded AND chrome elements excluded, there are zero VT participants in Firefox — no snapshot overlay, real DOM stays painted, the CSS transition plays fully visible over its 380ms. The `!important` is belt-and-suspenders against the implicit-root default that the UA sets at the spec level. **Side effect**: in Firefox, ALL route changes (not just preview) skip the default UA cross-fade. Acceptable — Firefox's VT cross-fade was buggy for our setup anyway, and CSS transitions on the panel margin still animate the preview-specific morph. **Why this didn't bite Chrome**: in Chrome we have NO CSS transitions on chrome elements (B.PT65 removed them — VT API drives the morph entirely). Real DOM being hidden during VT doesn't matter because nothing's running on it. The B.PT69 fallback re-introduced CSS transitions for Firefox, which exposed this previously-irrelevant interaction. **Pattern lesson logged**: when adding CSS-transition fallbacks alongside View Transitions, ensure the fallback target isn't covered by a VT root snapshot — otherwise the transition runs invisibly. Either opt root out of VT (this commit's approach) OR call `transition.skipTransition()` on the returned ViewTransition object (requires JS plumbing into next-view-transitions). The CSS-only path is cheaper and ships in this commit. References: developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/View_Transition_API/Using ("Elements participating in a transition need to skip painting in their DOM location"), drafts.csswg.org/css-view-transitions-1, MDN ViewTransition.skipTransition(). Gates: tsc clean, lint 0 errors. |
| B.PT69 | Firefox chrome-morph fallback — VT API broken on fixed-position sidebar in Firefox | SHIPPED | _to be filled by commit_ | User report: chrome morph works in Chrome, fails in Firefox. **Diagnosis** (verified via web research — caniuse, MDN, Mozilla Bugzilla, GitHub issues): Firefox's same-document View Transitions API support landed in Firefox 133 (Nov 2024), but the initial implementation has known limitations with `position: fixed` elements (Bugzilla #1688813 family — "drawSnapshot doesn't handle fixed element properly") and miscalculates VT snapshot coordinates when transformed parents create containing blocks (storybookjs/storybook#33631 — confirmed in Firefox 134+). Our shadcn sidebar primitive renders `[data-slot="sidebar-container"]` with `fixed inset-y-0 z-10` (sidebar.tsx:233) — exactly the bug class. Even Firefox 144 (Oct 2025), which shipped more complete VT support and added pseudo-element styling parity, still hits the corner case for our specific setup of fixed sidebar + transformed parent + multi-element named-group morph. Chrome and Safari handle the same code correctly because their VT implementations have had a longer runway (Chrome 111 from March 2023, Safari 18 from Sept 2024). **Fix**: runtime UA detection in `OhDashboardLayout` — if the user-agent string matches `firefox`, tag `documentElement.classList.add("oh-firefox")`. CSS rules under `:root.oh-firefox` (a) drop `view-transition-name` on all chrome elements so Firefox doesn't try (and fail) to snapshot them, and (b) apply a simple CSS transition on the panel's margin so the most visible part of the morph still animates smoothly. Bar/sidebar snap (no morph) — acceptable degradation since the panel's expansion is the focal point. The CSS-transition is a single property on a single element, so the layout-thrash that motivated the B.PT65 VT-API rewrite isn't an issue here (B.PT60-B.PT64 thrashed because they animated FOUR properties across FOUR elements simultaneously; one property on one element is fine on any modern engine). **Why UA sniffing instead of `@supports`**: CSS `@supports` doesn't distinguish Firefox 133+ from Chrome reliably — both implement `view-transition-name` and `mix-blend-mode: plus-lighter` (Firefox added the latter in 119). The bug is implementation-specific (how Firefox handles fixed elements during VT), not a missing CSS feature. UA sniffing is the right tool for engine-specific quirks; we accept the tradeoff. When Firefox catches up (likely a future release post-144), we can remove `.oh-firefox` and the detection effect — every browser falls into the GPU-driven morph. **Reduced motion**: respected in Firefox fallback too — `@media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce) { :root.oh-firefox .oh-host-content { transition-duration: 0ms; } }`. **Other Firefox-quirky paths in the codebase to audit if VT issues recur**: nothing else in the repo uses `view-transition-name` directly (only the chrome morph). Other VT users would need the same audit. Gates: tsc clean, lint 0 errors. References: Bugzilla #1688813, storybookjs/storybook#33631, caniuse.com/view-transitions, Firefox 144 release notes (developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Mozilla/Firefox/Releases/144). |
| B.PT68 | Preview chrome-morph sidebar fade-out overlays panel — z-index sidebar VT group below | SHIPPED | _to be filled by commit_ | User report after B.PT67: sidebar fading out during preview entry overlays the morph instead of receding behind it. **Diagnosis** (verified against the CSS View Transitions API spec, drafts.csswg.org/css-view-transitions-1): `::view-transition-group(name)` pseudo-elements inherit z-stacking from the captured element's original z-index in the page. The shadcn sidebar primitive ships `fixed inset-y-0 z-10 hidden h-svh w-(--sidebar-width)` on `[data-slot="sidebar-container"]` (`src/components/ui/sidebar.tsx:233`) — that `z-10` puts the original sidebar at z-index 10 in the page, so its VT group lands at z=10 too. The panel's VT group has no explicit z-index, so it defaults to z auto = 0. Result: sidebar snapshot stacks ABOVE panel snapshot during the cross-fade — as the sidebar fades out (opacity 1→0), its snapshot is visually on top of the morphing panel for the full ~190ms it stays partially opaque. **Fix**: override z-index on the sidebar's VT groups to 0, panel group to 1. Sidebar fade-out now happens BEHIND the panel — the morph's focal point stays the panel as it grows to full bleed; the sidebar quietly recedes underneath. **Why mobile didn't show this**: at <md the sidebar primitive's wrapper has `hidden md:block` (`display: none` at <md), no snapshot is captured for non-rendered elements, no z-index conflict. Only the bar + panel VT groups participate at <md, both default z-index, no stacking issue. **Why this wasn't a problem before B.PT65**: the previous CSS-transition approach animated the sidebar's opacity directly on the real element, which kept the original z-10 in the page's stacking context — but the panel's CSS-transitioned margin animation also ran on the real panel (z=0), so the sidebar opacity-fade was always above the panel's margin morph. The user just didn't catch it then because the entire morph was choppy on desktop (B.PT65 root cause); the fade-overlay was masked by the chop. With B.PT65-B.PT67's clean GPU-driven morph, the previously-masked z-index quirk became the most visible artifact. **Pattern lesson logged**: when adding view-transition-name to elements that already participate in the page's z-index hierarchy (especially primitive-sourced fixed/absolute elements like sidebars, modals, drawers), audit the original z-index and explicitly set `z-index` on the corresponding `::view-transition-group(name)` to make stacking deterministic during the transition. Gates: tsc clean, lint 0 errors. |
| B.PT67 | Preview chrome-morph panel bg fades through to cream frame — paint solid paper on the VT group | SHIPPED | _to be filled by commit_ | User report after B.PT66: "remember we are in desktop is the one not working should we also in the transition add like maybe a placeholder in the background that is the same color as the background because right now im pretty sure the like animation is animating the background and fading it." Diagnosis matches the user's hypothesis: during the cross-fade window, the panel's paper bg fades along with its content. At ~100ms in, OLD snapshot opacity is ~50% — through the snapshot you see whatever is BEHIND the `::view-transition-group(oh-chrome-panel)` container, which is the document body / cream `--oh-frame`. The fade reads as "the panel itself is fading," not "content swap inside a stable panel" — exactly the desktop-only artifact (mobile doesn't show it because the cream frame is already adjacent to the panel on three sides). **Fix**: paint `background: var(--oh-paper)` on the group pseudo-element. The group is the container that morphs position + size; the snapshots fade in/out inside it. With a solid paper bg on the group itself, the morphing container always shows paper-colored fill regardless of snapshot opacity. The snapshots' own paper bg stacks on top of this; both layers are paper so the cross-fade reads as a stable-bg content swap. Effectively a "placeholder layer" the user suggested, baked into the existing group pseudo-element instead of a new DOM node — no JS plumbing, no extra elements, single CSS line. **Why this didn't show on mobile**: at <md, the panel margin is already uniform 12px on all sides outside preview, AND the panel bg matches what's on three sides of it (cream frame). The fade-to-transparent reveals frame… which is paper-adjacent so the eye doesn't catch the swap. On desktop with sidebar visible, the panel's left edge hugs the sidebar (which is sidebar-bg, distinct from paper), and the morph pulls the panel's left margin from 0 → 12px — so during the fade you see the sidebar-bg AND cream-frame transitioning through, more visually noisy. Painting paper on the group neutralizes both. **Same group already had** (B.PT63) `border-radius: var(--oh-r-window); overflow: hidden;` to clip the snapshots to the rounded corners; bg paint stacks under the existing radius/clip so the painted bg also respects the rounded corners. **What about bar/sidebar groups**: their bgs match what's behind them anyway (bar is transparent letting frame show, sidebar uses `bg-sidebar` which sits in the same general frame palette). Bg fade-through for those is invisible to the eye, so we only paint the panel group. If a future complaint surfaces around bar/sidebar visibility during fade, paint their respective bgs on their groups — same one-line pattern. Gates: tsc clean, lint 0 errors. |
| B.PT66 | Preview chrome-morph cross-fade overlap ghost — sequenced old/new keyframes | SHIPPED | _to be filled by commit_ | User report after B.PT65: at the start of the morph, the OLD state and the NEW (animating) state visibly overlap — "weird overlapping effect" / ghosting. **Cause** (verified via WebSearch + MDN View Transitions API docs): the default UA cross-fade runs `::view-transition-old(name)` opacity 1→0 AND `::view-transition-new(name)` opacity 0→1 SIMULTANEOUSLY over the same window, with `mix-blend-mode: plus-lighter` on the image-pair. Plus-lighter is supposed to keep the visual sum at ~100% throughout the overlap (additive blend) — in practice for the panel snapshot (the only one whose CONTENT differs between routes; bar/sidebar contents are identical so their cross-fades are visually no-ops), `BookingsList` and the visitor surface ghost at the ~50% midpoint instead of looking like one solid image. **Fix**: sequenced cross-fade — OLD fades out over the first 190ms, NEW fades in over the second 190ms (delayed by 190ms). No overlap window. Same shape we shipped in B.PT63 before the B.PT65 architecture restructure flattened it into the UA default. The position/size morph on `::view-transition-group(name)` continues uninterrupted over the full 380ms so the chrome motion (bar slide, sidebar fade, panel margin equalize) is unaffected — only the snapshot opacity timing changes. **Trade-off**: replaces the overlap-ghost with a sub-frame "swap moment" at 190ms where old is gone and new is just starting (~16ms of near-blank). Reads as a snap rather than a ghost; preferable per the user's criteria ("snappy and smooth" was the goal). If the swap moment becomes noticeable on certain hardware, follow-up would be a 50-60ms overlap window with eased opacity curves so the visual sum stays at ~100% (relying on `mix-blend-mode: plus-lighter` to combine cleanly) — defer until a complaint. **Debug methodology** (logged for the next round): Chrome DevTools Animations panel (Cmd+Shift+P → "Show Animations") supports View Transitions explicitly — pause then trigger the morph, the panel captures and freezes mid-flight; the `::view-transition-*` pseudo-tree appears in the Elements panel on top of `<head>` while paused; slow playback to 25%/10% to scrub frame-by-frame. The Performance panel with "Screenshots" enabled catches dropped frames and identifies layout/paint as the bottleneck (B.PT60-B.PT64's flavor of bug). No instrumentation library needed — DevTools is purpose-built for this. References: developer.chrome.com/docs/devtools/css/animations, developer.chrome.com/blog/view-transitions-in-2025, debugbear.com/blog/view-transitions-spa-without-framework. Gates: tsc clean, lint 0 errors. |
| B.PT65 | Preview chrome-morph desktop chop — replace CSS transitions with View Transitions API | SHIPPED | _to be filled by commit_ | User feedback after B.PT64: chrome morph still choppy on desktop, snaps between states, "still kinda work still choppy and it just snaps in between preview and the normal view." Mobile remained smooth (no sidebar elements rendered at <md). **Diagnosis**: layout thrashing. The B.PT60 architecture used CSS transitions on layout-affecting properties (`margin`, `width`, `flex-basis`, `height`) on FOUR elements simultaneously: bar (transform + opacity + flex-basis + height + padding-block), sidebar-gap (width — affects flex sibling layout), sidebar-container (opacity + width/left), panel (margin + height). On desktop with a visible sidebar, every frame of the 380ms morph the row's flex distribution recalculated (because gap's width was changing, bar's flex-basis was changing) AND the panel's margin and height recalculated. Frame budget (~16ms at 60Hz) was routinely blown by the cumulative layout work, frames dropped, the morph read as choppy / "snappy." Mobile didn't show it because `<OhAppSidebar />` returns null at <md → only the bar + panel transitioned, half the elements, half the layout work. **Fix**: replaced the CSS-transition-driven morph with a **View Transitions API named-group** approach. Each chrome element (`.oh-dashboard-bar`, `[data-slot="sidebar-gap"]`, `[data-slot="sidebar-container"]`, `.oh-host-content`) carries a `view-transition-name` (`oh-chrome-bar`, `oh-chrome-sidebar-gap`, `oh-chrome-sidebar`, `oh-chrome-panel` respectively). On route change between `(host)/*` and `(host)/preview/<handle>`, the browser snapshots each named element at its OLD bounds + visual state and at its NEW bounds + visual state, then **morphs between them on the GPU compositor** — zero main-thread layout work during the transition. The real DOM is hidden behind the snapshots and resumes at the END state when the transition ends. The `[data-oh-preview="true"]` rules now declare ONLY the END state (no `transition` declarations); the property changes are what the browser uses to compute the NEW snapshot. Reverse direction (exit preview) works automatically because the API snapshots both directions on every route change. **Why VT works where CSS transitions failed**: VT runs entirely on the compositor thread (GPU), bypassing the layout pass for the transition window. Even with 4 named elements morphing simultaneously, the work is GPU bitmap interpolation — orders of magnitude lighter than the per-frame flex-distribution + margin recalc the CSS approach was doing. **Migration details**: removed `view-transition-name: oh-host-content` from `.oh-host-content-inner` (the ScrollArea Root) — replaced by `oh-chrome-panel` on the outer `.oh-host-content` so the panel snapshot includes the rounded corners + bg + content. The B.PT63 snapshot-clip rule (`border-radius: var(--oh-r-window); overflow: hidden`) renamed from `oh-host-content` group to `oh-chrome-panel` group — same fix, new name. The mobile-menu opt-out (`html:has([data-oh-mobile-menu="true"]) .oh-host-content-inner`) renamed to target `.oh-host-content` instead. Removed orphaned `oh-vt-fade-out` / `oh-vt-fade-in` keyframes — VT default `animation-name` (browser-supplied cross-fade) replaces them; we just override `animation-duration: 380ms` on each named group/old/new pseudo. Reduced motion: the global VT reduced-motion rule (`::view-transition-group(*) { animation: none !important }` earlier in the file) handles the chrome morph; removed the chrome-specific reduced-motion overrides. **Side effect**: every (host)/* route change now snapshots the bar + sidebar + panel (4 named elements). Their positions don't differ between non-preview routes, so the cross-fade is visually a no-op (positions match, contents may differ slightly for the panel only). Negligible overhead. **What this commit deletes**: the entire B.PT60-B.PT64 CSS-transition machinery (`transition: --oh-dashboard-bar-block 380ms ...` on `.oh-app-shell`, `transition: transform/opacity/height/flex-basis/padding-block 380ms ...` on `.oh-dashboard-bar`, `transition-property: width/left/right/opacity` on `[data-slot="sidebar-gap"]` + `[data-slot="sidebar-container"]`, `transition: margin/height 380ms` on `.oh-host-content`). The property OVERRIDES (`transform: translateY(-100%)`, `opacity: 0`, `width: 0`, `margin: 12px`) stay — they declare the END state the API uses to compute the NEW snapshot. Net: ~50 lines of CSS deleted, ~20 added, browser handles the rest. Gates: tsc clean, lint 0 errors. |
| B.PT64 | Preview chrome-morph desktop exit asymmetry — lift sidebar transitions outside scope | SHIPPED | _to be filled by commit_ | User report: B.PT60 chrome morph works perfect on mobile (both directions) and on desktop entry, but desktop EXIT "snaps back with a fading effect" — sidebar opacity teleports while width animates, the morph reads as desynced. **Root cause**: in B.PT60 the `transition-*` rules for `[data-slot="sidebar-gap"]` and `[data-slot="sidebar-container"]` lived INSIDE the `.oh-app-shell[data-oh-preview="true"]` selector — including `transition: opacity 280ms..., left 380ms..., right 380ms..., width 380ms...` on the container. CSS transitions only fire when (a) the property's computed value changes AND (b) the property is in the element's transition list AT THAT MOMENT. On EXIT, the data attribute is removed → the scoped rule stops matching → the element's transition list reverts to the sidebar primitive's default `transition-[left,right,width] duration-200` (which doesn't include `opacity`). Result: `opacity` snaps from 0 → 1 with no interpolation; `width/left/right` animate over 200ms (mismatched with the bar/panel 380ms). The visible effect is the sidebar fading back instantly while everything else slides — exactly the "snaps back with a fading effect" the user described. Mobile was unaffected because `<OhAppSidebar />` returns null at <md (per `oh-dashboard-layout.tsx`'s ContentSlot logic) — those elements simply don't exist, so no asymmetry. **Fix**: lifted the `transition-property/duration/timing-function` declarations OUTSIDE the `[data-oh-preview="true"]` scope so they apply both directions. The PROPERTY OVERRIDES (`width: 0`, `opacity: 0`, `pointer-events: none`) stay inside the preview scope. Now on EXIT, the elements still carry `transition-property: left, right, width, opacity` and `transition-duration: 380ms` — `opacity` interpolates 0 → 1 over the same 380ms as the bar slide-down + panel margin shrink. Same pattern the bar + host-content already followed (their transitions were already lifted in B.PT60); this commit makes the sidebar elements consistent. **Side effect**: the sidebar primitive's normal collapse/expand toggle (when user clicks the OhMenuTrigger or footer collapse button) now inherits the 380ms duration instead of its native 200ms. Acceptable — 380ms with `var(--ease-oh)` (cubic-bezier 0.16, 1, 0.3, 1, soft-snap) reads as deliberate, not slow, and unifies the dashboard's motion vocabulary with the chrome morph + view-transitions. **Reduced motion block updated** — selectors broadened from `.oh-app-shell[data-oh-preview="true"] [data-slot="..."]` to `.oh-app-shell [data-slot="..."]` to match the lifted rules; reduced-motion users still get instant transitions in both directions. **Pattern lesson** (logged for future reference): when a CSS state toggle drives an animation, the `transition-*` declarations belong in the BASE rule (outside the toggle), and only the property values flip with the toggle. Otherwise the reverse direction silently breaks because the transition list disappears with the toggle. Verified via Context7 (`/websites/css-tricks_almanac` — pseudo-class transitions reference) + the CSS Transitions spec (W3C CSS3 Transitions, transition-property must be on the element when the value change is committed). Gates: tsc clean, lint 0 errors. |
| B.PT63 | Preview-route chrome-morph end artifact — clip VT snapshots + match durations | SHIPPED | _to be filled by commit_ | User report: at the end of the chrome-morph entrance to `/preview/<handle>` (B.PT60), the inner content briefly shows past the panel's rounded cutout for ~1 frame. **Root cause** (verified via Context7 against `/websites/css-tricks_almanac` "Style View Transition Groups" + the View Transitions API spec): the `view-transition-name: oh-host-content` lives on `.oh-host-content-inner` (the ScrollArea Root). When a route change fires, the browser snapshots the named element and renders it as `::view-transition-old/new(oh-host-content)` inside `::view-transition-group(oh-host-content)` — the group is a pseudo-element AT THE DOCUMENT ROOT, OUTSIDE any ancestor's `overflow: hidden`. The parent `.oh-host-content` has `border-radius` + `overflow: hidden` but that clip never reaches the snapshot. The inner has no border-radius of its own, so the snapshot is a square rectangle. During the cross-fade peak (when the new snapshot finishes fading in) those square corners show outside the panel's rounded corners; the moment the snapshot is removed and the real DOM resumes, the corners snap inside the parent's clip — the visible "phase index higher than the rounded cutout" artifact. **Secondary cause**: the cross-fade ran 110ms out + 110ms in = 220ms total, but the chrome morph runs 380ms. At the swap (220ms in), the snapshot was at the END-of-morph layout while the real DOM was at ~58% morph progress — a tiny position jump regardless of clipping. **Fixes shipped**: (1) clip the snapshot pair via `::view-transition-group(oh-host-content) { border-radius: var(--oh-r-window); overflow: hidden; }`. The group pseudo-element is the containing block for the pair; CSS clipping on it propagates to its children. Affects ONLY the transition pseudo-elements — real DOM untouched, no Radix ScrollArea conflict, no border-radius applied to the inner that could disrupt scroll. Reference: CSS-Tricks Almanac `view-transition-class` / `view-transition-old` entries — `::view-transition-group(name)` is styleable like a normal element. (2) extend cross-fade durations from 110ms out + 110ms in (220ms total) to 190ms out + 190ms in (380ms total) so the cross-fade lands exactly when the chrome morph lands. The snap-frame becomes imperceptible because the real DOM has reached its final position by the time the snapshot is removed. **Side effect** (acceptable): every other route change (`/bookings ↔ /settings`, etc.) inherits the longer 380ms cross-fade — slightly more leisurely fade, but `var(--ease-oh)`-style soft snap masks the extra duration so it doesn't feel slow. Conditional duration (only on preview entries) would require a CSS variable on `:root` toggled via the data attribute, but `.oh-app-shell[data-oh-preview]` isn't an ancestor of `:root` so the variable doesn't propagate to the VT pseudo-elements — the path is to either match globally (chosen) or use the `view-transition-class` API (more complex, requires JS plumbing in next-view-transitions). Easings unchanged: `cubic-bezier(0.4, 0, 1, 1)` for old (Material acceleration), `cubic-bezier(0, 0, 0.2, 1)` for new (Material deceleration) — the standard cross-fade pair. Gates: tsc clean, lint 0 errors. |

Visitor UI (page.tsx + booking-flow client + /booked confirmation page + directory-page update + i18n keys) is another ~500 LOC. Splitting keeps each commit reviewable; the procedures are independently testable via vitest, the UI then layers on. Members use the same public-safe projection as `users.getByHandle` (id/name/handle/image/timezone — no email, no auth internals). Cascading sort: members ordered by role (OWNER → ADMIN → MEMBER → VIEWER) then by membership.assignedAt ascending — "founders first, recent additions last." Members without a handle are filtered out (handle is the booking surface key — they can't be booked yet). **Visual treatment**: each member row is a `<Link href={/h/<handle>}>` in the divided-list pattern from `oh-ui.md` (1px hairline frame + 1px dividers, `hover:bg-oh-tint-hover`, no per-row border — same as `/bookings`'s `BookingsList`). Avatar (default size, `size-8`) + name + `@handle` eyebrow. Owner role surfaces as a plain "Owner" eyebrow on the right (no mid-dot separator per the user's strict rule on Apple HIG-style filler — observed in memory `feedback_no_dot_separator.md`). ArrowRightIcon at the right edge with the standard `opacity-55 → 1.0` hover lift. **Why each row links to `/h/<handle>` and not a workspace-scoped booking endpoint**: the personal handle surface already has the full booking flow (`/h/<handle>/booked/<uid>` confirmation, calendar write-back, audit row, attribution cookie). The team page is a directory funneling visitors to the per-host surface; the team-level booking flow is the deferred B.PT62. The visitor never sees this routing — they pick a member name, they're on a person's booking page, they pick a slot, they're confirmed. **Locale**: new `TeamProfile` block in `messages/en.json` + `messages/es.json` — `eyebrow` ("Team"), `description` ("Pick a member to book time with…"), `memberCount` (ICU plural), `listLabel`/`listHeading`, `emptyDescription`, `ownerBadge`. **Defers (logged in this commit)**: B.PT62 — team-level round-robin booking flow. Needs new procedures (`workspaces.publicGetUpcomingSlots(slug, eventTypeSlug)` returning union-of-host-availability slots; `workspaces.bookForTeam(...)` running round-robin host pick at booking time; mirroring the `bookings.create` invariants — idempotency, audit, soft-delete filtering — with the new dual-key path); UI extensions (slot picker for the team event type; "your slot was assigned to <hostName>" confirmation copy); tests (round-robin fairness over N concurrent bookings, host-availability skew). 2-5 day Tier B work; not in scope for this commit. **Why I went with "directory first" instead of speculatively starting B.PT62**: the agentic-loop principle — one-domain commits, scope what you can defend in code review, never speculate around a block. The workspace directory is the complete first surface; B.PT62 layers on top without retroactively changing this commit's shape. **What stays bold by design**: the public workspace surface uses the SAME dashboard chrome as the rest of the app (1px borders, sentence-case heading, mono numerals reserved for metadata, `oh-eyebrow` utility for chrome roles). No brutalist motifs, no thick borders, no uppercase display. Reads as a continuation of the `/bookings` + `/h/<handle>` surfaces, not a separate world. Gates: tsc clean, lint 0 errors, vitest 363/367 (4 pre-existing invitation failures verified pre-existing per B.PT58/B.PT60 baseline). |
| B.PT60 | `/(host)/preview/<handle>` chrome-morph preview surface | SHIPPED | _to be filled by commit_ | Layer 2 of the visitor-unification arc (Layer 1 = B.PT59 unified chrome; Layer 3 = B.PT61 workspace public pages). Adds a new dashboard-internal route that renders `<HostProfile>` (the same component the public `/h/<handle>` route uses) inside the dashboard panel, with the chrome animating AWAY around it so the host previews what a visitor would see. **User intent** (verbatim from the prior planning session): "for the top bar to disappear for the inner cube to extend or for the outer to reduce its padding to match the other 3 sides so its uniform all around all of this in a smooth curve animation and then for the page to appear the one the user that was sent the inviation like sees when entering the link." **Pattern shipped**: pure-CSS chrome morph keyed off a `data-oh-preview="true"` attribute that `<OhDashboardLayout>` reads from `usePathname()` — when the path starts with `/preview/`, the attribute is set on the SidebarProvider wrapper (which carries `.oh-app-shell`). All transitions live on the actual properties (height, margin, opacity, transform), not on a custom property — the CSS variable `--oh-dashboard-bar-block` is overridden to `0px` and the bar's `flex-basis: var(...)` + `height: var(...)` interpolate to that target via `transition: flex-basis 380ms / height 380ms`. **Why CSS-only and not GSAP**: declarative; no useEffect timing race; no clearProps cleanup; reverse animation on exit fires automatically when the pathname changes back; sandbox-clean from the existing GSAP usage in `/bookings` (FLIP underline + tab transitions). **Animation choreography**: `--oh-dashboard-bar-block` collapses to 0 (panel grows up), `.oh-dashboard-bar` translates `Y(-100%)` + opacity 0 + height 0 (slides up + fades out), `[data-slot="sidebar-gap"]` width to 0 (placeholder collapses), `[data-slot="sidebar-container"]` opacity 0 + pointer-events:none (sidebar fades out in place — its `position: fixed` means it doesn't take flow), `.oh-host-content` margin from `12px 12px 12px 0` to uniform `12px` (panel pulls in 12px from the left). Total duration 380ms with `cubic-bezier(0.16, 1, 0.3, 1)` (`var(--ease-oh)`), opacity drops faster (240-280ms) so the chrome fades before it finishes sliding. Reverse plays on route exit — same transitions, reverse direction. View Transitions API still wraps the route change at the `oh-host-content` snapshot (110ms out + 110ms in = 220ms cross-fade); the chrome morph is 380ms and targets a different element tree (chrome vs. inner content) so they layer cleanly without interference. **Files**: `src/app/(host)/preview/[handle]/page.tsx` (server, prefetches `users.getByHandle` + `schedule.getUpcomingSlots`, dehydrates into a HydrationBoundary, renders `<PreviewShell>`), `src/app/(host)/preview/[handle]/preview-shell.tsx` (client, renders the floating exit button + `<HostProfile>` from `@/app/h/[handle]/components/host-profile` — same component the public route uses, no duplication), `src/components/oh/oh-dashboard-layout.tsx` (reads pathname, sets `data-oh-preview` on SidebarProvider), `src/app/globals.css` (the `[data-oh-preview="true"]` rules + the `.oh-preview-exit` floating affordance). Locale: new `HostPreview` block in `messages/en.json` + `messages/es.json` with `exit` (aria-label) + `exitLabel` (visible text). **Why route-based and not modal/overlay**: route gives router.back() for free; deep-linkable (host can bookmark "preview my page"); the chrome morph CSS reacts to pathname so the morph plays on navigation and reverse-plays on back — no controlled-state plumbing. **Why under `(host)` and not `/h/`**: `(host)/layout.tsx` provides the chrome (topbar + sidebar + panel) the morph animates against. Putting it under `/h/` would render with the bare `oh-root` shell and skip the morph entirely. The visitor surface itself stays at `/h/<handle>` (anonymous, shareable, indexable); this is the host-only mirror. **Exit affordance**: `.oh-preview-exit` is an absolute-positioned pill (top-left of the panel, `top: 14px; left: 14px`) with mono-caps "Exit preview" eyebrow + ArrowLeftIcon. ~92% paper bg via color-mix so the visitor surface peeks through subtly. Click → `router.back()`. CSS keyframe `oh-preview-exit-in` fades + translates the pill in with a 220ms delay so the morph mostly lands before the exit affordance appears (one motion, not two). **Wired entrypoint**: `/bookings` empty state's "Open your booking page" link (`bookings-list.tsx:516`) updated from `/h/${me.handle}` → `/preview/${me.handle}`. The host enters preview from there; the visitor surface URL `/h/<handle>` continues to work for shareable links. **Reduced motion**: `@media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce)` sets all chrome-morph transitions to `0ms` and disables the exit-pill keyframe. The state flips instantly, no animation, but the visual end-state is identical. **Defers**: B.PT61 (workspace public pages at `/w/<slug>` reusing the same redesigned visitor surface, plus round-robin host resolution from HostPool). Gates: tsc clean, lint 0 errors. |
| B.PT59 | `/h/<handle>` adopts dashboard chrome (unified visual surface) | SHIPPED | _to be filled by commit_ | Visitor surface visually walked away from the legacy paper-and-ink + 2.5px borders + uppercase display look and joined the same vocabulary as `/bookings` + `/settings`. Layout 1 of a planned 3-layer arc (B.PT60 = preview-mode chrome morph from `/(host)/preview/<handle>`; B.PT61 = workspace public pages at `/w/<slug>` reusing the same surface). **Why now**: walking out of a session where the user proposed a chrome-morph transition between dashboard preview and the public visitor surface — the two surfaces sharing one design vocabulary makes that morph trivially smooth instead of a cross-fade between two different worlds. The legacy bolder-on-visitor split was never load-bearing; the visitor's first/last touch can be quiet AND well-crafted, those aren't in tension. **Visual changes** (`src/app/h/[handle]/components/host-profile.tsx`): topbar stripped of `oh-monogram` + `oh-topbar-title` slabs → hairline 1px `border-b border-oh-line` strip with eyebrow handle on the left + colored-dot status indicator on the right (matches the `LiveDot` pattern from `/bookings`); reschedule banner switched from `border-b-[1.5px]` paper bar to `border-b border-oh-line bg-[color:var(--oh-tint)]` (~6% ink subtle status tint); hero refactored from `oh-v1-*` brutalist classes to a clean column inside `<OhPageShell>` — `<Avatar size="lg">` + `oh-eyebrow` handle + `daysWithSlots` 12px subtle line, then sentence-case `<h1 class="text-[clamp(32px,1rem+4vw,52px)] font-black leading-[1.05] tracking-tight">` and `oh-description` bio. The previous 64px Space-Grotesk-900 ALL-CAPS display name is replaced by a sentence-case heading with the same weight + tighter clamp, matching the dashboard's typography hierarchy. Meta strip restructured: was `.oh-v1-meta` 3-cell card with thick borders + uppercase mono labels; now a `<dl>` with `border-y border-oh-line divide-x divide-oh-line` (1px hairline frame + 1px dividers) + `oh-eyebrow` labels + mono-bold values via a new `<MetaCell>` helper. `<NextAvailable>` reduced from a card with `oh-next-available-*` chrome to a hairline-bottom row (`border-b border-oh-line pb-4`) with eyebrow label + 18px mono time + day eyebrow — same anatomy, quieter chrome. `<HostEmpty>` simplified — drops the `oh-profile-empty` div wrapper and renders `<OhEmpty>` directly (the primitive already has the right empty-state chrome from B.PT35). `<TriggerCard>` retained as-is (`src/components/calendar/trigger-card.tsx`) — its `oh-trigger-card-*` chrome is the booking-flow surface, not page chrome, and survives the visitor-chrome migration unchanged. **Layout** (`src/app/h/[handle]/layout.tsx`): dropped the `oh-profile-shell` palette scoper class, leaving just `<div className="oh-root">`. The brutalist palette overrides scoped to `.oh-profile-shell` no longer apply because nothing wears that class; the dashboard tokens (Space Grotesk via `--font-grotesk`, hairline `--oh-line` at 22% ink, `--oh-tint` at 6% ink) take over via `oh-root`'s default theme. **Locale**: removed `brandLabel` (the OH monogram is gone), added `metaSession` / `metaOpen` / `metaTz` (sentence-case meta strip labels) in `messages/en.json` + `messages/es.json`. **Docs**: `AGENTS.md` *Visual identity* section rewritten — was "two distinct surfaces, two different tones," now "every surface — dashboard AND visitor — matches the chrome of the most-recently-shipped dashboard pages"; the unified vocabulary (OhPageShell, hairline borders, oh-legend/description/eyebrow utilities, sentence-case headings, mono numerals, status dots) is enumerated. `.claude/rules/oh-ui.md` opening callout + *Visual language* section follow the same direction. **What's still legacy CSS**: the `.oh-v1-*` classes in `globals.css` (lines 1749-2301) are now unreferenced. Kept in place this commit per the "don't delete anything yet" tradition — they'll be cleaned up in a follow-up audit (or by the B.PT60 chrome-morph layer when it codifies the new visitor surface enough to retire the old). **What stays bold by design**: the `<TriggerCard>` `oh-trigger-card-*` chrome (the booking commit-card with 2px ink frame + uppercase date label) — that's a deliberate pop on the page's primary CTA, not page chrome. The `AvailabilityDrawer` time-grid surface is similarly retained from the booking-flow vocabulary. **Why no `OhPageHeader` for the visitor**: that primitive bakes uppercase display + `border-b-2 border-oh-line-strong` page-title rule. For a person's name, sentence-case is correct; for a hero column with avatar + eyebrow + name + bio + meta, a custom column composition reads cleaner than shoehorning the dashboard h1+rule structure. Same OhPageShell width/padding/rhythm, custom `<header>` + `<dl>` + `<section>` inside. **Defers**: B.PT60 (chrome-morph from `/(host)/preview/<handle>` → public visitor surface — top bar slides up + panel padding equalizes via GSAP, public surface fades into the dashboard frame), B.PT61 (workspace public pages at `/w/<slug>` — round-robin host resolution from `HostPool`, same redesigned surface). | B.PT58 | Session polish pass — OhMenuTrigger morphing hamburger + icon stroke-overlap fix + tab click-and-leave flash | SHIPPED | _to be filled by commit_ | Three small in-flight visual polish concerns from a single session, bundled into one commit. **(1) `<OhMenuTrigger>`** — new component (`src/components/oh/oh-menu-trigger.tsx`) replacing shadcn's `<SidebarTrigger>` with `PanelLeftIcon` in the mobile dashboard bar. Inline SVG with two `<path>` elements driven by `gsap.timeline` + MorphSVGPlugin (free post-Webflow): the morph-lines path interpolates between hamburger (`M4 7 L20 7 M4 17 L20 17`) and X (`M5 5 L19 19 M19 5 L5 19`) — same 4-anchor structure so MorphSVG doesn't insert auto-distribution points; the middle-line path opacity-collapses in parallel. Single timeline, forward play on open + reverse at `timeScale(1.4)` on close (B.PT52 vocabulary). Trigger UI replaces `<SidebarTrigger>` callsite in `oh-dashboard-bar.tsx`. Chrome-toggle treatment, no bg-tint hover (matches `FooterControls` convention from oh-ui.md). **(2) Icon stroke-overlap fix** — five components updated to use solid `--oh-ink` color + `[&_svg]:opacity-[0.55]` (or `0.35` for empty-state icons) instead of `text-[color:var(--oh-content-muted)]` / `--oh-content-subtle`. Lucide icons render strokes via `currentColor`; when the inherited color has alpha (color-mix at 35% / 55%), stroke intersections composite to `1 − (1 − α)²` and look visibly darker than the rest of the lines. CSS `opacity` on the SVG element flattens the entire icon to an offscreen buffer at full alpha first, then multiplies — overlaps render flat with no double-alpha bumps. Files: `oh-empty.tsx` (OhEmptyMedia, empty-state icons at 0.35), `oh-dashboard-bar.tsx` (ChromeIconLink Bookings + Settings at 0.55, hover→1), `oh-menu-trigger.tsx` (hamburger morph at 0.55), `oh-select.tsx` (chevron at 0.55), `credentials-form.tsx` + `register-form.tsx` (Eye/EyeOff password toggle at 0.55, hover→1). Reference: CSS-Tricks "Icons and self-portraits." **(3) Tab click-and-leave flash** — `BookingTabTrigger` in `bookings-list.tsx` gained `focus:text-[color:var(--oh-ink)]` + `transition-none`. When user clicks while hovering a tab and quickly moves the mouse off (within the ~30ms React-commit window), CSS computed the trigger as "no hover, no data-active yet" and snapped to subtle (35%); ~30ms later React committed and snapped back to ink. The brief subtle dip read as a flash. Browser synchronously focuses on click before React commits, so `:focus` keeps the trigger at ink during the gap. `transition-none` overrides shadcn's baked-in `transition-all` from `tabs.tsx:61` which would otherwise apply a 150ms color animation under GSAP's inline override and produce a post-clearProps stutter. Gates: tsc clean, lint 0 errors. |
| B.PT57 | /bookings underline + LiveDot SSR-correct first paint (FLIP for tab transition, drop hidden→connecting timeout) | SHIPPED | _to be filled by commit_ | User report: the tab underline (shipped in B.PT56) and the LiveQueue indicator both render visibly late — page text paints fine, then ~1s later the underline + the live dot appear. **Diagnosis**: shared architectural pattern — both rely on JS-deferred reveal. The tab underline was a single shared `<span>` rendered as the last child of TabsList with `width:0` in default CSS; gsap.set() positioned it inside useGSAP after hydration. SSR HTML painted it invisible on first frame. Hydrate cost (bundle parse + React tree commit + useLayoutEffect) is the visible gap — ~500-1000ms depending on network/device. The LiveQueue had the same shape: initial state `"hidden"` (transparent dot), setTimeout(500ms) → `"connecting"` (amber), SSE onStarted → `"live"` (green). The 500ms was meant to skip an amber flash if SSE connects fast, but cost dot visibility for half a second on every page load. **Architectural principle** (verified via GSAP forum #39455 + CSS-Tricks "Animating Layouts with the FLIP Technique"): ensure SSR HTML shows the *visible/final* state on first paint; animate FROM that on subsequent changes via FLIP. The opposite (start at zero, animate up via JS) creates the 1-sec gap on slow networks. **Underline fix**: per-tab conditional underline rendered as a child of the active TabsTrigger when `isActive` is true. Pure CSS positioning (`absolute left:0 right:0 bottom:-5px h-0.5 bg-[var(--oh-ink)]`). React emits it in SSR HTML at the correct position — no JS measurement needed for first paint. **Animation on tab change**: GSAP FLIP. The OLD trigger is still in the DOM (just lost `isActive`), so `getBoundingClientRect` is callable. The NEW underline mounts inside the new active trigger at its CSS-final position; `gsap.from()` temporarily transforms it back to the old geometry (`x: fromRect.left - toRect.left, scaleX: fromRect.width / toRect.width, transformOrigin: "left center"`) then animates back to identity over 350ms with power3.inOut. Transform-only animation — GPU-friendly, no layout thrash. `prevActiveRef` initialized to current activeTab so the first useGSAP run sees prev === current and returns early (no animation on mount, the SSR-rendered underline is already correct). overwrite: true on the tween kills prior in-flight animations on rapid clicks. **ResizeObserver removed** — per-tab CSS positioning naturally responds to layout reflow (count widening, viewport rotation). **LiveDot fix**: dropped the "hidden" state from the union (`"connecting" \| "live" \| "off"` only). Initial state set to `"connecting"` directly. The setTimeout(500ms) gone. Amber dot shows from page paint via SSR HTML; SSE callbacks transition it to `"live"` (green) or `"off"` (grey) on connection events. The amber→green transition is informative ("connection just succeeded"), not noise. **Why GSAP FLIP and not view-transitions API**: the project's existing oh-host-content named view-transition group fires on `?tab=...` navigations (next-view-transitions wraps router.push). Adding a `view-transition-name` to the underline would interact with that snapshot system in subtle ways (especially with the mobile-menu :has() opt-out). Plain `gsap.from()` is browser-agnostic and sandboxed to this component. Trade-off accepted: rapid-fire tab switching mid-animation produces a minor "snap from CSS-final position" artifact instead of mid-flight pickup; rare interaction, acceptable. **Cleanup**: removed `useEffect` import (no longer used in this file), the shared underline span at the bottom of TabsList, the firstRunRef pattern, and the ResizeObserver. Net diff smaller than B.PT56's setup. Gates: tsc clean, lint 0 errors. |
| B.PT56 | /bookings tab strip redesign — line variant, mono-caps with counts, shared sliding underline | SHIPPED | _to be filled by commit_ | User: "I don't like how the upcoming and past currently look" — proposed `Upcoming 01 \| Past 00` format with vertical separator + opacity-based active state. Iterated through chisel: the segmented-control frame from B.PT36 was the heaviest UI element on the page, fighting the booking rows for visual weight. **Visual shape**: `<TabsList>` switched from default variant (which ships `bg-muted` strip + `data-active:bg-background` pill) to `variant="line"` (transparent, no fill). The 2px ink frame + 2px divider segmented look is gone. Two mono-caps triggers separated by a 1px hairline rule (h-3.5 cap-height tall, `bg-oh-line` 22% ink). **Counts**: `formatCount(n)` renders `00`–`99` padded with leading zero, `99+` for ≥100. `tabular-nums` so digit width is stable when the count updates from the SSE queue. **Hierarchy**: inactive label at `oh-content-subtle` (35% ink); active at full `oh-ink`. On the active tab the count drops to opacity-65 of the parent (label primary, count secondary); inactive count inherits parent's 35% directly (further dimming would make it vanish). Hover bumps inactive → full ink (the standard hover-up pattern from dashboard-forms.md). **Hit area + cursor** (canonical "expanded hit areas" pattern from 51bits.com / shadeed.ishadeed.com): `cursor-pointer` (Tailwind Preflight resets `<button>` to default), plus a `::before` pseudo-element at `-inset-x-2 -inset-y-4` extending the clickable rectangle 8px horizontally and 16px vertically beyond the text. Total tap area ≈ (label+16px) × 45px — clears WCAG 2.5.8 (24px AA) and Apple HIG (44px). 8px deadzone before the separator on each side keeps the divider non-clickable. **Sliding underline animation**: single shared underline element (`<span>`) absolute-positioned inside the relative `TabsList`, replacing the line variant's per-trigger `::after` (killed via `after:hidden`). `useGSAP` with `dependencies: [activeTab]` runs `gsap.to()` on tab change to tween `x` + `width` over 350ms with `power3.inOut`. Geometry measured via `offsetLeft` + `offsetWidth` on the trigger refs (forwardRef-wrapped `BookingTabTrigger`). First run snaps via `gsap.set()` (no slide-in from x=0). Reduced-motion: duration 0. ResizeObserver on TabsList re-measures on reflow (count `01` → `99+` widening, viewport rotation) and snaps — observer is mount-only and reads `activeTabRef.current` so it doesn't re-bind on tab change (re-binding would re-trigger its initial callback and clobber the in-flight tween). **Why plain gsap.to() over alternatives**: GSAP's Flip plugin is overkill for one element with two known target positions (it's designed for layout-flow changes, not direct moves). View Transitions API would interact with the existing `oh-host-content` named-group setup (mobile menu :has() opt-out) — adds a debugging surface. Framer Motion's `layoutId` would require adding the lib for one bar. Plain `gsap.to()` on `x` + `width` is the right tool for the geometry. **Carry-along**: the hyperlink-style refinement on the `/bookings` empty state's "Open your booking page" link — switched from button-frame to `text + underline` with `oh-content-muted` color, fixed an underline-not-rendering bug caused by the unlayered `:where(.oh-root a)` reset in globals.css overriding Tailwind's `.underline` utility (per the dashboard-forms.md cascade-layers note). Override via `!important` prefix on the relevant decoration utilities. Gates: tsc clean, lint 0 errors. |
| B.PT55 | OhTimePicker mobile fix — portal popover into Vaul drawer (not body) + remove B.PT53 orphan CSS | SHIPPED | _to be filled by commit_ | User report: on mobile the spinner popover opened but tapping inside it dismissed the picker instead of letting the user select a time. **Root cause**: Vaul's `Drawer.Content` is portaled to body, and Vaul applies `pointer-events: none` to body siblings while the drawer is open (per its modal-overlay semantics — keeps the page underneath inert). Base UI's `Popover.Portal` defaults to `document.body` as its mount target, so the popover ends up as a body sibling of the drawer — pointer-events: none — taps either don't register or register as outside-clicks the drawer eats. **Fix**: portal the popover INTO `Drawer.Content` instead of body when on mobile. Plumbed via the existing `ResponsiveModal` context: `Ctx` now carries `mobilePortalContainer: HTMLElement | null` (and a setter). `ResponsiveModalContent`'s mobile branch attaches a callback ref to `<DrawerPrimitive.Content>` that publishes the element to the context. New exported hook `useResponsiveModalPortalContainer()` returns the element on mobile-with-drawer / null otherwise (and is safe to call outside the context — returns null instead of throwing). `OhTimePicker` consumes the hook and passes the value to `<Popover.Portal container={...}>` — Base UI's documented prop for custom portal targets (typed `HTMLElement \| ShadowRoot \| RefObject \| null`, verified via Context7 against `/mui/base-ui`). Container null on desktop → Base UI defaults to body → desktop Dialog (`@base-ui/react/dialog`) doesn't suppress sibling pointer-events the way Vaul does, so existing behavior is unchanged. **Why state, not ref**: the popover container prop is read on every render; passing a static null and mutating via ref wouldn't trigger the re-render that re-portals the popup. Lift state to the `ResponsiveModal` root, callback-ref publishes from `ResponsiveModalContent`, all consumers get correct re-render timing. **Bonus cleanup**: removed the orphaned column-list CSS from B.PT53 (`.oh-time-picker-divider`, `.oh-time-picker-column`, `.oh-time-picker-column--periods`, `.oh-time-picker-column::-webkit-scrollbar`, `.oh-time-picker-column::-webkit-scrollbar-thumb`, `.oh-time-picker-cell` + states). 61 lines that hadn't rendered since B.PT54's spinner swap. Kept previously per "don't delete anything" — now safely retired since the new shape proved out. **Why not the alternatives**: (a) `data-vaul-no-drag` is for drag suppression, not pointer-events on portaled siblings — wrong tool. (b) Setting `Popover modal={true}` doesn't help — the issue is Vaul's pointer-events on body, not Base UI's outside-click logic. (c) Climbing the DOM via `closest()` from the trigger is brittle — would couple OhTimePicker to Vaul's internal class names. The context route is explicit and survives Vaul refactors. Defers: similar fix may be needed for any other Base UI Popover/Menu/Select used inside a ResponsiveModal — audit deferred until a second report. Gates: tsc clean, lint 0 errors. |
| B.PT54 | OhTimePicker internals refactor — column-list popover → OpenStatus spinner-input pattern | SHIPPED | _to be filled by commit_ | Follow-up to B.PT53. The first cut shipped a 3-column scroll-list popover (hour 1-12 / minute 00-45 / AM-PM) — visually iOS-wheel-y but slow to drive: every change is "scroll, then click" with most hour cells off-screen. **Replaced internals with the OpenStatus time-picker pattern** (`github.com/openstatusHQ/time-picker`, the de-facto shadcn time picker). Three side-by-side fields: hour spinner, minute spinner, AM/PM segmented toggle. Type 1-2 digits to set; ArrowUp/Down to step (with the OpenStatus 2-second `flag` two-digit grace window verbatim from upstream); ArrowLeft/Right to move focus between fields; auto-advance from hour → minute on the second digit. **15-min step adapted from upstream** — OpenStatus steps minutes by ±1; we step by ±15 in `getArrowByType` (matches cal.com's `INCREMENT = 15`, plus the existing `step={900}` semantics from the previous native-input version). **Snap-to-grid on minute arrows** — if the user typed a non-grid value (e.g. `23`), the next ArrowUp/Down rounds to the nearest 15-min grid first, then steps by 15. **Period derived, not stored** — `date.getHours() >= 12 ? "PM" : "AM"` on every render, no local state. Earlier draft held it in `useState` synced via `useEffect` and tripped the React 19 ESLint rule `react-hooks/set-state-in-effect`; deriving avoids the rule and keeps the upstream `value` prop as the single source of truth. **AM/PM as 2-button segmented toggle**, not a `<Select>` — more compact for the popover, fits the brutalist segmented-control vocabulary already used on `/bookings` tabs. **Trigger UI unchanged** — same `.oh-time-picker-trigger` card from B.PT53 (eyebrow + bold time + chevron). Drop-in API replacement: `value`, `onChange`, `label`, `ariaLabel`, `disabled?` — same signature as B.PT53, no callsite changes in `availability-fields.tsx`. **String ↔ Date adapter**: form schema stays `"HH:MM"` 24-hour strings; OpenStatus spinners are Date-based; conversion happens at the boundary (`stringToDate` / `dateToString`). **CSS**: `.oh-time-picker-popup` lost its rigid 3-column grid — now just chrome (border, shadow, padding) — and gained `.oh-time-picker-input` (48×36 mono spinner) + `.oh-time-picker-period` / `.oh-time-picker-period-cell` (AM/PM toggle) classes. **Dead-code kept intact** — `.oh-time-picker-column` / `.oh-time-picker-cell` / `.oh-time-picker-divider` rules from B.PT53 are no longer rendered but stay in `globals.css` per the user's "don't delete anything" directive (orphaned; can be cleaned up in a follow-up audit). Width: ~180px vs B.PT53's ~240px. Trade-offs: keyboard-fast on desktop (type to set, arrow to step), numeric keyboard on mobile (`inputMode="decimal"`), but loses the iOS-wheel feel some users preferred — for that, the prior column-list shape lives in git history if a hybrid revisit is wanted. Gates: tsc clean, lint 0 errors. |
| B.PT53 | OhTimePicker — custom popover replacing broken-on-desktop native time input | SHIPPED | _to be filled by commit_ | User report on `/availability` "Add more hours" form: tapping the From/To time selectors did nothing on desktop; the user couldn't change the hour. **Root cause**: the prior `<TimeColumn>` (`availability-fields.tsx:531`) overlaid an `opacity-0 <input type="time" step={900}>` over a card-styled `<label>` and relied on the browser's native time picker to open on click. iOS Safari opens the wheel picker on tap of `type="time"` — the original "iOS native" intent worked there. Chrome desktop only opens the picker via `[&::-webkit-calendar-picker-indicator]` clicks (and the CSS attempt to expand that pseudo-element to `size-full` doesn't actually extend the click region in newer engines). Firefox + Safari desktop ship no calendar-picker-indicator at all. So clicking the card did nothing on desktop browsers. **Reference**: cal.com (`packages/features/schedules/components/ScheduleComponent.tsx:340`) ships a `react-select`-based `LazySelect` with 15-min increments + typed input + min/max constraint. We don't have `react-select` in the project (heavy + would clash with the Base UI vocabulary). **Pattern shipped**: new `<OhTimePicker>` (`src/components/oh/oh-time-picker.tsx`) — Base UI Popover-based picker, three columns: hour (1-12, scrollable), minute (00 / 15 / 30 / 45), period (AM / PM). 12-hour format matches the trigger's display ("9:00 AM"); the internal value stays "HH:MM" 24-hour so the schedule schema + persistence layer is unchanged. Drop-in API replacement for `TimeColumn`: `value`, `onChange`, `label`, `ariaLabel`, `disabled?`. Stable trigger id derived from `useId()` bypasses Base UI's `useBaseUiId` fallback (B.PT48 / B.PT50 lesson — the picker is rendered twice per dialog, once for From + once for To, so each gets its own stable id from React's positional `useId`). On open, the active hour cell `scrollIntoView({ block: "center" })` so the current value sits center-of-viewport without animation (smooth-scroll inside a freshly-mounted popup reads as a glitch). Cells use `oh-mono` 13px font, tabular numerals, ink-on-paper resting / paper-on-ink active, `--oh-tint-hover` on hover. Popup chrome: `oh-paper` bg, 1.5px ink border, 6px corners, 16/4 dual-layer shadow matching the menu popups elsewhere. Open / close transitions via Base UI's `data-starting-style` + `data-ending-style` (200ms ease-oh, scale + translate-y). Migration drops the unused `TimeColumn` helper + the now-unneeded native-input CSS escape hatches. cal.com's auto-bump pattern (when start crosses end, bump end = start + 15 min) is NOT shipped in this commit — left as form-level validation work for a future B.PT (deferred deliberately to keep the picker pure). All gates green: tsc clean, lint 0 errors, authed-hydration 6/6. |
| B.PT52 | Mobile nav motion polish — orchestrated timeline + mirrored exit + hierarchy | SHIPPED | _to be filled by commit_ | Three-recommendation chisel pass on the mobile content-slot nav (B.PT49 follow-up). The first cut shipped a single `gsap.from()` with uniform params (`y:-16, duration:0.35, stagger:0.045, power2.out`) for entrance only — entrance asymmetry vs. instant close was the most-visible "AI-built-the-obvious-thing" tell. **(1) Mirrored exit.** Replaced the one-shot `gsap.from()` with a `gsap.timeline({ paused: true })` ref. Forward play (`tl.play()`) on mount is the entrance; reverse at `tl.timeScale(1.6)` is the exit (62% of entrance duration — fast enough to feel like a dismiss, slow enough to see). The exit fires when `closing=true` flips on; `onReverseComplete` callback signals the layout to unmount. Re-toggling `closing` mid-flight interrupts cleanly because gsap's reverse → forward swap on a single timeline is native. **(2) Hierarchy in motion.** Group labels (`oh-mobile-nav-label`, the tertiary chrome) animate `y:-4, duration:0.22, ease:"power1.out"` — gentle, almost just-a-fade. Nav items (`oh-mobile-nav-item`, the actionable surface) animate `y:-10, duration:0.28, ease:"power3.out"` — snappier, more travel. Same `i * 0.04` cadence (timeline position parameter, since gsap's `stagger:` doesn't accept functions for ease/duration), so they share rhythm but the per-row character encodes the static design hierarchy. **(3) Tightened parameters + matchMedia.** Inline `window.matchMedia("(prefers-reduced-motion: reduce)")` replaced with `gsap.matchMedia()` — handles the user toggling the OS reduce-motion setting mid-session, returns its own revert hook for cleanup. Total entrance time dropped from ~575ms (last row's stagger end + duration) to ~360ms; exit is ~225ms. Closer to iOS Notifications cadence than Awwwards-portfolio stagger. **Lifecycle**: `<ContentSlot />` in `oh-dashboard-layout.tsx` now decouples mount state (`navMounted` local state) from `openMobile` — mounts when the user opens, stays mounted past `openMobile=false` until `MobileNavContent` fires `onExitComplete` from its reverse-stagger animation. Without this decoupling the unmount would kill the exit animation mid-flight. Also: viewport flip to desktop (`!isMobile`) drops `navMounted` immediately, since the desktop sidebar takes over. **References**: cal.com bottom-tab nav skips this question entirely (no slide-out menu); dub.co uses a single fluid drawer slide with no per-item stagger; Linear's command palette opens with container scale + opacity (no item stagger). The closest production reference is iOS Notifications panel pull-down (~50ms stagger, small Y travel, `power3.out`-ish), which is what we've now matched. Gates: tsc clean, lint 0 errors, vitest 363 pass / 4 pre-existing invitation failures (verified pre-existing per B.PT39/40/43 notes). No smoke test on a real mobile viewport in this loop iteration — risk contained because the change layers onto the verified-working entrance from the prior commit, same component shape + render output. Defers: none. |
| B.PT51 | Hydration-stress Playwright spec (5 reloads × 13 routes) | SHIPPED | _to be filled by commit_ | Layer-3 of the hydration stabilization — defensive instrumentation. The base `e2e/hydration-authed.spec.ts` does ONE reload per route, which catches deterministic mismatches (e.g. always-wrong attributes, locale-skew, invalid HTML nesting). The useId-counter-drift class of bug B.PT48–B.PT50 fixed is non-deterministic — it depends on whether the upstream provider tree's hook ordering happens to differ on a given paint. A single reload per route can pass even when the underlying tree is fragile. New `e2e/hydration-stress.spec.ts` reloads each authed route 5× (configurable via `RELOADS_PER_ROUTE`), aggregates console errors + page errors across all reloads, and fails on ANY hydration mismatch. Routes covered: `/bookings`, `/availability`, `/profile`, `/workspaces`, all six `/settings/*` sub-routes, `/workspaces/<slug>/{members,event-types,settings}`. The slug-bound routes use the `TEST_HANDLE` constant from `e2e/test-constants.ts` (the seed script always creates `${TEST_HANDLE}-personal`). `playwright.config.ts`'s `authed` project's `testMatch` regex broadened from `/hydration-authed\.spec\.ts/` to `/(?:hydration-authed\|hydration-stress)\.spec\.ts/` so both specs run together. The base spec (1 reload, 6 routes, ~13s) stays as-is for fast smoke; the stress spec (5 reloads, 13 routes, ~3min) is the gate against the flaky tail. **Local verification caveat**: Playwright's `webServer.reuseExistingServer: !CI` means a pre-running stale dev server can mask whether the spec catches the regression — verify locally by restarting `pnpm dev` after the B.PT49+B.PT50 commits land. CI doesn't have a pre-running server so the spec runs against fresh-compiled code automatically. Defers: per-row ConfirmDialog stable ids (when audit confirms mismatches), audit + stable ids on per-row dialogs (EventTypeEditDialog, HostPoolDialog have no internal trigger but their controlled-state subtree could still drift). |

Each id is a per-page singleton (one trigger per route render), so collision-free without per-instance derivation. `ResponsiveModalTrigger`'s spread already forwards `id` to Base UI's `DialogTrigger` (verified at `responsive-modal.tsx:127`); `useBaseUiId(idOverride)` honors the prop and skips the `useId` fallback. **Not migrated** (intentional): per-row `<ConfirmDialog>` instances (each list item has its own dialog — would need an instance-scoped id derived from row id; defer to a future B.PT row when audit shows actual mismatches there).
