| B.PT49 | Migrate `prefs-context` to `useMounted()` (stop upstream useId drift) | SHIPPED | _to be filled by commit_ | Diagnostic follow-on to B.PT48 — surveying every Base UI primitive trigger that auto-generates an id via `useBaseUiId(idProp)`-fallback-to-`useId`, the upstream culprit shifting React 19's `useId` counter between SSR and the first client paint was `OhPrefsProvider` (`src/components/oh/prefs-context.tsx:39-49`). It used the exact `useState(false) + useEffect(() => setMounted(true), [])` pattern AGENTS.md "Never do" forbids, with an `eslint-disable-next-line react-hooks/set-state-in-effect` to bypass the project lint rule. Sits ABOVE the entire dashboard chrome — every Menu/Dialog trigger downstream re-renders when `mounted` flips from `false` → `true`, and any hook ordering inside that re-render that differs from the SSR pass shifts `useId`'s positional counter, producing `base-ui-_R_…` mismatches on the auto-id'd triggers. **Fix**: replace with the canonical `useMounted()` from `src/hooks/use-mounted.ts` (`useSyncExternalStore`-based with an explicit server-snapshot of `false`). Same return value, no useEffect, no setState-in-effect, no lint-disable. SSR + first client paint both see `mounted=false` deterministically; the post-mount upgrade happens through `useSyncExternalStore`'s subscription path which doesn't ripple a re-render through child useId calls in the same way. Lint rule no longer needs the disable; the comment block on the hook explains the why. Bonus: the existing `eslint-disable-next-line` was a code smell that future audits would have flagged. Defers: B.PT50 still needed (per-callsite `id` props on Dialog triggers — defensive belt-and-suspenders even after the upstream is stable, because third-party providers like `<TooltipProvider>` + `<FloatingDelayGroup>` from `@floating-ui/react` may still produce minor counter drift on HMR or future provider upgrades). All gates green: tsc clean, lint 0 errors. |
| B.PT48 | Stable Menu.Trigger ids (fix Base UI useId hydration mismatch on dashboard chrome) | SHIPPED | _to be filled by commit_ | User report: hydration mismatch error on every page load — `<button id="base-ui-_R_1kmitmlb_">` (SSR) vs `<button id="base-ui-_R_d5itmlb_">` (client) on the workspace switcher trigger AND the user-menu trigger. Same shape, different positional `useId` counter values. The mismatch DIDN'T patch up — React said "tree hydrated but some attributes of the server rendered HTML didn't match the client properties." Root cause: Base UI's `Menu.Trigger` calls `useBaseUiId(idProp)` (verified at `node_modules/@base-ui/react/menu/trigger/MenuTrigger.js:67`); when no `id` prop is passed, it falls back to `useId` to generate one. React 19's `useId` returns a positional ID derived from the fiber tree path. The dashboard chrome lives below `<TooltipProvider>` + `<FloatingDelayGroup>` + `<SidebarProvider>` (`useIsMobile` + cookie-driven state) + `<OhPrefsProvider>` (theme + density + typeface state) — the upstream tree has enough state that any small SSR/CSR shape difference shifts `useId`'s counter for downstream consumers. The mismatch surfaced visibly on the two top-level triggers; submenu triggers (theme + language) inside the popup don't fire `useId` on SSR because the popup is portal-mounted only on open, so they were unaffected. **Fix**: pass an explicit `id` prop to both `Menu.Trigger` instances. Per Base UI Menu docs (verified via Context7 `/mui/base-ui` *Multiple Triggers* + *Controlled Mode with Multiple Triggers*) the `id` prop is the documented escape hatch — `useBaseUiId` honors `idOverride` over the auto-generated one. New IDs: `oh-workspace-switcher-trigger` (was just a className on `oh-dashboard-bar.tsx:98`) + `oh-user-menu-trigger` (matching the existing className on `user-menu.tsx:74`). Class selectors and id selectors don't collide in CSS; the existing `.oh-user-menu-trigger { ... }` rules continue to apply. Inline comment block on each callsite explains *why* the `id` is hard-coded — so a future agent doesn't refactor it back to auto-id. Defers: none. The fix doesn't address the underlying useId-counter drift (which would require chasing every upstream provider's hook order); it side-steps the question entirely by giving Base UI a deterministic id to use. Authed-hydration spec 6/6 still green. |
| B.PT47 | Delete route-level `loading.tsx` (no skeleton flash, no "Settings" → "General" title flip) | SHIPPED | _to be filled by commit_ | User report on /settings entry: (1) the page didn't cache like /bookings — every nav flashed the skeleton, (2) the title showed "Settings" then snapped to "General" once the skeleton resolved, (3) "can we apply the same pattern that makes /bookings work everywhere appropriate?" Same root cause B.PT39 surfaced + B.PT40 fixed for /bookings: Next App Router auto-wraps any async `page.tsx` in a Suspense boundary whose fallback is the nearest `loading.tsx`. Four host routes (`/settings`, `/workspaces`, `/profile`, `/availability`) shipped a `loading.tsx` from B.PT24's audit-driven skeleton sweep. Result: every hard refresh + every client-side nav into one of these routes showed the skeleton frame for the duration of the prefetch, even when the cache was warm. The /settings flavor was extra-bad because `loading.tsx` hardcoded `<OhPageHeader title="Settings" />` while `/settings/general/page.tsx` rendered `<OhPageHeader title="General" />` — title flipped Settings → General on every paint. /bookings doesn't have this issue because B.PT39 + B.PT40 deleted its `loading.tsx` and reverted to the upstream "page.tsx awaits prefetch synchronously, no Suspense fallback" shape. **Fix**: delete the four `loading.tsx` files (`/settings`, `/workspaces`, `/profile`, `/availability`). Each route's `page.tsx` already awaits its prefetches at the top synchronously — Next holds the streaming response until the prefetch resolves, then sends complete HTML. On client-side nav, App Router holds the previous page visible (URL changes in address bar + browser shows loading indicator) until the new page is ready — same UX as cal.com (`apps/web/app/(use-page-wrapper)/(main-nav)/...`) and dub.co (`apps/web/app/app.dub.co/(dashboard)/...`), neither of which ship per-route `loading.tsx` files for their dashboard surfaces. TTFB is ~50-200ms longer in dev (the prefetch round-trip blocks), well under the streaming threshold. /bookings keeps its `loading.tsx`-less shape; nothing else changes. Authed-hydration spec 6/6 still green (the spec walks /bookings + the four formerly-loading routes + /settings/billing — none regress since each page.tsx still wraps in HydrationBoundary). Defers: none. |
| B.PT46 | Host shell consistency pass — `<Button>` variants audit + ESLint guard, mobile dashboard-bar centering, bookings list frame cleanup | SHIPPED | _to be filled by commit_ | Three host-chrome concerns from a single design-pass session, all about the dashboard reading consistently against the `oh` aesthetic. **(1) `<Button>` variants audit + lint guard.** User flagged the "Mark done" button on the onboarding "Share your link" step as "doesn't match the other buttons." Root cause: it shipped as `<Button variant="outline">` — shadcn's stock variant (`border-border bg-background hover:bg-muted hover:text-foreground`), not the project's `oh` family. Audit found 12 stock-variant Buttons across the host shell — every one a Cancel / secondary / icon-only. All 12 swapped to `variant="ohGhost"` (paper bg, ink border, ink-invert on hover): `workspace-create-dialog`, `event-type-create-dialog`, `event-type-edit-dialog`, `event-types-panel` (×2), `onboarding-checklist` (×1) in this commit; `invite-member-dialog` (×3) + `members-panel` (×3) landed in earlier commits in the same session. One raw `<button>` (the X-dismiss in the onboarding card) replaced with `<Button variant="ohGhost" size="icon-sm">` matching the canonical close pattern in `responsive-modal.tsx`. Six other raw `<button>` usages kept (segmented tab `<SegButton>`, block chip, day toggle, day-row trigger, sidebar-collapse trigger, link-style "Follow OS") — legitimate custom chrome where wrapping in `<Button>` would over-style; documented inline. **Lint guard**: `eslint.config.mjs` adds a `no-restricted-syntax` rule scoped to `src/app/(host)/**/*.{ts,tsx}` + `src/components/oh/**/*.{ts,tsx}` that errors on `<Button variant="default\|outline\|secondary\|ghost\|destructive\|link">` — same enforcement vocabulary catches the issue at PR time. Auth pages, public visitor surface, `src/components/ui/` primitives NOT in the glob (stock variants OK there). Verified the rule fires by temporarily reintroducing a stock variant: ESLint emits the descriptive message at the right line. `.claude/rules/oh-ui.md` extended with a *Button variants* section documenting the two-variant `oh` / `ohGhost` policy + the lint rule + when raw `<button>` is acceptable for tertiary chrome. **(2) Mobile dashboard-bar centering + workspace dropdown popup alignment.** `oh-dashboard-bar` was `display: flex; justify-content: space-between` with three children on mobile (sidebar trigger / workspace switcher / icon-group + user-menu). With unequal side-group widths (1 icon left, 3 icons + avatar right), `space-between` placed the workspace trigger at the visual center *of the gap between the two side groups*, NOT the container center — the trigger drifted right of true center by `(right_group_width - left_icon_width) / 2`. Fix: 3-column CSS grid (`1fr auto 1fr`) on mobile only — middle column self-centers relative to the bar; side columns absorb the side groups via `justify-self: start / center / end`. Reset the trigger's `margin-inline-start: -8px` (a desktop-only flush-left offset) when centered. Desktop keeps the existing flex (sidebar trigger is `md:hidden` — only 2 visible children, `space-between` gives the correct workspace-left / icons-right rhythm). Workspace dropdown popup: switched `Menu.Positioner` from `align="start" alignOffset={-8}` to Base UI's default `align="center"` (verified via Context7 against `/mui/base-ui` — `Align = 'start' \| 'center' \| 'end'`, default `'center'`). Popup now centers under its trigger; combined with the grid-centered trigger on mobile, the menu opens centered in the viewport. Desktop collision avoidance handles edge clipping automatically. **(3) Bookings list — drop top/bottom frame, single-item fallback.** The `<ul>` used `border-y border-oh-line divide-y divide-oh-line` — hairlines top + bottom of the list AND between rows. User wanted only the inter-row dividers. Dropped `border-y`. Edge case: a single booking would have NO visible separators at all. Used Tailwind's `:only-child` arbitrary variant on the `<li>` (`[&:only-child]:border-b [&:only-child]:border-oh-line`) so a lone item still gets a bottom rule for visual closure. With 2+ rows, the inter-row dividers do that work and the only-child rule doesn't fire. **Plus two tiny follow-ups to B.PT44 + B.PT45**: (a) the mobile "Add more hours" CTA on `/availability` had its B.PT45 dotted placeholder border still visible against the `ohGhost` variant's hover-fills-with-ink state — added `hover:border-transparent` so the dots vanish on hover (border-style can't transition smoothly, but border-color can). (b) `.oh-modal-actions` footer divider in `globals.css` migrated from `1px dashed var(--oh-line-dashed)` (22% ink) to `1px dotted var(--oh-line-placeholder)` (12% ink) — consistent placeholder treatment with B.PT45's empty-state borders. Gates: tsc clean, lint 0 errors. No procedure logic touched, no vitest delta. |

| B.PT43 | Onboarding state in DB (no more "Share your link" flash on hard refresh) | SHIPPED | _to be filled by commit_ | User report after B.PT42 shipped + dev server reset: the "Share your link" checkbox still flashed unchecked for ~1s on hard-refresh of `/bookings`. Diagnosis (user's own words): "whatever information we use to determine whether to show it or not is arriving late." Root cause: the manual-mark step (`share`) and the dismiss flag both lived in `localStorage` (`officehours.onboarding.{hide,manual}`). localStorage is a browser-only API — Node has no way to read it during SSR — so the server-rendered checklist always saw `manuallyDone: new Set()` regardless of what the user had clicked. First paint rendered the share row unchecked; client mounted → `useSyncExternalStore` snapshotted localStorage → row snap-flipped to checked. Same class of "the data the SSR pass needed wasn't readable on the server" bug B.PT42 fixed for `schedule.get`, but for client-only state instead of an unhydrated query. cal.com stores `User.completedOnboarding: Boolean` on the User row (verified via the cal.com submodule at `apps/web/`); dub stores `Workspace.completedOnboardingSteps: String[]` on Workspace. We follow cal.com — onboarding is a per-host concern, not per-workspace, so there's no benefit to keying it off the active workspace. **Schema**: two new columns on `User` — `onboardingDismissed Boolean @default(false)` + `onboardingManualSteps String @default("[]")` (JSON-stringified array, since SQLite has no native array type — same shape `WorkflowAction.params` uses). Migration `20260430120000_onboarding_state` adds both as `ADD COLUMN ... NOT NULL DEFAULT`, idempotent on existing rows. Synced to dev.db via `prisma db push` after `migrate dev` failed against pre-existing migration drift (`20260429100000_workspace_aware_webhooks_audit` was modified after apply; the manual migration file lets prod pick the change up via the normal `migrate deploy` path). **API**: `users.me` query select extended with both fields; `onboardingManualSteps` parsed via a defensive `parseManualSteps()` helper that JSON-parses, asserts an array, and filters against the canonical `ONBOARDING_STEP_IDS` const (malformed cell or unknown id → empty array). New `users.setOnboardingState({ dismissed?: boolean, manualSteps?: OnboardingStepId[] })` mutation — both inputs optional, refine() requires at least one; manualSteps de-dupes via `Set` + sorts before JSON.stringify so the on-disk shape is stable. zod enum derived from `ONBOARDING_STEP_IDS` (single source of truth — same const drives the parser, the input validator, and the runtime type). **Component**: `onboarding-checklist.tsx` rewritten — drops `useLocalStorageBool` + `useLocalStorageStringSet` + `useSyncExternalStore` + the `subscribeToKey` / `notifyKey` / `EMPTY_SET` / `isStepId` machinery (~110 LOC of localStorage glue removed). Reads `hidden` from `me.data.onboardingDismissed` and `manuallyDone` from `new Set(me.data.onboardingManualSteps)`. Hide button + Mark-done button both call `setOnboardingState.mutate(...)` with optimistic `setData` on the `users.me` cache + onError rollback (the global `useMutation.onSuccess` override in `hooks.ts` invalidates everything on success, so the server-confirmed value re-lands automatically — only the optimistic update matters for instantness). **Constant export**: `ONBOARDING_STEP_IDS` lifted to a top-level `as const` array in `src/lib/onboarding.ts`; `OnboardingStepId` derived as `(typeof ONBOARDING_STEP_IDS)[number]`. The previous string-literal-union type stayed compatible (same five ids in same order). Result: the SSR pass reads the user's actual dismiss + manual-step state from the User row → first paint renders the checklist with correct done/pending markers → no snap, no flash, no "card briefly reappears." **Migration footnote**: existing dev DBs that had localStorage state before this commit lose that state (the new columns default to `dismissed: false` + `manualSteps: []`). Acceptable for dev. Prod has no impact yet (no real users on the new columns). All gates green: `pnpm tsc --noEmit` clean, `pnpm lint` 0 errors, vitest 363 pass / 4 pre-existing invitation failures (verified pre-existing on B.PT42), authed-hydration 6/6. Defers: none. |

## What's next (auto-derived)

**No OPEN rows.** B.PT16 (booking reads + SSE workspace scope),
B.PT17 (smart switcher + broad invalidation), and B.PT18 (workflow
scope — option A: keep personal, fix UI gate) all shipped in this
loop. B.PT19 (workspace-scoped workflows / cal.com dual-FK) is
SIGNAL-GATED — promote when a multi-workspace host actually asks
for per-workspace automation scope.

What remains beyond those is Tier B's three SIGNAL-GATED rows (B.PT10
/ B.PT11 / B.PT13) plus the entire Tier C surface — none of which
should be started without the matching signal:

| Signal | Promotes |
|---|---|
| "I want to schedule something X minutes after this other thing" | B.PT10 multi-step workflows |
| "Our customer asked for native Slack / SMS / Discord on booking" | B.PT11 with the named provider in scope |
| "We measured uneven host distribution under round-robin" | B.PT13 lookback decay cron |
| Real prod traffic justifying push channels | C.PT1 calendar push notifications |
| Pick a metric / incident sink (BetterUptime / Vercel / UptimeRobot) | C.PT2 / C.PT3 / C.PT4 status-page extensions |
| User demands hidden / parent-driven embeds | C.PT5 / C.PT6 / C.PT7 embed extensions |
| 2FA / SSO / enterprise SSO requirement | C.PT8 better-auth migration |
| Multi-instance serverless deploy | B.PT14 Upstash Redis swap for `createRatelimit` |
| Postgres migration itself | C.PT9 Postgres-mode CI workflow |
| Measured `/h/<handle>` p95 > 800ms or sustained > 5 reads/s | C5 cache-aside (deferred-by-design — see `docs/C5-cache-aside-deferred.md`) |
| Multi-workspace host asks for per-workspace automation scope | B.PT19 workspace-scoped workflows (option B or cal.com dual-FK) |

The right next move is a product call (which signal flips an item
from gated to actionable), not engineering.

## Why this file replaces the older docs

- `OFFICEHOURS-PROJECT-GUIDE.md` — project philosophy now lives in `AGENTS.md` + `.claude/rules/`. The §10.1 priority list is summarized in this file's first section.
- `OFFICEHOURS-DEPTH-IDEAS.md` — Tier A/B/C originals fold into this file's Tier A/B/C tables.
- `OFFICEHOURS-FOLLOWUPS.md` — same; rolled into the Post-§10.1 sub-tables.
- `OFFICEHOURS-OPEN-DEFERRALS.md` — replaced wholesale; this file IS the open-deferrals view.
- `OFFICEHOURS-CLI-IDEAS.md` — moved to `docs/cli-design.md`. Different concern (design exploration for a CLI surface).
- `OFFICEHOURS-IMPLEMENTATION-AGENT-PROMPT.md` — moved to `docs/implementer-agent-prompt.md`. Different concern (reusable system prompt for a managed agent).

The three stub redirect files (`OFFICEHOURS-DEPTH-IDEAS.md`, `OFFICEHOURS-FOLLOWUPS.md`, `OFFICEHOURS-OPEN-DEFERRALS.md`) were hard-deleted via `git rm` after the consolidation commit. To recover the original content of any of those, run:

```bash
git log --diff-filter=D -- OFFICEHOURS-<NAME>.md       # find the deletion commit
git show <commit>^:OFFICEHOURS-<NAME>.md > restored.md  # restore from one commit before
```

`OFFICEHOURS-PROJECT-GUIDE.md` is gitignored (private to user) and was preserved with a small note pointing here for status. Long-form mental model stays in that file.
